  - **Nothing is measured twice:** [company.js](src/features/scorecard/lib/company.js) `buildCompanyView` adds up the marketplace tabs' own rows (auto_key `map_pct`, `sales`; and their "On-Time Shipping %" rows, by that name since 2026-11-17, when they became typed). MAP = the plain average of every marketplace's % ("el average de todos los MK %"), company and per country; Sales = each marketplace's weekly sales, the country's total and "Total vs target" (green at 100% or more) — Canada in CAD, the USA in USD, never added together; On-time shipping = the same average; Inventory available = units available and the share of products with stock per country.
  - **Targets** (user, 2026-11-12, who typed Canada's and the USA's sales targets from the tab that day). One target per number:
    - A marketplace's sales row carries the target of its own tab's Sales row (`scorecard_metrics.goal`): set here or there, by an admin, it is the same one.
    - **A country's total is given no target: it takes its marketplaces' targets added up** (user: "debería llenarse automáticamente al sumar el target de todos los marketplaces"; about the USA, where Menards had none, "con ese debes hacer lo mismo") — the ones that have one; the cell's tooltip names those that do not (`targetHint`). "Total vs target" is the total ÷ that sum.
    - The rows the tab works out — the MAP and on-time averages, the inventory, and "Total vs target", born at least 100% — keep theirs in `scorecard_company_targets (key = the row's id, goal, goal_op)` ([20261112_scorecard_company_targets.sql](supabase/migrations/20261112_scorecard_company_targets.sql)), read and written by the viewers only, set from the cell like any other. Emptying "Total vs target" takes it back to 100%.
    - **The "Weekly target" row is gone** (user: "no sé qué es, podemos quitarlo"; nothing had been typed in it): no row of the tab is typed any more.
  - **The week's strip and its filter** are on this tab too (2026-11-12; until then it had no strip): the tab counts its own rows and draws the strip into the page's pinned controls (`stripSlot`, a portal), and the filter is the page's own, so it is kept from tab to tab.
  - **Its own numbers** live in `scorecard_company_values (key, week_start)`: the inventory snapshot (`inv_units:<market>`, `inv_in_stock:<market>`) that `scorecard_company_snapshot()` writes every hour (cron `scorecard-company-snapshot`, :30) into the column of the week in progress — a stock has no history, so the weeks before 2026-11-11 are empty.
  - A new company row is one more entry in `buildCompanyView` (and its auto_key in `COMPANY_AUTO_KEYS`). The definitions above are defaults taken while waiting for the user's list: confirm them with it.
- **Operations tab** (user, 2026-11-16: "Debemos crear ahora el EOS para Operations, por los momentos hazlo solo visible para mí"). `?board=operations`, before the marketplaces: the Operations department's own EOS scorecard, from the workbook's five Ops sheets (Customer Care, Inventory / Purchasing Canada and USA, Warehouse Canada and USA), in the same grid and the same two tables (`scorecard_metrics.board = 'operations'`).
  - **Who sees it:** `app_settings.scorecard_restricted_boards` = `{ "operations": ["jose@stylishkb.com", "jenny@stylishkb.com", "pedro@stylishkb.com"] }` — Yenny and Pedro since 2026-11-23 (user: "habilita el score card de Operations para Yenny también … y para Pedro"; one Activity Log entry); until then Jose alone. A board listed there is for those emails only; one that is not listed is open to everyone — so taking the key out opens the tab (then take its line out of `RESTRICTED` in [sync-assistant-knowledge.mjs](scripts/sync-assistant-knowledge.mjs) too), and adding an email adds a person. Nobody else gets the button, its address shows them the first marketplace, and the database refuses them its rows — read, write, add, move or remove (`scorecard_hidden_boards()`, [20261116_scorecard_operations.sql](supabase/migrations/20261116_scorecard_operations.sql); a value is read by whoever may read its measurable; the policies take the function as `(select …)::text[]`, read once per statement). What is typed there still writes the Activity Log, which every admin reads.
  - **A department's board** (`DEPARTMENT_BOARDS` in [boards.js](src/features/scorecard/lib/boards.js)) differs from a marketplace's in three things. Its own themes (`sections`: Warehouse — the first since 2026-11-23, user: "este debe ir de primero" —, Customer care, Returns, Sales — added 2026-11-23 for Open Box sales, which left Inventory & purchasing (user: "esto es sales, no inventory purchasing, hay que moverlo") —, Inventory & purchasing, Same-day shipping — it was "On-time shipping" until 2026-11-23 (user: "On Time Shipping no hace falta volver a escribirlo, es redundante, se puede quitar y poner el marketplace mejor, se cambia on time shipping a same day shipping" — "eso es solo en el Operations, más nada"): that day its 12 rows named "<marketplace>: on-time shipping" / "Wayfair: same-day shipping" / "BB&B: same-day fulfillment" took the marketplace alone (Home Depot, Rona, Amazon, Wayfair, Walmart, Best Buy, Lowe's, BB&B; one Activity Log entry with the names before), since the theme says what is measured; the fill rates, Processed on time, Not processed in 24 h and Late orders shipped keep their names, and the marketplace tabs' "On-Time Shipping %" rows and the Company tab were not touched — `sectionsFor(board)` in sections.js; every measurable names one, and the pencil has no "From its name"). A measurable may be of both countries (`shared`: market NULL, shown under Canada and under USA; "Both countries" in the pencil). And the rows the PIM fills there are a kind of their own (`ops: true` keys in autoMetrics.js, offered in the pencil only on a department's tab), worked out by `scorecard-fill` when the board is asked for by name (`serverFill`) — see "Filled by the PIM" below; every other number is typed, by its owner or an admin, once its week has ended. A new department's tab is one more entry there, its key in the setting while it is not open to everyone, and its rows.
  - **Loaded that day:** 88 measurables (49 under Canada, 47 under USA; 63 with a target). Names tidied; the sheet's Measurable and Source kept as the source (the row's tooltip); the Weekly Goal as the target where it is one number with a direction — "20-50", "Lead Time 1 BB", "TBD" stay in the source as "Target in the workbook: …"; times in seconds are numbers with "(sec)" in the name. The sheet's Who was kept as the owner's NAME only (`owner_name`: no PIM user linked, so it let nobody type) until 2026-11-23, when the names were removed from the tab's 83 measurables that had one (user: "en operations quítale el owners name a todos los KPI, solo en operations"; the names before are in that day's Activity Log entry) — the user assigns the owners himself, as on the marketplace tabs. No weekly values: that copy of the workbook ends in October and the scorecard starts at W44 (the Ops sheets label a week by its Monday and hold that week's own numbers; the Care sheet by the Sunday after it, like the PIM — map them before loading any). Left out, and listed in that day's Activity Log entry: the rows nobody fills any more (Warranty items cost, the two claims rows, a "days to close a return" without data since April 2025, the two "orders not processed within 24H" marked Obsolete, Continuous Improvement, % of refund Home Depot USA), the rows the sheets repeat, and the notes and rocks under each table. Loader: that session's scratchpad (`ops-load.mjs`). **Added 2026-11-23: "Open orders not shipped"**, Canada and USA (user, showing the workbook's row — "How many orders are open (not shipped) with more than 24 hours", PowerBI dashboard operations, goal 0 %: "falta open orders no shipped en ambos países"): two typed rows at the end of Same-day shipping, percent, target at most 0 %, without an owner. The unit follows the workbook's goal cell (0 %); if the team types a count, make them numbers.
  - **Filled by the PIM: 55 of the 88 rows — 37 since 2026-11-17**, when what came from ShipStation's labels became typed (the label rows, the shipping times and the copies of the marketplaces' on-time rows below; see "ShipStation is out of the ScoreCard") (user, the same day: "necesito que la mayoría de todo eso se pueda traer automático, sea de ShipStation, de Odoo o de algún otro sitio" — "Conéctalo pues, dale"). [_shared/scorecardOperations.ts](supabase/functions/_shared/scorecardOperations.ts) works them out inside `scorecard-fill`, with that function's own tools — when an order shipped (`shippedAt`), on time or late (`shipTally`, taken out of the marketplace loop that day; `shipDeadline(order, days)`), the value of a return, a product's cost — so a rule is one piece of code for every tab. Everything is read. Its header says where each number comes from; a row's tooltip says how its number is worked out (`AUTO_KEYS[key].label`, now on every PIM row of every tab).
    - **ShipStation's labels** (`shipstation_labels`): Cost per label — Canada, the week's cost ÷ EVERY label, the marketplaces' prepaid ones ($0) included, which is ShipStation's dashboard and what the team typed (24.75 / 25.23 / 27.90 in October, to the cent); USA, the average of the labels we paid for (almost every USA label is a marketplace's) —, Orders processed (USA: the labels made; 226 and 211 as typed) and Return label cost (USA: 32.76, 27.27 as typed).
    - **WeCare** (user, the same day, showing "WeCare — Stylish Customer Care", the company's other app, where Zoho's tickets land: "¿Sirve para traer data a Operations?" — "Primero traigamos la data de wecare", and, asked how the PIM should reach it, "¿Podemos hacerlo por API?" — "Dale, aprobado"). The PIM asks WeCare's own API, never its database: `GET https://stylish-care-app.vercel.app/api/pim/scorecard?ranges=<from>_<to>,…` (ISO instants; the PIM names its own Eastern weeks) with `Authorization: Bearer` + the key both apps share — `PIM_API_KEY` on WeCare's Vercel project, `WECARE_API_KEY` (and `WECARE_API_URL`) in the PIM's function secrets and in `.env.secrets.local`; made by a script, never shown. WeCare's side is its `server/pim.js` and one route above its auth gate (repo `stylish-care-app`, commit edff5bb): it fails closed without the key and answers with TOTALS only — no ticket, subject, name or address — worked out with its dashboard's own rules. Two rows, without a country: **Tickets closed** (status "Closed", "Closed Wayfair" or "Closed Best Buy", closed in the week, automated notifications left out: 33 / 34 / 26 the first weeks, where the team typed ~90–120 from Zoho, which counts the automated mail too) and **Resolution time** (creation → closing on the clock, averaged over the week's closed tickets: 354 / 583 / 473 hours — weeks-old tickets being closed — where the team typed 44–50 from Zoho's own report). WeCare not answering leaves the rows as they are and the rest of the tab still fills (`diag.wecare`). WeCare is in another Supabase account, which the PIM's management token does not reach — and its keys are never used from here.
    - **The marketplace tabs' own rows, copied** (never measured twice): on-time shipping of Home Depot, Rona, Amazon (the orders we ship), Walmart, Best Buy and Lowe's — the scorecard's 2-business-day rule, not the portal's "on-time delivery". They are the store's tab value of the same country and week, so the tab's own jobs run 8 minutes after the marketplaces'.
    - **Odoo, every order of the country** (not Amazon FBA; not the "marketplaces" that are no sales: Warranty Claim 18, Missing Items 19, Correct Items 20, Wrong Items 21, Review 22 / 23): Wayfair same-day shipping (every label made the day of the order), Processed on time (the 2-business-day rule over all of them), Not processed in 24 h (not labelled by the end of the next business day: 1 / 8 / 4 the first weeks — a strict 24 hours on the clock gave ~190 a week), Open Box sales (the -OB products, before tax: 84.72 and 943.92 as typed in October), Shipment errors (orders held as Wrong Items or Missing Items — **typed by hand again since 2026-11-23**, user: "Shipment errors queda manual": its two rows lost their key in the database and the 10 numbers the PIM had written, all 0, were cleared and kept in that day's Activity Log entry), Returns Over Sales (value Odoo received back ÷ sold over 4 weeks, as on the marketplace tabs; **one row per country since 2026-11-23**: the row of both countries together, "Both countries: returns over sales", in USD, was removed that day — user: "que quede Returns Over Sales solamente, eso de both countries no", and, asked which of the three rows should stay, he chose to take the combined one out; archived with its numbers kept, the two country rows renamed "Returns Over Sales", one Activity Log entry) and Assembly sales lost (products with a bill of materials at 0 units now × their weekly sales of the 8 weeks before).
    - **Odoo, bodega:** Returns processed (return pickings received at CBG / ATL — created and done within seconds, sometimes in batches: 101 and 82 in one October week), Returns to stock / to Open Box (the units' destination: CAN/OB/…, USA/OB/… or stock; Open Box only ever comes straight from a return — **both rows were removed from the tab on 2026-11-23**, user: "Returns to Open Box, Returns to stock (RTI): borrar esos"; archived in both countries with their numbers kept and the list in that day's Activity Log entry, so the fill no longer writes them: the tab then showed 79 measurables, 33 of them the PIM's), Assembly (manufacturing orders done at ATL: sinks, and S-6 among them), Cycle count and Inventory accuracy (a product's `last_count_date` in the week; off = an inventory move of more than 5 units) — **both are typed by hand again since 2026-11-23** (user: "Inventory accuracy este queda manual", then "Cycle count también es manual"): their four rows (Canada, USA) lost their key in the database — a PIM row can no longer be handed back from the app — and the 11 numbers the PIM had written there (W46, W47 and the week in progress) were cleared so the team types its own; they are in that day's two Activity Log entries. The keys `ops_cycle_count` / `ops_inventory_accuracy` stay in the code, offered in the pencil. The tab then had 79 measurables, 29 of them the PIM's. Odoo keeps only the LAST count date, so a week's count shrinks as products are counted again: the week in progress is written at every run and a closed week only while it has no number (`ONCE`).
    - **Today's state, on this week's column only** (`SNAPSHOT`, as the team types it the day of the meeting): Stock available (the Company-style share of tracked products with stock), the "under 70 days" counts (selling products whose stock — available for use plus on order — lasts under 70 days at the 90-day pace, from `product_inventory` / `product_sales`), Excess over 180 days (sinks and faucets ON HAND beyond 180 days of sales × Odoo's average cost — not `available`, which counts what could be built), and the days to zero of ST-05, A-918KIT and A-919KIT, which are Odoo's own replenishment report (`doi_on_hand_transit`, read with the warehouse and both companies in the context, like the stock).
    - **Left typed on purpose** (33 rows): First response time and the review tickets (WeCare keeps neither the first answer's time nor a "review" mark or a country on a ticket: to add there first), Customer satisfaction (the key `ops_care_csat` is ready — the share of WeCare's survey answers of 4 or 5 — but its survey had no answer yet, and a PIM row cannot be typed); the PowerBI times (Odoo's manufacturing orders last seconds and have no work orders); Amazon FBA and Prime; BB&B same-day fulfillment (our same-day read 0–25 % against the 75–92 % the team typed: BB&B measures something else); Home Depot USA returns (Odoo received ~66 USD a week against the 2,300–3,000 the team types: Home Depot keeps the goods — the PIM's since 2026-11-24, from the credit memos: see "Rows that repeat another tab's" below); Obsolete inventory (no product is marked obsolete in the PIM — the key `ops_obsolete_value` exists for when they are); Returns pending / in transit and lost to scrap (Odoo has neither a waiting return nor a scrap); Late orders shipped (the same thing as Processed on time under another name); fill rates, Wayfair SP, cancelled orders shipped, ShipSavvy, WFS, negative reviews, sales refunded.
    - **Credit memos, Canada** (user, 2026-11-23: "en Canadá en retornos meter mejor el total de las credit memo, eso se saca de Odoo, ahí sacaremos el total en dinero y en SKU o números de parte"). Two PIM rows, the first of Canada's Returns, added beside the others (his choice over replacing Returns processed): **Credit memos ($)** (`ops_credit_memos_value`) = Odoo's posted customer credit notes (`account.move`, `out_refund`) of the Canada company whose own date falls in the week, before tax, in CAD — a note in USD (Wayfair Canada's) at Odoo's rate of its day (`amount_untaxed_signed`) —, and **Credit memos (SKUs)** (`ops_credit_memos_skus`) = how many DIFFERENT SKUs those notes' product lines name (asked units or different SKUs, he chose the SKUs; a line that is no product — "Special Discount" — names none). Every column and the week in progress, like the tab's other weekly numbers. The code works for either country (a USA row with these keys would read the USA company, in USD): only Canada's rows exist. First numbers, W44–W47: 14,262.32 / 44, 4,174.01 / 29, 18,841.14 / 66, 4,925.12 / 22 (Nov 15–21: Home Depot 3 notes, Sinks Direct Canada 2, Wayfair Canada 1). Checked that day: the notes added up apart from the function (the same numbers), the function's own file run on the dev machine against Odoo (the same), the deployed function in preview with the keys assumed (the same), then the fill (76 values written on the tab).
    - **Rows that repeat another tab's** (user, 2026-11-23, about "Amazon USA FBA inventory excess (CBM)": "se debe traer ya del tab de Amazon de cada país y colocarle el mismo nombre de la tab de Amazon"; [20261123_scorecard_copy_of.sql](supabase/migrations/20261123_scorecard_copy_of.sql)). `scorecard_metrics.copy_of` names the row a measurable repeats, and such a row carries the key `copy` (`linked` in autoMetrics.js: never offered in the pencil), so it is a PIM row like any other — the chip, no pencil, nobody types it. **The database copies each number the moment it is saved on the source row** — typed, changed or cleared, by whoever may type there, also a person who does not see this tab (trigger `scorecard_values_copy`, which runs as its owner): no cron and no deploy. The tab's two Amazon FBA inventory rows are the first: Canada repeats the Amazon tab's "Inventory Age ≥ 90 -180 Days" and the USA its "Inventory Age > 180 Days", each named "Amazon: " + that name (the marketplace in front, as the tab's other rows; told to the user) and loaded with the Amazon tab's numbers from W44 on. Their target (at most 0, from the workbook's CBM goal) was left as it was. A new one: set `copy_of`, `auto_key = 'copy'`, the name and the unit on the row and copy the source's numbers once (that day's scratchpad `copy-setup.sql`). **"Home Depot USA returns ($)" repeats the Home Depot tab's Returns Value $ (USA) since 2026-11-24** (user, the day that row became the credit memos: "Home Depot USA returns ($) — ahí va lo de credit memo también, lo llena PIM"): the week's credit notes made out to Home Depot USA, the same number on both tabs, written there by `scorecard-fill` and copied here by the trigger (tried inside an undone transaction: a week changed and a new week both reach the copy). It kept its own name (he did not ask for the tab's), had no number typed since W44, and took W44 on from the source: 4,152 / 2,599 / 4,646 / 3,444. One Activity Log entry. **A copy's description is its Source** (`autoDescription` in autoMetrics.js: the key `copy` is the same for every such row, so the tooltip reads "Filled by the PIM: The same number as on its marketplace's tab, copied here the moment it is saved there — " and the row's Source, which names the row it repeats and how that one is filled); until that day the label said "typed there", which this row would have made false. Write a new copy's Source that way. The tooltip's change went to main on 2026-11-26, with his next "commit y push". Checked that day inside an undone transaction and then for real: the copies equal the Amazon tab's numbers; a number typed, changed and cleared on the Amazon row by an admin who does not see this tab is copied, changed and cleared here; typing on the copy and renaming it are refused, its target is taken.
    - **"SKUs not on Amazon Prime" waits for the FBA Analyzer** (user, 2026-11-23: "pendiente ese de conectar con el FBA Analyzer"): typed for now, both countries. What the FBA Analyzer is and how the PIM would reach it is to ask him when he brings it up — not looked into.
    - **When:** crons `scorecard-fill-operations-daily` (`8 10,11 * * *`, `{ boards: ["operations"], running: true, backfill: 4, refresh: 4 }`) and `scorecard-fill-operations-running` (`23 * * * *` since 2026-11-22, every hour like the marketplaces' job — it was `23 1,13,16,19,22 * * *`; `running: "only"`), copied inside the database from the marketplace jobs (their text carries the cron's secret: never select `cron.job.command` — read its shape with regular expressions); "Refresh PIM numbers" on the tab does the same now. A run takes 10–17 s. Preview with rows that have no key yet: `{ mode: "preview", boards: ["operations"], assume: [{ board: "operations", market, key }] }`.
    - **Checked that day:** the marketplace tabs' numbers before and after the change, the committed file against the new one in the same minute (74 values, none different); the 53 numbers against the workbook where it has them (above); the deployed function (v40) against the local run (133 values, one live drift); the first real fill (133 values, W44–W47), the cron's own call (200), and the tab in a browser — 26 of 49 filled under Canada, on / off target colored, the PIM chip and its tooltip, no sideways scroll, axe as before. Names of the PIM rows were shortened that day (the marketplace first: "Home Depot: on-time shipping") — with the chip, "On-time shipping: H…" and "…: R…" could not be told apart.
  - **Checked that day:** the permissions as four people inside an undone transaction — an admin and an editor who are not on the list read none of its rows and every other board as before (423 measurables, 5,575 values, 2 ms), and cannot add, rename, move, type or clear there; the person on the list can; a board taken off the list opens; a list that is not a list hides it from everyone. The tab in a browser with a stand-in sign-in, live reads and every write captured: Canada 49 rows, USA 47, the five themes, the pencil (Both countries, its themes, no PIM field), a target's dialog, Add measurable (sent with the board, no country, its theme), a number typed in W46 (sent, and the strip counts it), an admin who is not on the list (no button, the address opens Amazon), an editor on a marketplace tab as before — at 1440 / 390 in both themes: no sideways scroll, and axe's only findings the top bar's account button and the pencil dialog's known `aria-allowed-role`. The assistant's function carries this tab's group since its deploy of 2026-11-23 (until then it opened this piece for nobody). **Not done:** what would fill more rows — in WeCare, the first answer's time, a mark for review tickets with their country, and survey answers (4 rows); whatever feeds the PowerBI operations dashboard (5); Amazon's SP-API with the company's own credentials (4).
- **A pass before the weekly meeting** (user, 2026-11-17: "revísalo y púlele lo que hay que pulirle"; every tab and country opened with live data at 1920 / 1440 / 390). **The measurable's column is 21rem from xl** (17rem below, and on the company view, whose names fit): at 17rem the owner, the PIM chip and the pencil left the name ~18 characters — "Loss Due to Out of …", two rows both reading "Refunds Lost $ (Accumulat…". The room comes from the weeks that are still empty (`min-w-[3.5rem]`, it was 4rem), so the 13 weeks still fit at 1920. **A number never breaks into two lines** (`whitespace-nowrap` on the value: "138.3 h" made its row taller). **Money under $100 shows both cents** (`dollars` in format.js: "$9.30", "≤ $1.40"; a round amount stays "$25", from $100 whole dollars as before). **The weeks' label says the days the numbers cover**, like the column headers ("W44 – W4 · Oct 25 – Jan 23"; it gave the columns' own Sundays, "Nov 1 – Jan 30"). **"N numbers filled from the PIM" no longer adds a line to the pinned controls** — a few seconds after a tab opened the table jumped under whoever was reading it: the Refresh button says "N numbers updated" for five seconds (a screen reader hears the sentence), and only an error takes room. Checked that day with a stand-in sign-in, live reads and every write captured: no sideways scroll, the document never scrolls, axe's only finding the top bar's account button.
- **Loaded 2026-11-03** from the user's workbook (287 measurables, 4,145 values W14–W44; parser and loader in that session's scratchpad): owners Jessica (Amazon), Vladimir (Wayfair), Abhijit (Sinks Direct, ads rows); Lowe's, Rona, Home Depot and BB&B had no owner column. Lowe's is the USA channel although its sheet said Canada (its sales are in USD). The sheets mixed 0.98 and 98 for the same percentage (a value over 1.5 was divided by 100; ODR / late shipping / cancelation / unresponsive rates over 0.5 too); Wayfair's two "W31" columns: the August one wins. The targets came from the workbook's "Weekly Goal" column and from names ("Goal < 5%", "Goal > 3.5", "… is 80%").

### Assistant (2026-11-11)

User request: an AI "que entienda la app y sepa exactamente cómo funciona y pedirle cosas por chat"; the same day, "Haz todo eso!" to eleven improvements. A chat with Claude (`claude-opus-5-5`, Anthropic API, secret `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`) on its own page, `/assistant` ("Assistant" in the sidebar), and in a panel over any page (the top bar's button). It explains how the PIM works and looks live data up. **By itself it writes nothing to the PIM's data, and it cannot** (its access to the database is read-only by a gate, see Guardrails below): a change it is asked for is only PREPARED, and the person confirms it on a card (below). Nothing can be deleted from it.
- **Who uses it** — Settings → Assistant ([AssistantSettings](src/features/assistant/components/AssistantSettings.jsx)): On / Off, the people, the limit per 24 hours (5 USD when not set — a question is refused once that much was spent), how much it thinks (`effort` low / medium / high; changing it writes the cache again once) and the usage (`assistant_usage()`: cost of the last 24 hours and 30 days, per person, the latest questions with their thumbs). All of it — and which changes it may prepare (Guardrails, below) — is `app_settings.assistant` (`{ enabled, emails, effort?, daily_limit_usd?, model?, actions? }`); only jose@stylishkb.com at first. Nobody else gets the menu item or the top bar's button ([useAssistantAccess](src/features/assistant/hooks/useAssistantAccess.js)), and the function refuses them.
- **It sees what the person asking may see.** Every lookup is a read with THAT PERSON'S session, so RLS decides what comes back (the Activity Log only for admins). The tables it may read are one list, [_shared/assistantTables.js](supabase/functions/_shared/assistantTables.js) (`hide` = bulk columns it never selects; `key` = the columns that tell one row from another, the order an Excel sheet is read in); a new table the PIM keeps goes there with a one-line note and its key.
- **What it knows:** this file, from "## Architecture" on. [sync-assistant-knowledge.mjs](scripts/sync-assistant-knowledge.mjs) cuts it into pieces (one per top-level paragraph or bullet, a very long one also at its sub-bullets, short neighbours together) and writes `assistant_knowledge`: `guide` (the pieces), `guide_index` (one line per piece) and `database` (the readable tables with their live columns and list values). The model is always shown the index and the tables, and opens the pieces a question needs (`search_guide`, `read_guide`). Whole, the guide is ~90,000 tokens: sent with every question it cost ~0.75 USD each time an hour had passed without one (first test, 2026-11-11), which is why it is read by pieces.
  - **It updates itself**: after a commit that changes this file or the tables list, `.githooks/post-commit` runs the script (`git config core.hooksPath .githooks`, set on the dev machine 2026-11-11 — a new clone sets it once). It never blocks a commit; by hand: `node scripts/sync-assistant-knowledge.mjs` (`--dry-run`, `--index`, `--no-user-guide`). Settings → Assistant says when the knowledge was last written.
  - **Two guides.** Admins are answered from this file's pieces. Everyone else is answered from the **team's guide**: each piece rewritten for a person who uses the app and does not program — rules, dates and numbers stay; file, function and table names, deployment, tests and how access is enforced go ([_shared/assistantGuide.js](supabase/functions/_shared/assistantGuide.js), Claude Sonnet 5.5, the function's mode `user_guide`, called by the sync script). A rewrite is kept by the hash of its piece, so only a changed piece is paid again: 1.18 USD for the first 173 pieces, cents after. A piece with nothing in it for a user stays with admins. Until the team's guide exists, a person who is not an admin is told it is not ready.
  - **A piece only a few people may see** (`RESTRICTED` in the sync script) is marked `only`: it is left out of the index, the assistant opens it only for those people (`groups`, from their own setting) and it never reaches the team's guide. Write about such a thing in its own bullet, starting with its name in bold, and do not name it elsewhere in this file.
- **Lookups** ([_shared/assistantTools.js](supabase/functions/_shared/assistantTools.js), all reads): `search_guide` / `read_guide`; `find_products` (words); `product_details` (one SKU: fields, prices, variants, media, marketplace ids, stock, sales, Where to Buy, promotions — the long texts only with `include_content`); `listing_health` (one product through the app's own completeness.js and listingHealth.js: PIM completeness with the tab › section of each gap, and each connected store's score and failing checks from its latest snapshot item, `channel_health_item`); `price_alignment` (the Pricing page's own report, priceAlignmentReport.js, read through the person's session: every store's % aligned, one store's problems, or one product on every store — `classifySnapshot` also returns `aligned` for that); `sales_overview` (totals per market and marketplace, ranking with stock and how long it lasts); `promotions_overview` (real dates per country through `promoWindow`, running / upcoming / ended today, a promotion's products and prices); and `query_table` for everything else (PostgREST select / filters; `count_only`; `group_by` / `sum` are added up in the function, since the API's own aggregates are off). Change a rule's maths in the app's own code, never in a lookup.
- **What it may say about a listing** (2026-11-26). A note went round the team that day: the assistant "may flag information as missing when, in fact, it is already published on the platform … because the data is not presented in the specific format the assistant expects", and people were assigning tasks from its answers (user: "Comentarios sobre el asistente, puedes arreglar?"). The 156 questions asked since 2026-11-11 were read (Jessica 65, Jenny 50, Jose 28, Hitesh 12, Vladimir 1; many are "give me the to-do list for <marketplace>", "which tasks should I assign to each department").
  - **The cause.** `listing_health` gave the model only the NAME of each failing check ("Bullet Points (major)") under the store's name, so it said the store lacked it. But most checks of Wayfair, Best Buy and Walmart read the PIM's own fields, never the store's page; the "listed" / "linked" checks also count the products the team switched off for that store (Walmart USA that day: 186 scored as not listed, with 159 products switched off for it; 19 switched off for Best Buy and 19 for Walmart Canada); and Wayfair's "Spec Attributes in Sync" fails on any attribute whose value at Wayfair is not the one the PIM would send — 303 of the 303 linked Wayfair Canada listings and 336 on Wayfair USA that day, so no Wayfair Canada listing is at 100% — of which the snapshot keeps only the attribute names. It reported those counters as "365 issues". And its lists of "sold out on the store while we have stock" came from the page behind the saved Where to Buy link: 24 of 41 on Walmart USA were products we do not list there (another seller's page), S-822N's Lowe's link was an older listing, K-131SR's Wayfair price had changed since it was read.
  - **The lookup says what each failing check read** (`healthLooksAt` / `healthFound` in assistantTools.js): `looks_at` "pim" (the PIM's own field: "2 bullet points in the PIM (the check wants 4 or more)"), "store" (the store as last read: "the product is hidden on the store"), "store_vs_pim" (a difference: "the store shows 854 and the PIM expects 970"; Wayfair's attribute names) or "link" (no link or offer in the PIM — or `switched_off`: "no listing is expected"), plus `store_read_at` per store (Sinks Direct Canada is scored on the copy of its page the PIM last pulled, three weeks old that day) and `how_to_read`. Nothing of Listing Health's scoring changed.
  - **The instructions have a section for it** ("What you may say about a listing" in assistantTurn.js): a finding about a store is something the PIM read there, shown with its value and when; a PIM gap is never "missing on <store>"; a difference is not something missing and nobody is told to change a store to the PIM's format for it; "no link" is not "not listed"; nothing is said about the content of a listing on a marketplace the PIM is not connected to; before a list of products sold out or off price on a store, the ones switched off for it and the pages not confirmed as the product are left out and each is to be opened first; when the person says the store shows otherwise, their word is the newer fact; a to-do list holds only what a value backs, each item "seen on the store on <day>" or "to verify on the store first", with no general advice nothing supports. The notes of `where_to_buy_links`, `where_to_buy_readings`, `channel_health` and `kpi_snapshots` carry the same limits. On the way: the instructions and the table notes said the stock is read every hour and the sales every 3 hours (5 minutes and an hour since 2026-11-22).
  - **The assistant gives the day a page was read** when it shows a finding or is asked to verify one — the screens and the Excel files still show none (2026-11-16): my choice, said to the user.
  - **Checked that day:** the lookup's own file run on the dev machine against live data on seven products (every request a GET of a readable table), lint. **Deployed the same day at the user's word** ("Despliega el asistente … commit y push": `assistant` **v13**, 22 files, `verify_jwt` off as it was, from a clean copy of commit 3b2c097 — another session was editing the same folder). The deploy also carried what had waited in those files since v12: that day's Price Alignment rule (a page sold out on the store is left out when our warehouse has none either), the Costco group of the price rules, and comments. After it: a call without credentials gets the function's own "Sign in first.", and its keep-alive mode sent the new beginning of a question to the model, which took it (39,259 tokens written to the cache, 0.31 USD — what the next question would have paid). **Not run for real:** a signed-in question through v13, so no answer written under the new rules has been read yet.
  - **Left as it is, told to the user as his to decide:** Listing Health itself still scores a product switched off for a store as not listed there, and still fails every Wayfair listing on the specs check — so the panel and Analytics' Progress to 100% count them.
- **Changes it prepares** (`propose_action`; admins and editors): two kinds — `set_obsolete` (the obsolete mark, on or off) and `set_prices` (MAP Blue / Orange / Purple and MSRP, CAD / USD), at most 50 items a card. UPC, HS codes, every other field, promotions, settings and anything sent to a marketplace or to Odoo stay out: it says where in the app that is done. The function keeps a row in `assistant_actions` and the page draws a card ([ActionCard](src/features/assistant/components/ActionCard.jsx)) with what would change and the values before. **Nothing changes until Confirm**, and then the BROWSER makes it through the app's own saves ([assistantActions.js](src/features/assistant/api/assistantActions.js): `setProductsObsolete`; `updateProduct` after `priceProblems`, and a price that moved since the card was made is not overwritten) — that person's permissions, the same Activity Log entries, the database's own rules. `assistant_decide_action()` records done / cancelled / failed, once, and the model is told with the person's next question. What is checked before a card is shown and when it is confirmed: Guardrails, below. A new kind goes in the tool (`ACTION_KINDS`), in assistantActions.js (`applicable`), in Settings' switches (`CHANGES`) and in the instructions.
- **Excel files it prepares** (`create_excel`; user, 2026-11-12: "agrégale la función de crear documentos en Excel … en Word no, solo en Excel"; everyone on the assistant's list, viewers too — it only reads). Asked for an Excel, a file to download or a list too long for the chat, the assistant DESCRIBES the file and the person gets a card with **Download** ([FileCard](src/features/assistant/components/FileCard.jsx)). Only Excel: no Word, PDF or PowerPoint; the marketplace templates, the promotion files and the Full Report stay on their own pages, and it says where.
  - **A sheet is one of two kinds.** *Read from a table*: one of the assistant's tables with `select` / `filters` / `order` as in `query_table`, and its columns (`key`, `header`, `format`: text, number, money, percent, date, datetime, link). Its rows never pass through the model — the app reads every matching row itself, up to 20,000 a sheet — so a file of thousands of rows costs what its description costs and no row can be miscopied. *Typed by the assistant*: a short table it worked out from its other lookups (a summary, what Price Alignment or the sales returned), at most 300 rows.
  - **Tables are joined by the API's own embedding**, and an embedded table's fields become columns named "alias.field" (`flattenRow`): from a table with a sku to its product, `products(model_name)`; from products to a table with several rows per product, an alias filtered down to one row — `ca:product_inventory(available,eta)` with `ca.market=eq.ca`. It needs a foreign key: `where_to_buy_links`, `product_history` and `stylish_event_discounts` have none to `products`, so they cannot be joined with it. A JSON column opens the same way (`warehouses.CBG.on_order`). Several values left in one cell are joined with "; " and the model is told how to avoid it.
  - **One module fills a sheet, in two places** ([_shared/assistantExcel.js](supabase/functions/_shared/assistantExcel.js), re-exported by `src/features/assistant/lib/excelSpec.js`). *In the function, when the file is prepared*: the description is checked (`checkExcelSpec`) and each query is tried on its first page with the person's session — a wrong column, a refused read or a sheet no row matches goes back to the model with the reason, instead of reaching the person — then the description is kept in `assistant_files` (`spec`, and `summary` = each sheet's rows and columns that day; [20261112_assistant_files.sql](supabase/migrations/20261112_assistant_files.sql): a person reads their own, not even admins another's). *In the browser, at Download* ([excelFile.js](src/features/assistant/lib/excelFile.js)): every row is read with THAT PERSON'S session, 1,000 at a time, in the order asked for and then by the table's key, and written by the PIM's own Excel writer under a progress card. So **the file holds what the person may see, as it is at the moment of the download** — downloaded again next week from the same conversation, it has next week's data — and no file is stored anywhere.
  - **Cells:** a number stays a number and a text a text (a UPC never becomes a number), true / false read Yes / No, a timestamp is shown on Eastern time, HTML is its words. The file is `Stylish_PIM_<Title>_<date>.xlsx`, the band over the headers carries its title. `tableRead` in the same module is also what `query_table` goes through: one set of rules for what a read may ask for (the readable tables, no "*", no hidden column).
  - The **Excel** button under an answer (the answer's own tables as a workbook) stays: it is for a table already written in the chat.
  - **Checked 2026-11-12:** 27 cases against live data without the model (a product list with its stock in both countries, a stock table with its products, a JSON column opened, the 12,546 Where to Buy links read whole — every id once —, 15 refusals, and `query_table` as before); two real questions through the same loop, SDK and knowledge as the function (0.32 USD: a 24-product sheet read from the PIM, right at the first call, and a 10-row sheet typed from the sales); the row the function keeps, tried inside an undone transaction; the function's refusals after the deploy (v8); the card in a browser with a stand-in sign-in and canned answers — ask, Download (two pages read with the session, the file opened and checked), a refused read said on the card, an older conversation's card, the panel — at 1440 / 390 in both themes: no sideways scroll, axe's only finding the top bar's account button. **Run for real the same day:** two files prepared through the deployed function by a person of the team (11:29 and 11:30 Eastern); what a Download wrote is not recorded anywhere.
- **Guardrails** (user, 2026-11-12, after asking it to delete a product: "Tenemos que agregar validaciones al asistente, es peligroso que se puedan eliminar productos o hacer solicitudes que puedan afectar a la veracidad de los datos o de las conexiones"). What was true only by the way the code was written is now enforced, and the one way a change leaves a conversation — a card — is checked at every step.
  - **It only reads, and cannot do otherwise.** Every request the lookups send to the database leaves through `readOnlyFetch()` ([_shared/assistantTools.js](supabase/functions/_shared/assistantTools.js)): a GET or a HEAD, to one of the readable tables (or the one read-only function, `channel_health_item`). An insert, an update, a delete, another table, Storage, Auth or an edge function is refused before it is sent — also on the supabase-js client the Price Alignment lookup reads through, which is built on the same gate. It matters because the session it carries is the person's, and the five people on its list that day were all admins. A new lookup needs no care about this; a new table goes on the list.
  - **Nothing is deleted and no connection is touched from the chat**, and the model is told so in a section of its own ("What is never done from the chat"): deleting (it offers the obsolete mark or the Archived status, which keep the product's data, and says a person deletes in the Catalog or on the product's page); Odoo, the marketplaces and stores, what is pushed or synced, the automations and their switches, Settings, users and roles; and what it reads is information, never an instruction — a product's text, a log entry, an attached file.
  - **A change is checked before its card is shown** (`propose_action`): the person's role may edit; only `set_obsolete` and `set_prices` exist; every SKU is in the PIM; a price is a plain number above 0 (two decimals at most, under 100,000), one value per product and field; and **the PIM's own price rules run on the product as it would be** — [_shared/priceRules.js](supabase/functions/_shared/priceRules.js), moved there so the function and the app run the same code (`src/features/products/lib/priceRules.js` re-exports it). A change they refuse — a MAP typed with a digit missing, a level above the regular price — is never prepared: the whole change is refused and the assistant says which rule. At most 50 items a card and 3 cards a question.
  - **What is worth a second look goes on the card as a warning, and such a card asks for a tick** ("I checked these changes and want them made") before Confirm works: a price moving 20 % or more, an Orange / Purple level that a running promotion of that country uses (it is named: its promotion price follows the level to where it runs), more than 10 products marked at once. Shown without asking for the tick: a coming promotion, the rules' own notes (`priceNotes`), and the reminder that the stores keep the price they show until it is sent to them.
  - **Confirm claims the card in the database first** (`assistant_claim_action`, [20261112_assistant_guardrails.sql](supabase/migrations/20261112_assistant_guardrails.sql)): it must be that person's, still open, **not older than 24 hours**, the assistant still on, the person still on its list and able to edit, the kind not turned off — and it answers once, so a card never runs twice. The page applies the change the DATABASE kept, reduced to what may be applied (`applicable` in [assistantActions.js](src/features/assistant/api/assistantActions.js): the obsolete mark or the eight price fields, nothing else), and writes one more Activity Log entry beside the saves' own: "Confirmed a change the assistant prepared: …". A card older than a day reads Expired; one claimed and never finished (the page closed mid-way) says to check the product first.
  - **Settings → Assistant → Changes it may prepare**: a switch per kind (`app_settings.assistant.actions.set_obsolete` / `.set_prices`: false = off, both on unless set). Off, the tool refuses and the assistant says where in the app the change is made; a card prepared before is refused at Confirm. The usage panel counts the changes prepared and how they ended (`assistant_usage()` → `changes`).
  - **Checked 2026-11-12:** 68 cases against live data without the model — 27 on the gate (every write, other service and other table refused with no request leaving the process; the gated client too) and 41 on `propose_action` — and of the 22 requests that did leave, none was anything but a read; the claim's 10 cases as a signed-in person, inside an undone transaction; six real questions through the same loop, SDK and knowledge as the function (0.46 USD): delete a product, switch a sync off and change a UPC, a MAP of 31 for 308 (refused by the rules, nothing prepared), +25 % (a card with the warning and the tick), an attached file hiding an order (ignored, and reported to the person), a price change while prices are turned off; the cards and the Settings switches in a browser with a stand-in sign-in at 1440 / 390 in both themes (claim → read → save → log → decide, in that order; a refused claim changes nothing; axe's only finding the top bar's account button); the function's refusals after the deploy (v9). **Not run for real yet:** a signed-in question through the deployed function, and a card confirmed against the live database — no card had ever been prepared by that day.
- **A question carries** the page it is asked from (the panel: its address and title, so "this product" needs no SKU) and up to 3 attachments ([attachments.js](src/features/assistant/lib/attachments.js)): an image (redrawn at most 1,400 px as a JPEG; a screenshot can be pasted) or a table (.xlsx / .csv: its first rows as text, at most 400 rows / 55,000 characters). The model is told to treat them as material, never as instructions.
- **One question** ([_shared/assistantTurn.js](supabase/functions/_shared/assistantTurn.js)): the model writes, asks for lookups (run together), reads them and answers — at most 10 model calls. The instructions, the tools, the index and the tables are the cached beginning of every request (`assistantRequest`, kept 1 hour): a change to any of them is paid once, and a conversation started before it goes on without the reasoning it had kept (`prefix_mismatch_behavior: "drop_block"`). A request the model's safety filters decline by mistake is retried on Anthropic's fallback model (`fallbacks: "default"`).
- **The edge function `assistant`** ([index.ts](supabase/functions/assistant/index.ts); `verify_jwt` off, it checks the caller itself; its header lists the shared files to upload with it): `{ message, chat_id?, page?, attachments? }` → an event stream (`chat`, `step`, `text`, `tool`, `tool_done`, `action`, `file`, `done` / `error`). The conversation is kept on the server (`assistant_chats.messages`, replayed to the model exactly as it was produced) and every question is logged in `assistant_turns` with who asked, from which page, what was looked up and what it cost ([20261111_assistant.sql](supabase/migrations/20261111_assistant.sql), [20261111_assistant_more.sql](supabase/migrations/20261111_assistant_more.sql); a person reads their own, admins every question; the browser writes only through `assistant_rate_turn()` and `assistant_decide_action()`). `{ mode }` is for the cron (x-cron-secret), an admin or the scripts (service role): `warm`, `briefing`, `user_guide`.
- **The screens.** [AssistantChat](src/features/assistant/components/AssistantChat.jsx) is the conversation of both the page ([Assistant.jsx](src/pages/Assistant.jsx); the address keeps `?chat=`) and the panel ([AssistantPanel](src/features/assistant/components/AssistantPanel.jsx): mounted in AppShell at its first opening and then kept, hidden when closed, so a conversation survives closing it and changing page; not a modal; Escape closes it; it gives way on `/assistant`). Suggestions on an empty chat; the answer drawn as it is written by [Markdown](src/features/assistant/components/Markdown.jsx) (React elements, never raw HTML; `/catalog/<SKU>` links open in place); a chip per lookup; the cards (a change to confirm, an Excel file to download); Copy; **Excel** (the answer's tables as a workbook, a sheet per table, [answerTables.js](src/features/assistant/lib/answerTables.js) with the PIM's own writer); a thumb up / down (`assistant_rate_turn()`; a thumb down asks what was wrong); the cost of each answer for admins; "Conversations"; the box to ask from, with the paperclip (registered with `useDockClearance`). A question is an `h2` and an answer's headings start at `h3`.
  - **Drawn with the app's own parts** (user, 2026-11-11: "revisa todo para que todo esté parejo y todo acorde al diseño global"; a pass over every assistant screen and the Dashboard's card against what the rest of the app uses). The page's buttons sit on the title's right (its description is kept `max-w-xl`); primary buttons are `font-semibold`; chips are `bg-surface-container-high` (the page's background IS `surface-container`: a chip of that colour disappeared on it); an answer's table is a card with the app's header row (`bg-surface-container-low/60 text-label-md text-on-surface-variant`, `font-medium`); the question bubble is in the body face; fields take `focus:border-primary` with their ring; the panel's shadow is the dialogs' (`shadow-xl`). The Settings section uses that page's own field, sub-label (a `<p>`) and error pill, and the app's one button (see "One look across the app"). On a phone the top search keeps its right padding only while something is typed, so "Search…" fits beside the assistant's button.
  - **A font utility on a heading does nothing without `!`**: index.css sets the face and weight of `h1`–`h6` outside Tailwind's layers, which beats every plain utility (`font-sans`, `font-normal`, `font-semibold`). Use `font-sans!` / `font-normal!` (the question bubble), or an element that is not a heading (a label).
- **Kept warm while it is in use.** Cron `assistant-warm` (every 45 minutes) calls the mode `warm`: only if someone asked in the last 3 hours, a request that writes nothing (`max_tokens: 0`, the same beginning as a question) reads the cache and starts its hour again — ~0.5 cent. An idle day costs nothing. (A fixed office-hours schedule would cost ~0.25 USD a day: each day's first call writes the cache.)
- **The week's briefing.** The Dashboard's first card, "This week" ([WeeklyBriefingCard](src/features/dashboard/components/WeeklyBriefingCard.jsx)), shows `app_settings.assistant_briefing`, built **every 5 minutes** (cron `assistant-briefing`, `3-59/5 * * * *`, three minutes after the stock is read, mode `briefing`, [20261126_assistant_briefing_live.sql](supabase/migrations/20261126_assistant_briefing_live.sql); an admin can build it again from the card) — until 2026-11-26 only on Monday at 7 AM Eastern: that Thursday its Broken links tile still showed Monday's 283 with 222 broken that day (user: "Todo ese panel debe actualizarse al día, en vivo pues"). The job's body lost its `cron: true`, which makes the function skip any hour but 7 AM Eastern; no deploy was needed. The card reads the setting again every minute while the page is in view and when the person comes back to the tab, so an open Dashboard follows it. Its days run from today to six days ahead (what starts and ends in the next 7 days), so the range in its title moves with the day. Its Broken links count is one per product and store over the three sites, Walmart USA left out: it is not the Where to Buy panel's count, which is per site (222 against 205 / 202 / 17 that day). Built by [_shared/assistantBriefing.js](supabase/functions/_shared/assistantBriefing.js) **straight from the data — no model writes it**: per market, the products that sell and have no stock, and those whose stock lasts under 21 days at the 90-day pace, each with its arrival date; the promotions starting and ending in the next 7 days; the broken Where to Buy links of the pages by store (Walmart USA left out, as in the panel); the products waiting for Odoo. No email: the PIM has no mail service. **Its look** (user, the same day, about a first version of three columns of sentences: "No me gusta como se ve eso"): the week's numbers as tiles — Selling, no stock / Running low (Canada, USA), Promotions (starting, ending), Broken links (links, products), Waiting for Odoo — and a tile opens its detail under them, one at a time, pressed again it closes: the top 5 per country as a small table (sold or stock, on order, arrival), the promotions by day, the broken links by store, the Odoo reasons. From 1024 px it opens on Selling, no stock; on a phone it starts closed, two tiles across. The tiles are the Dashboard's own (Marketplace Health's box and label), an open one takes the app's selected look (the primary border over `bg-primary-container/30`), and each detail ends in a footer strip like the Price Alignment card's (what the numbers mean, and the link to the page where it is fixed).
- **Cost, measured 2026-11-11:** what is always sent is ~26,000 tokens. The first question after an hour of silence ≈ 0.20–0.25 USD (the cache is written), the following ones 0.03–0.12 USD each (more lookups, more cost); an answer takes 8–40 seconds.
- **Checked that day:** every lookup against live data; twelve real questions through the same loop, SDK and knowledge as the function, from the dev machine with the service key (among them "what is this product missing and is its price right in the stores" from a product's page, and two changes asked for and only prepared); **the user's own first four questions from the page, through the deployed function** (12:06–12:09 Eastern); the keep-alive (0.21 USD cold, 0.0055 warm, and the next question read that same cache); the function's refusals (no session, the anon key, the service key as a session; a mode without the cron secret, an admin or the service role); the screens in a browser with a stand-in sign-in and canned answers — attach an image, ask, Confirm a card (the app's own PATCH went out), thumb down with a note, Excel, Conversations, the panel over the Dashboard with the briefing, Settings — at 1440 / 390 in both themes: no sideways scroll, and axe's one finding is the top bar's account button (`label-content-name-mismatch`), not these screens. **Not run for real yet:** a card's Confirm against the live database, an attachment through the deployed function, a person who is not an admin (the team's guide), the Monday cron.

### PIM completeness (Listing Health → PIM tab)

The rules live in [supabase/functions/_shared/completeness.js](supabase/functions/_shared/completeness.js) (re-exported by `src/features/products/lib/completeness.js`), so the panel, the browser snapshot (`takeSnapshot`) and the daily `health-refresh` snapshot score the same way — after changing a rule, redeploy `health-refresh` with its shared files. Archived and obsolete products are left out (`inCompleteness`; `completenessContext` still takes every non-archived product, so a UPC an obsolete product shares is caught). User decisions of 2026-10-05:
- **The mandatory fields are the user's list for Overview and Specifications** (2026-10-05): SKU, UPC, Brand, Manufacturer, Category, Series, Product Type, Country, HS Code, the six warranty fields; Material, Finish, Craftsmanship, the sink fields (Physical Properties + Bowl Configuration), the faucet fields (Faucet Configuration, Handles / Spray / Cartridge, Faucet Dimensions), all of Dimensions & Weight and Shipping. Content, Pricing and Media keep their earlier checks.
- **Each field only where it applies** (user choice): bowl configuration / grooves / grids / strainer / radius / low divider / cabinet sizes / sink deck only on kitchen-type sinks; Basin Split and the per-bowl depths on double bowls; Spout Type, Swivel, Lock, Hot & Cold, Cold Start, Spray Included and Install Hole (mm) on kitchen faucets (and pot fillers); Spout Rotation when the spout swivels; the spray fields when a spray is included; the hose model on pull-downs. Overall Shape is required on every faucet (Spout Type does not stand in for it). An EMPTY Yes/No is missing — a stored "No" counts. **An on / off switch of the product page is never missing** (user, 2026-11-19: "el producto lo tiene apagado, significa que no tiene, pero lo marca como faltante"; `onOff` in completeness.js): the page draws a value that was never stored as off, exactly like a stored No, and a save with the switch off stores nothing (`isEmptyish` in ProductDetail), so nobody could answer "No" there — off IS the answer. The seven checks that read a switch (`AttrField type="boolean"`): Low Divider, Has Grooves, Includes Grids, Hot & Cold Dispenser, Handle(s) Included, Cold Start Handle, Spray Included. A Yes / No DROPDOWN keeps the first rule: its empty choice shows empty. That day, with the rules before and after on all 378 scored products: 105 "missing" went away (Hot & Cold Dispenser 48, Cold Start Handle 46, Low Divider 5 — C136L, E-120, S-520C / C1 / C2 —, Includes Grids 3, Spray Included 3), none appeared, 59 scores moved (average 94.9 → 95.4, complete products 101 → 104). Nothing was written to the products: where an exporter tells No from empty (Wayfair's Short Height Divider), a switch never stored still goes out empty. **`health-refresh` and `assistant` carry these rules and were NOT deployed that day**: until their deploy of 2026-11-23 (at the user's word, with the Craftsmanship rule below) the daily snapshot and Analytics still counted those 105 gaps, and the assistant still named them.
- **Only a sink has a Craftsmanship** (user, 2026-11-22: first "los faucets no tienen craftsmanship, hay que quitárselo", then, asked about the accessories, "solo los sinks de cocina y los porcelain sinks traen el craftsmanship, lo demás no lo trae … todo lo demás en templates o envíos a marketplaces saldrá como NO, no valid o lo que sea"). `hasCraftsmanship(category)` in [_shared/categories.js](supabase/functions/_shared/categories.js) is the one list (`SINK_CATEGORIES`): the kitchen-type sinks — kitchen, bar / prep, laundry, outdoor, read as his "sinks de cocina" and said to him — and the bathroom (porcelain) ones. On a faucet or an accessory the product page does not show the field, it is no completeness check (`cats: SINKS`; the key stays), the PIM Excel's sheets have no "Craftsmanship Type" column (the two faucet sheets lost it; the accessory sheets never had one), and the import does not write that column onto such a row (a vendor's sheet, a Full Report or a PIM Excel downloaded before that day still has it) nor count it as missing. Sinks keep it as it was.
  - **A marketplace that asks is given its own word for "none".** Of the 44 listing templates in Templates only two ask: Wayfair's bathroom sinks (588) and kitchen faucets (653, Recommended) — the rule in wayfairMapping.js answers what is not a sink with Wayfair's "No Craftsmanship", whatever is stored, which is what every faucet sent until then. BB&B's "Customization" (Handmade / Made-to-Order / Personalized) has no word for none: a sink that is Handmade says so, everything else leaves it empty, as before. No other template has such a column, and the pushes through an API send no craftsmanship for any product, sinks included (Wayfair's API rules have none) — not changed.
  - Checked that day with the rules before and after on all 378 scored products: the 108 faucets and the 97 accessories have one check less; the "missing" went away on K-153S (the one faucet without a value) and on the 97 accessories (all empty: average 88.9 → 91.3, the catalog's 95.4 → 96.0), none appeared, nothing else changed; Wayfair's column gives the same answer on every sink and faucet but K-153S (empty → "No Craftsmanship"). 8 kitchen sinks have no value and still count it as missing (C133L, C234L and the six S-828 / S-831 kits). The page was not opened in a browser.
  - **Deployed 2026-11-23 at the user's word** ("Desplegar health-refresh y assistant"): `health-refresh` v53 and `assistant` v12 (the Management API's count), each with the shared files its index.ts imports (8 and 22), `verify_jwt` off as it was; after it both answer a call without credentials with their own refusal. The deploy also carried what had waited in those files since their deploy of 2026-11-13: the on / off switches rule of 2026-11-19 (above), and in the assistant the Operations tab's group and Cabinet Depot's removal. The first daily snapshot with these rules is that day's 1 PM Eastern run, which replaces the morning's rows of the day.
  - **The value the faucets stored was removed on 2026-11-23** (user: "SI BORRALO, CON TAL NO AFECTE EN NADA NI EN NINGUN ARCHIVO"): "No Craftsmanship" came off the 107 faucets that held it (50 kitchen faucets, 12 combos, 6 cold water taps, 36 bathroom faucets, 3 pot fillers; no accessory stored one), in one statement with one Activity Log entry that lists them. Compared before and after, product by product: only that value and the last-updated stamp changed on those 107, the other 271 products not at all; every completeness result and the daily snapshot's rows are the same, and so is every answer of Wayfair's file rules (36,747 compared). What it leaves in sight, said to him: in the Full Report those faucets' Craftsmanship cell is empty and their Last Updated is that day, and each one's History has the change. Nothing else reads the value on a faucet (the keyword generator's prompt no longer carries the line).
- **Groups are the product page tabs** (Overview, Specifications, Content, Pricing, Images / Documents → Media) and each check names its `section`, so the panel links to the tab where the field is filled. Check keys never change: the daily KPI snapshots count gaps by key.
- **Filled but wrong counts as missing.** A check returns `true` / `false`, or a string = the reason (shown next to the field): UPC with a wrong check digit or not 12–14 digits, or on another SKU too ("Same UPC as …", needs `completenessContext(products)` passed to `scoreCompleteness`); HS code not `####.##.####`; a country not in the dropdown list; a shipping weight under the product weight; Install Hole Ø (mm) more than 2.5 mm away from the inches × 25.4.
- **HS code applies to every category** (it was sinks only).
- **The panel** filters by brand (All / Stylish / Azuni, totals included) and groups By category or By field (`summarizeByCheck`: each field's products missing it with the reason, its tab › section, a search and "Copy N SKUs"; fields nobody misses wait behind a toggle).
- Left as they are on purpose: accessories and colanders keep the EN-FR set / keywords / collection / spec sheet checks, combos don't inherit their components' documents, and the Spare Parts Diagram is not a check.

### Progress to 100% (Analytics, 2026-11-25)

User request (notes pasted from a meeting): "Medir issues semana por semana, algún apartado donde se pueda ver el progreso de mejora de productos … de completitud … Qué listados faltan para llegar al 100%. Cuántos están malos. De PIM y de los marketplaces." Analytics already kept the PIM's weeks (products at 100%, by category, how old the gaps are); of a marketplace it kept only the average score, the linked count and the score bands. **Progress to 100%** is the second section of the Analytics page (`#progress`, in the pinned index, every role): the PIM and the ten marketplaces Listing Health scores, side by side ([ListingProgress](src/features/analytics/components/ListingProgress.jsx), [progress.js](src/features/analytics/lib/progress.js)).
- **One vocabulary for the PIM and the marketplaces**, said on the page and in the Excel: a *listing* = a product as the PIM scores it (its completeness) or as a marketplace's Listing Health scores it; an *issue* = one check one listing fails (a field missing or wrong in the PIM, a failed check on the marketplace); *at 100%* = no issue; *left* = the others; *bad* = a score under 70 (`BAD_UNDER`: Listing Health's Needs Work and Critical bands — my reading of "malos", said to the user). A product that is not on a marketplace yet fails that marketplace's "Listed / Linked" check, as in Listing Health: it is a listing left there, not a bad one by itself.
- **What it shows.**
  - Two headlines — the PIM, and every marketplace together: the open issues, their change in the selected week, the listings left and the bad ones, and a column per week for the last 12 weeks.
  - A table, a row per place (the PIM, the marketplaces in Listing Health's order, then "All marketplaces"): at 100% (meter, count, share), left, bad, open issues, the week's change (fewer is green) and a 12-week line of the issues.
  - A row opens: **Week by week** (the last 8 weeks with numbers: at 100%, left, bad, issues, the change against the week before), **Failing most** (each check with how many listings fail it and the week's change; the PIM's with the tab › section where the field is filled) and the buttons to the listings.
  - **The listings** ([ListingsDialog](src/features/analytics/components/ListingsDialog.jsx)): Left to 100% / Bad / Reached 100% this week / Got worse this week / At 100% with their counts, a search, the choice of one issue, and the order — the closest to 100% first (the quickest wins; Bad opens the worst first). A row is the product, its score (with the score it had when the week began) and what it fails; a check it did not fail then is marked "new", and among those that reached 100% the row names what was fixed. Over the list: "In Week 48: 450 issues fixed · 4 new". The SKU opens the product on the tab of its first gap (a marketplace's: Marketplaces). On a phone a row stacks its score and its issues under the product.
- **The week is the page's own week picker**: how each place stood at the last snapshot of that week (today, for the week in progress) against the last one before it began. The headlines' columns and the rows' lines always run to today.
- **Export to Excel** (every role): `Stylish_PIM_Progress_to_100_<W48>_<date>.xlsx` — Summary (the table, and what moved in the week), Week by week (every place × 12 weeks), **Listings left** (one row per listing and place: score, score at the week's start, Bad / Left, every check it fails, what is new and what was fixed in the week), This week (reached 100% / improved / got worse), By issue, Notes. "Export this list" in a place's list is the same file for that place alone. In the PIM Excel's look ([progressExcel.js](src/features/analytics/lib/progressExcel.js) the sheets, [api/progressExcel.js](src/features/analytics/api/progressExcel.js) the reads), loaded on click, one progress card, one Activity Log entry. The page's "Export week" CSV carries the section's table too.
- **Everything is read from the daily snapshots** (`kpi_snapshots`); nothing is scored when the page opens.
  - The PIM's catalog row (`pim` / `all`) has held every product's score and the products missing each check since September 2026, so **the PIM has its whole history**: issues = the sum of `missing`, bad = the scores under 70.
  - **A marketplace's counts are kept since 2026-11-25.** `channelSnapshotRows(perMarketplaceData, date)` in [_shared/listingHealth.js](supabase/functions/_shared/listingHealth.js) — one function for the daily cron (`health-refresh`) and the page's Take snapshot — writes two rows per marketplace: scope `channel` (as before total, linked, avg, distribution; new `complete` = at 100%, `issues`, `by_check`) and scope `channel_detail` (`scores` per SKU, `missing_skus` per check: ~6.5 KB a marketplace and day). Its weeks before that day show a dash for at 100% and issues, while its bad count — read from the bands — has every week. **No backfill**: what the old rows kept of the issues (the ten checks failed most, in `channel_health`) is not exact where a marketplace fails more than ten (Wayfair, Best Buy).
  - **The detail rows are never loaded whole**: `loadSnapshots` leaves `channel_detail` out, and `loadChannelDetail(keys, dates)` reads the two days a week compares — when a marketplace's list opens, or for the Excel. `marketplaceChecks(marketplace)` gives a check's label, category and severity by its key.
  - `rowAt`: a marketplace's row written without the counts gives way to the newest row of the six days before it that has them — the days between the app's change and the function's deploy; older than that, the counts read as unknown.
  - A sum with a marketplace that has no count is unknown, never the total of the others. A listing no longer scored (archived, obsolete, out of a store's brand) fixed nothing: "N no longer scored".
- **A change of the rules reads as issues fixed.** Week 48's −447 PIM issues include the ~200 that left with the rule changes deployed on 2026-11-23 (the on / off switches, Craftsmanship only on sinks); the rest is the team's work that week (Series −40, Pull-Down Hose Model −43, Warranty −14…). Nothing tells the two apart: say it when a rule changes.
- **Not covered:** the marketplaces Listing Health does not score (Amazon, Home Depot, Lowe's, Rona, BB&B / Overstock, Menards: template-only, the PIM cannot read their listings), and the Where to Buy links, which keep their own panel.
- On the way: the `Columns` chart no longer draws or reads out a week without a snapshot as a zero; what opens under a row of the table keeps to the part of the table in view (a ResizeObserver on its scroll box), since on a phone the table scrolls sideways; the assistant's note on `kpi_snapshots` names its scopes and warns that the per-listing rows are large.
- **Checked 2026-11-25.** The section's own code on the live history, read only: the PIM's numbers equal the database's (377 scored, 227 at 100%, 150 left, 4 bad, 502 issues, −447 in the week; W47 949 / 101), today's marketplace rows as the new code builds them have the totals the cron wrote that day, and each totals row agrees with its detail row on the ten marketplaces (2,089 issues, 1,579 listings left, 78 bad). 11 edge cases of the calculation (old-shape rows, the six-day rule, sums, a listing that left, a new one), with `health-refresh`'s own file loading on the dev machine with its shared files. In a browser on a scratch build, with a stand-in sign-in, the history answered from files read that day, any other read passed on read-only and every write kept in the test — 88 checks at 1440 / 390 in both themes: the table, a row opened from the keyboard, the lists of the PIM and of Wayfair Canada (filters, search, issue, order, "+N more", the product links), both Excel files opened and read back (Listings left = 150 + 1,579 rows), another week, the week CSV, Escape and the focus — axe 0, no sideways scroll, the document never scrolls; again with a made-up start of the week for the marketplaces (89), as a viewer (46), and against the live history once today's rows were saved (46). The page's Take snapshot with its save kept in the test: one save, ten totals rows and ten detail rows that agree. `impeccable detect`: nothing on the two components.
- **State 2026-11-25: live.** That afternoon the day's marketplace rows were saved in the new shape (the same save as the page's Take snapshot: the day's ten totals rows with the same totals plus the counts, and ten detail rows; the rows before are kept in that session's scratchpad), so the history starts that day. **`health-refresh` was deployed the same evening at the user's "Sí, despliega"** (v54 by the Management API's count, 8 files, `verify_jwt` off as it was; its files had no commit since v53, so the deploy carried only this change). After it: a call without credentials gets 401, and one run through the scheduled job's own command wrote the day's rows — ten totals rows with the counts and ten detail rows, each pair agreeing (Best Buy 265 at 100% and 168 issues on that pull). The app's change went to main the same evening at his "Sí, commit y push a main" (only this change's files: what another session had pending on the ScoreCard stayed in the working tree). **Not seen yet:** the 6 AM run of the next morning, and a second week of marketplace counts — the first week-over-week change of a marketplace's issues shows in Week 49.

### Auth & roles

Supabase Auth + a `profiles` table with roles `admin | editor | viewer`. `ProtectedRoute` gates login; `RequireRole` gates routes (`/import` → admin+editor, `/users` and `/activity` → admin only; `/settings` is every role's since 2026-11-23 and shows the PIM's rules to admins only — see Passwords below). Anything needing the service_role key (creating/deleting users, etc.) lives in the `admin-users` edge function — never in the browser. RLS is enforced via migrations in `supabase/migrations/` (see `20260614_rls_lockdown.sql` and the storage RLS migrations).

### Passwords (2026-11-23)

User request: "permitir que los usuarios puedan cambiar su clave directamente … un menú dentro de Settings que seccione todo, y en una parte diga como Security" (the password an admin generates "es una contraseña muy difícil y muy loca"), "desde el login gestionar olvidé mi contraseña … le va a enviar un correo allí bien diseñado", and "cuando un usuario nuevo ingrese al sistema, que de una vez le salga como una alerta … el mismo estilo de alertas, por ejemplo, el de las fotos". Until that day only an admin could give someone a new password (Users → Reset password), and the sign-in page said so.
- **Settings has a menu and is for everyone** ([SettingsPage](src/pages/SettingsPage.jsx)). One section at a time, named in the address (`/settings?section=security`), so a link opens the right one: **Your account** — Security, every role — and **PIM rules**, admins only — Promotions (the automation and the file owners), Warranty documents, Stock, Odoo, Assistant (`SECTIONS`; a new section is one entry there). It opens on Security; a section this person may not see, or an unknown one, opens the first. A column that stays in view from lg, a strip that scrolls inside itself under it; its items are links with `aria-current`. Each section loads its own data when it is shown, so nobody but an admin reads the PIM's rules (the database refuses them anyway). The sidebar shows Settings to every role, the account chip's panel has **Change password**, and the Dashboard's Odoo foot opens `?section=odoo`.
- **Security → Password** ([SecuritySettings](src/features/auth/components/SecuritySettings.jsx), [password.js](src/features/auth/api/password.js), [passwordRules.js](src/features/auth/lib/passwordRules.js)). ONE field, New password, with the eye to check it — no form asks for a password twice ([PasswordField](src/features/auth/components/PasswordField.jsx): the hint above the box, the error under it and in `aria-describedby` only while it shows; a hidden `username` field tells the browser's password manager which account to update).
  - **The rules are the sign-in service's**: 10 characters at least (`MIN_PASSWORD_LENGTH`, the project's Auth policy, what `admin-users` checks too) and never a password found in a known data leak (Supabase's leaked-password check). The browser says the length first; the service's own refusals — leaked, the same password again — are said on the field (`authErrorText`).
  - **The current password is asked only when the sign-in is not fresh** (`signedInRecently`: the last sign-in with a password or a reset link was under 10 minutes ago). A new person on their first visit just chooses the new one; a session open for days proves the current one first, so a computer left unlocked is not enough. It is checked by a sign-in on a client of its own that keeps nothing and is closed at once (`checkCurrentPassword`): the page's session is not touched. **This is the page's rule, not the service's** — Supabase would take the change from any live session (`security_update_password_require_current_password` is off); it guards the person at the keyboard, not a stolen session. Under that field: "Don't remember it? Email me a link instead" (the same email as the sign-in page's).
  - **The save writes two things at once**, the password and `user_metadata.password_set_at` (`updateUser({ password, data })`): the note that the password is now the person's own. The card says which it is ("You are still on the temporary password you were given" / "You chose your password on …"), and the Activity Log gets "Changed their own password" / "Chose a new password from a reset link".
- **"Forgot your password?"** on the sign-in page ([Login](src/features/auth/pages/Login.jsx)) is an address of its own, `/login?forgot=1` (the browser's Back returns to the form): the email typed is kept, **Send reset link** asks Supabase Auth for the email (`sendPasswordReset`, `redirectTo` = this site's `/reset-password`), and the answer is the same whether the address has an account or not ("If there is a PIM account for … a link is on its way. It works for 1 hour, and only once").
  - **The link opens `/reset-password?token_hash=…&type=recovery`** ([ResetPassword](src/features/auth/pages/ResetPassword.jsx), a public route with the sign-in page's shell, [AuthShell](src/features/auth/components/AuthShell.jsx)). **Opening the page spends nothing: the link's token is taken when the person saves the new password** (`openResetLink` = `verifyOtp({ token_hash, type: 'recovery' })`, then the same save as Settings) — mail scanners open the links of an email, and a link that worked by being opened would be used up by them.
