A refused password is retried without the link (it was already taken); saving signs the person in and opens the Dashboard. A link that expired or was used says so with "Send me a new link". A reset link that lands on another address of the app is taken to the page (App.jsx — the mail service falls back to the site's front page when it is not given the page).
  - **The email** is [supabase/templates/recovery.html](supabase/templates/recovery.html): the brand's mark drawn with table cells (no image: Gmail shows no SVG), "Choose a new password", the account's address, one pill button, "works for 1 hour, and only once", "Didn't ask for this?", the address to copy, both color schemes. Its button is `{{ .RedirectTo }}?token_hash={{ .TokenHash }}&type=recovery`. No Outlook conditional comments: the service's template engine drops HTML comments. **`node scripts/apply-auth-email.mjs`** (`--dry-run` first) puts it into Supabase Auth with three more settings — the subject "Reset your Stylish PIM password", `site_url` = https://pim-stylish.vercel.app and the addresses a link may return to (production and `http://localhost:5173`, existing entries kept). Applied 2026-11-23; before it the site URL was Supabase's default `http://localhost:3000`, the list empty and the email Supabase's plain one. Run it again after changing the template.
  - **The PIM still has no mail service, so today the email reaches only jose@stylishkb.com.** Supabase's own mailer writes only to the members of the Supabase organisation (one) and a couple of emails an hour; for anyone else "Send reset link" answers "The PIM cannot send email to this address yet. Ask an administrator to reset your password." It needs SMTP in Supabase Auth (a sending service such as Resend with the company's domain, or the SMTP of a company mailbox) — the user's to decide and to give the credentials for; then raise `rate_limit_email_sent`. **Left as it is at the user's word the same day** ("será dejarlo así"), after looking at both ways: a sending service (Resend) needs its DNS records added to the domain, whose DNS is at Bluehost, which he did not want to go into; and the company's mail is Microsoft 365, where a mailbox sends over SMTP only if its admin enables "Authenticated SMTP", an access Microsoft switches off by default at the end of December 2026. So a person who forgets their password still asks an admin (Users → Reset password). A send-only Resend key he made is kept as `RESEND_API_KEY` in `.env.secrets.local`, not used by anything: to take it up again, the domain is added and verified in Resend first, and only then is Supabase's SMTP pointed at it (before that it would stop the email that reaches him today).
- **The reminder** ([PasswordNudge](src/components/layout/PasswordNudge.jsx)), the twin of the photo reminder and hanging off the same account chip: "Choose your own password — You are still on the temporary one you were given", **Update password** (opens Security) and **Later**. For anyone whose account has no `password_set_at` — every new account, and the 15 accounts that existed that day, all on generated passwords (my reading of "usuario nuevo", said to the user). It comes up 1.5 s after the page settles and does not fade by itself; Later, the ×, opening the account menu or the Security section put it away for 3 days (`pim.passwordReminder.<user>` in the browser); choosing a password ends it. While it is up the photo reminder waits (`blocked`), then takes its usual delay. Dev console: `passwordNudge()`.
- **An admin's reset brings the reminder back**: `admin-users` `resetPassword` also clears `password_set_at` (a null takes the key out of the metadata, the name and role stay). **Deployed 2026-11-23 at the user's "Sí, despliega"** (`admin-users` v33 by the Management API's count, `verify_jwt` on as it was; the version before, v32, was of 2026-08-16, so the deploy carried only this change). After it: a call without credentials is turned away at the gate, and one with the public key and no session gets the function's own "Invalid or expired session." A reset through the deployed function was not run: the first one an admin makes in Users is the first.
- **If the captcha is switched on in Supabase** (the sign-in page already takes a Turnstile key), the current-password check and Settings' "Email me a link" send no captcha token and would be refused: give them the widget then.
- **Checked that day.** In a browser against stand-in services (nothing reached Supabase; every call answered and kept by the test), 160 checks at 1440 / 390 in both themes: the sign-in page and its two new views (the address, the focus, the email asked for with `/reset-password` as its return), the reset page (opening spends nothing, a short and a leaked password, one use of the link, an expired link, a link on the front page), a new viewer (the reminder within seconds, the photo reminder waiting, a menu with Security only, no current password asked, one save with the note, an admin's section in the address not opened), an admin on a days-old session (the six sections each alone, a wrong current password stops the save and takes the focus back, the right one saves and the check's session is closed, the emailed link, the account menu's row) and Later — axe 0 on the sign-in pages and nothing but the top bar's account button elsewhere, no sideways scroll, the document never scrolls. Against the real service, with a throwaway account made for the run and deleted at its end (no email sent), 20 checks: the save with the note, the old password refused and the new one taken, the check's session closed with the page's alive, `weak_password` (leaked, short) and `same_password` as the page reads them, a reset link taken once and refused the second time (`otp_expired`), and an admin's reset clearing the note and keeping name and role. The email was drawn with sample values at 700 / 375 in both schemes. **Run for real the same day, by the user, on production:** "Forgot your password?" on jose@ — the email arrived, its button opened the page and the new password was saved ("llegó el correo, actualizó la contraseña y todo bien"). **Not run for real:** the email to anyone but jose@ (no mail service yet), and Settings → Security against the real service in a browser.

### Data model conventions

- **The PIM owns the WHERE TO BUY links of the Stylish pages** (user, 2026-10-19: "que PIM sea dueño, de esa forma podemos automatizar"). `product_where_to_buy` (sku, retailer, url — [20261019_product_where_to_buy.sql](supabase/migrations/20261019_product_where_to_buy.sql)) is ONE list per product: [_shared/whereToBuy.js](supabase/functions/_shared/whereToBuy.js) (re-exported by `src/features/products/lib/whereToBuy.js`) holds the store registry — one label per store, each under its own country (Build with Rise is Canada) — and builds BOTH pages' section from it: Stylish Canada = "In Stores in Canada" (store locator) + Online in Canada + Online in USA, Stylish USA = Online in USA + Online in Canada. The product page's **Where to Buy** tab edits it ("Save and publish": `publishWhereToBuy` reads each live page and replaces only that section, `only: ["additionalInfoSections"]`), then calls the audit's `sync` mode. Nobody edits that section in Wix any more; the daily scan reports pages that differ from the PIM ("Publish the PIM's links" in the panel). Loaded on 2026-10-19 from the live pages by `scripts/import-where-to-buy-from-wix.mjs` (5,534 links, 344 products; where the two pages disagreed: the address the audit found OK, then the page of the store's own country). The store locator, document links and other sites (Loblaws…) are not kept. BedBathandBeyond.ca and Overstock.ca no longer exist (user, 2026-10-20): out of the registry, their 240 links deleted and the pages republished. Nor does Faucetline.com (user OK 2026-10-29): its domain is parked and sends every address, even a made-up one, to unrelated sites (an adult one among them) — as "Check by hand" its 43 links had stayed on the pages; they were deleted (backup in that day's session scratchpad, one Activity Log entry) and the pages republished. Nor does Cabinet Depot's online shop (user, 2026-11-22: "si cerró vamos a quitar todo eso y no lo contamos"): shop.cabinetdepotnewmarket.com answers "This store is unavailable" since the pass of 2026-11-16 (the dealer's own website is still up, with a link to that closed shop). Out of the registry and of Price Alignment's page stores; its 191 links (381 check rows, all Broken, so none was on a page and no page changed) were deleted that day, the list kept in the Activity Log entry. The Sinks Direct links were checked against the live Sinks Direct catalogs the same day (`wix-pull-catalog`'s `url`; Wix writes `base//product-page/…`, normalize the double slash): 20 links on 16 products pointed to another product's page or a gone one (S-322XG → S-305XG's page, K-131GR → K-131SR's) and were replaced (source `auto`). Since then **the Sinks Direct links follow the stores by themselves** (`refreshSinksDirect` in every scan: the product's own page; a product HIDDEN on that store loses the link — its page answers 404 — and gets it back when shown; user chose this over making the 141 hidden SD USA products visible, 2026-10-20). **The websites show only working links that open the product** (user, 2026-10-20: "solo dejar los OK"): `linksToShow` leaves out every link `isHiddenLink` names — broken, or "Wrong product" (its page shows another product or another option of it, `sku_check` other / variant) — until it is fixed; an "Other finish" link (`listing`: the right Wayfair listing on its default option) stays; a link not verified yet stays shown (user's choice the same day: BB&B / Overstock wait for ScraperAPI). **A link whose page says the product is sold out leaves the pages too, until the store has it again** (user, 2026-10-26: some marketplaces showed sold-out products as Broken — "Ocultar hasta que vuelva"): `in_stock` false on any `sku_check` but `listing` (an Other finish page shows the default option's stock). Such a link stays OK, shows as **Out of stock** (panel stat, product tab chip, Excel status — not a problem, not in To fix) and is read again on every check day (Monday / Wednesday / Friday), so it comes back on the first pass that finds it in stock. Best Buy takes the product page down (404) while our offer has no stock: a dead page at the offer's own product id, with the latest `channel_health` 'bestbuy' snapshot saying the offer is inactive or at 0 units, is Out of stock with that reason instead of Broken (`bestBuySoldOut`); an address with another id (an old listing) stays Broken. The sold-out readings were checked against our own stock on 2026-10-26: of 137 Wayfair USA links read sold out, 130 were products at 0 units or not stocked in the USA (Lowe's 100 of 106; its scraper's `in_stock` is delivery availability, `available_to_delivery`, not a store's). The audit itself writes the pages (Wix product PATCH of `additionalInfoSections`) — a link leaving the pages or coming back queues its product in `app_settings.where_to_buy_publish_queue`, drained by its own run every 5 minutes (mode `publish`, cron `where-to-buy-publish`, up to 10 products / 20 pages — the check calls only drain it with time left over, and at 20 pages at a time they had none) — Wix asks to wait after ~30 changes — and the product tab's Save publishes through the same `sync` mode.
  - **The Azuni store's WHERE TO BUY is the PIM's too** (user, 2026-11-04: "Sí, hazlo"): `azuni_ca` is a third `WTB_SITES` entry (its own words: "Click here to locate a Dealer near you." — the here, plain underlined text on the old pages, now opens azuni.ca's dealer page `AZUNI_DEALERS_URL` https://www.azuni.ca/where-to-buy — then Online in Canada, Online in USA; `alwaysOwned`: the PIM's list rules every Azuni page, so **a product without a store link has no WHERE TO BUY** (user, 2026-11-04: "si no hay link, no hay where to buy") — the new C236L lost its section; C234L, C133L and C136L keep theirs because it is their page's ONLY section and Wix ignores an empty list: their first Push to Azuni adds the PIM's sections and drops it) and one of the audit's `SITES`; every scan / sync / publish / link-claim checks the brand per site (`sellsOn` = `siteSells`: Azuni only on its store, Stylish only on theirs — an Azuni product's stale links to Stylish pages, A906–A913, C122, C233, are skipped). Loaded the same day from the 23 Azuni pages (183 links; 62 to stores that no longer exist — Overstock.ca, Houzz, Faucetline — skipped; the PIM's own Sinks Direct links kept; C132, C133L, C136L, C234L, C236L had none); the first check hid what was broken or sold out (C126L's Amazon.ca and Walmart.ca: 404) and the pages were republished from the PIM. Its panel sits under Listing Health → Azuni → Where to Buy (user, 2026-11-09: "no puede estar dentro de stylish"), the Excel export / import take Azuni products, and "Push Azuni Canada" publishes it.
- `products` keyed by `sku`; media in `product_media` (`is_primary` + `display_order`, storage bucket `product-images/<SKU>/`).
- Videos and documents are FAMILY-SHARED (since 2026-07-30): uploading to one variant registers a row on every product with the same `family_number`, all pointing at ONE storage object; removal clears the whole family and the file is only deleted from Storage when no row references it (`deleteStorageObjectsIfUnreferenced`). Images stay per-variant. See `src/features/media/api/media.js`; `scripts/dedupe-family-media.mjs` dedupes pre-existing per-variant copies.
- **Image language** (`product_media.language`): NULL = Universal = language-neutral (the white main, the gray hero, loose photos); `en` / `fr` / `en_fr` / `en_es` = the artwork set the image belongs to.
  - A visual review on 2026-10-05 retagged 1,005 Universal images on 93 products (backup and plan in the session scratchpad; one `audit_log` row). Text images took the language of their text. The 6 English-only slides that live inside an EN-FR set stay `en_fr` with their set (user decision). Text-free photos took the set their file name names (`_e_f` / `_f_r` / `_ef` = EN-FR, `_e` = EN, `_e_s` = EN-ES, `_fr` = FR). Mains stay Universal even with text.
  - Every image push sends ONE text set plus every Universal photo (`SET_ORDER`, same in wix-push-media, wayfair-push-content, walmart-add-items): Canada sites and Wayfair (one listing for both markets) EN-FR → EN + FR → EN-ES; USA sites and Walmart USA EN → EN-ES → EN-FR. Universal used to count as a set of its own, so untagged EN-FR sets rode along with their EN-ES copies (S-627W sent 34 images, half duplicates) and K-131N/S sent French slides to US sites.
- Only two brands exist: **Stylish** and **Azuni** (user rule 2026-09-29). [brands.js](src/features/products/lib/brands.js) is the single list (every brand input is a dropdown; imports map any casing and refuse other values) and `products_brand_check` enforces it in the database ([20260929_brand_values.sql](supabase/migrations/20260929_brand_values.sql)). A new brand needs both. An Azuni product's Manufacturer is always "Azuni" (user rule 2026-10-06): the `products_azuni_manufacturer` trigger writes it on every insert / update ([20261006_azuni_manufacturer.sql](supabase/migrations/20261006_azuni_manufacturer.sql)) and the product page offers only Azuni; Stylish products keep theirs.
- Country of Origin is a dropdown of every country (user request 2026-10-01): [countries.js](src/features/products/lib/countries.js) (a re-export of `supabase/functions/_shared/countries.js`, so PIM completeness checks against the same list) is the single list (200 countries, ISO alpha-2 + alpha-3). The PIM stores the English name in `attributes.country_of_origin` ("China", "Vietnam" — CN / VN from the user's list); imports turn codes and other spellings into it (`canonicalCountry`; a 2-letter code counts only in capitals and never NA / NO), and exporters take codes from `findCountry`: Walmart Canada `"VN - Viet Nam"` (its own wording, `WALMART_COUNTRY_NAME`; no Iran/Libya/North Korea/Somalia/Syria), Lowe's alpha-3 (`VNM`, in its template list). The `products.country_of_origin` / `manufacturer` / `warranty` COLUMNS are read by nothing: those three live in `attributes`, and the catalog's bulk edit writes them there (`bulkUpdateProducts(skus, patch, attrPatch)` merges per product).
- The warranty document is GENERAL per brand (user rule 2026-09-29): one PDF for Stylish and one for Azuni, set in Settings → Warranty documents (`app_settings.brand_warranty`, [brandWarranty.js](src/features/settings/api/brandWarranty.js)). Saving one runs `apply_brand_warranty(brand)` ([20260929_brand_warranty.sql](supabase/migrations/20260929_brand_warranty.sql)): every product of the brand gets a `warranty_file` row pointing at that ONE storage object (their old copies are deleted when unreferenced), and the `products_brand_warranty` trigger gives new products (or a brand change) the brand's file. On the product page the Warranty row is read-only once the brand has one. Every reader of `product_media` (Wix links, Wayfair documents, template exports) keeps working unchanged. Documents can be dropped onto their row (DocumentsSection) as well as uploaded, and each linked one has a Download button that saves it under its own file name without opening it (`getDownloadUrl` in media.js: Supabase Storage's `?download=<name>`, user 2026-10-13).
- Bullets are normalized by the database (user request 2026-10-05, [20261005_normalize_bullets.sql](supabase/migrations/20261005_normalize_bullets.sql)): the `products_normalize_bullets` trigger runs `normalize_bullet()` on every write of `attributes.bullet_points` / `bullet_points_fr` — whoever writes them (product page, import, variant propagation, scripts).
  - The house style is "LABEL IN CAPITALS: Text starting with a capital."
  - The label before ": " is upper-cased (mixed-case marks like cUPC kept).
  - The first letter after the colon is upper-cased (a lower-case "cupc" → "cUPC").
  - CSV quotes and surrounding spaces are removed; nothing else changes.
  - The existing 632 bullets on 86 products were cleaned that day.
  - Change the rule there, never in the browser.
- Faucets have a **Spare Parts Diagram** document (`spare_parts_diagram`, PDF, user 2026-10-05, [20261005_spare_parts_diagram.sql](supabase/migrations/20261005_spare_parts_diagram.sql)). Like every faucet document it is family-shared; other categories only show it while one is stored.
  - It goes ONLY to the Sinks Direct and Stylish websites: a Wix push lists "SPARE PARTS DIAGRAM" in DOCUMENTS TO DOWNLOAD (`deriveWixSectionsFromPim(…, site)`).
  - **A Wix store's documents section is built from the PIM's documents** (user, 2026-11-04: as with the WHERE TO BUY links, "los que no tenga documento, no aparece"): `buildDocumentsHtml` in [wixInfoSections.js](src/features/syndication/lib/wixInfoSections.js) — INSTALLATION GUIDE (the sink's own mounting first), PDF CUT-OUT TEMPLATE, DXF FILE, SPEC. SHEET (the Azuni store: SPEC SHEET, section TECHNICAL DOCUMENTS), SPARE PARTS DIAGRAM; never the warranty. Canada stores take the EN-FR file first, USA ones the English one. A document the PIM lacks is not listed, a product with none loses the section, a page without one gets it. Until then a push only repointed the links it found, so the Azuni store still pointed at Dropbox, and links to files the PIM lacks would leave a page at its next push — so first (user: "Sí, hazlo") every Dropbox link of the five stores' documents sections whose document the PIM lacked was RESCUED into the PIM (2026-11-04: 928 links, 1,400 pages read): 263 documents on 154 products from 110 stored files (bucket product-documents; a product's own page link first, then its family for non-sinks; one per exact type, so dual-mount sinks keep both guides and DXFs; never over a document the PIM had of that kind). Not stored: S-520C1 / S-520C2 spec sheets (gone on Dropbox) and four links that were no documents (A-02's video, an A-909 Dropbox folder, two K130NK147N photos); 35 non-document links in those sections (Optional / Recommended Accessories) leave the pages at the next push. Scripts and lists in that day's session scratchpad `dropbox-rescue/`.
  - No other marketplace gets it.
- **The Sinks Direct and Stylish websites carry EVERY product** (user rule 2026-10-05). Their four exclusion keys (`wix_sinksdirect_ca/_us`, `wix_stylish_ca/_us`) are `alwaysOn` in [marketplaces.js](src/features/syndication/lib/marketplaces.js): `isExcluded` ignores them and the card shows "Always on".
  - The `products_websites_always_on` trigger drops them from `channel_exclusions` on any write ([20261005_websites_always_on.sql](supabase/migrations/20261005_websites_always_on.sql)). The 344 products that had them were cleaned that day.
  - **The Azuni store is always on for every Azuni product too** (user, 2026-11-04: "TODOS los productos de la marca Azuni siempre estarán disponibles en la tienda Azuni … igual que Stylish"): `wix_azuni_ca` is `alwaysOn` and the same trigger drops it ([20261104_azuni_store_always_on.sql](supabase/migrations/20261104_azuni_store_always_on.sql); 328 stored keys removed that day — 327 on Stylish products, moot by the brand rule, and C234L, back on its store). The card shows "Always on" for an Azuni product there and "Not sold (Stylish)" for a Stylish one.
  - **The brand rule is on top, both ways (user, 2026-10-06 "OJO"): Azuni is NEVER on the Stylish websites (USA and Canada) and Stylish is NEVER on the Azuni website; Sinks Direct carries both.** `wixSiteSells` / `siteSells` (Stylish sites `excludedBrands: ['azuni']`, Azuni `onlyBrands: ['azuni']`) gate every Wix push, create, import, Listing Health and price alignment; the product's Excluded marketplaces card shows "Not sold (<brand>)" there instead of "Always on" or a switch. On 2026-10-06 the 8 Azuni products once created on the Stylish sites (A906–A913, C122, C233, Aug 27) no longer existed on Wix — only 16 stale `wix_links` rows remain. The other marketplaces' exclusions are unchanged.
- The product page's Content tab no longer edits QuickBooks description or Ribbon (user, 2026-10-05). Their columns and values stay: QuickBooks is an import name fallback, and the ribbon of 4 products still goes to Wix.
- **UPC and HS Code are kept up to date by the user — never change them from the PTD, the GS1 list or any other file** (user, 2026-10-06), not even to fix what Listing Health flags; report the finding instead. **There are two HS codes** (user, 2026-10-15): `attributes.hs_code` = the USA one (what PIM completeness checks, `####.##.####`, and what every US channel reads) and `attributes.hs_code_ca` = Canada's own tariff code ("HS Code (Canada)" in Trade & Compliance, import header "HS Code Canada"; empty until the user fills it, not a completeness check yet). Home Depot Canada's Basic Data O takes the Canada code, else the USA one. The same hands-off rule covers both. Bulk fills from the PTD (2026-10-06: 2,851 empty fields on 264 products, normalized to the PIM's words — "Molded" → "Poured / Molded", "No Certified" → "Not Certified", "Does Not Appy" → "Does Not Apply") left out the channel IDs (aliases live in the Aliases tab), the PTD's outdated MSRP, and contradictory values (Canada Restriction "Yes" with reason "Not Applicable", Pull-Down Hose Model "Yes").
- **A UPC is on one product only** (user, 2026-11-11: "que no se pueda repetir un UPC en ningún producto"). The `products_unique_upc` trigger ([20261111_products_unique_upc.sql](supabase/migrations/20261111_products_unique_upc.sql)) refuses, whoever writes it, an `attributes.upc` another product already has — the same digits, leading zeros aside (`upc_key()`; `upcKey` in the browser) — and names that product ("UPC … is already on B-124N. A UPC can be on one product only."): the product page shows that message, and the import preview marks such a row as an error before anything is written ([upcCheck.js](src/features/import/lib/upcCheck.js) `flagRepeatedUpcs`: against the whole catalog and within the file). Change the rule in the database, never only in the browser.
  - Only a UPC that is being set or changed is checked (a pair stored before the rule could still be saved). Two products can't swap UPCs in one go: empty one first. **No repeated UPC is left**: the one pair, D-700C2 / D-701C2 (both 840994004963), was fixed by the user that day (D-701C2 → 840994004949).
  - **A UPC must also be well formed to be saved** (user, 2026-11-11: "¿podemos meterle una validación a los UPC?" — format and check digit, and it "no deja guardar"). The `products_valid_upc` trigger ([20261111_products_valid_upc.sql](supabase/migrations/20261111_products_valid_upc.sql)) refuses an `attributes.upc` that is not 12 digits (UPC-A, what all 376 were that day) with the right check digit, and says which (`upc_problem()`): "UPC 84099400440 is not valid: it has 11 digits and a UPC has 12." Only a UPC being set or changed is checked, an empty one is none, spaces around it are removed, and spaces or dashes inside are refused, not cleaned. The message never names the digit that "should" be there: correcting the last digit of a mistyped UPC makes a valid UPC that is not the product's.
    - The browser applies the same rule in the same words before it writes (`upcProblem` in [upcCheck.js](src/features/import/lib/upcCheck.js)): the product page's UPC field says "8 of 12 digits" or what is wrong as it is typed (`note` on `AttrField`) and Save stops without a round trip; the import preview marks the row (`flagInvalidUpcs`) — what Excel does to a UPC (8.40994E+11 → 840994000000) is caught by its check digit; the PIM Excel's UPC cell takes 12 digits.
    - Listing Health's own UPC check (12–14 digits) and `odoo-push`'s copy are unchanged: nothing they would flag can be saved any more.
  - The placeholder 840994000000 came off the six kits that shared it the same day (S-828WHK / WLK / WNK, S-831WHK / WLK / WNK — user: "vamos a quitarle el UPC y que aparezca como faltante"), and minutes later the user gave each the UPC its archived Odoo record holds (840994012791 / …12784 / …12777, 840994012821 / …12814 / …12807). Only A-931 and A-933 have no UPC.
- **What the PIM owns goes to Odoo the moment it is saved** (user, 2026-11-11: "apenas alguien le dé SAVE en PIM la información viaje a Odoo"). These travel, and nothing else is written in Odoo:
  - **UPC** (`attributes.upc`) → the product's Barcode (`product.product.barcode`, shown as "Master UPC").
  - **Shipping box** (`attributes.shipping_dimensions_in` length / width / height) → Shipping Length / Width / Height (Inches) (`shipping_length_in` / `_width_in` / `_height_in` on the template), the three sides together or not at all; **shipping weight** (the `products.shipping_weight_lb` column) → Shipping Weight (Lbs) (`shipping_weight`). Same units, no conversion. Odoo's standard Weight (empty on all but 2) is not written.
  - **Volume (m³)** (user, 2026-11-22: "nos faltó uno, y es el volumen que es el m3. Ese solo lo debe llenar PIM también") → Odoo's Volume (`product.product.volume`, on the Inventory tab's Logistics; Odoo's purchase orders work their CBM out from it). **The PIM keeps no volume of its own: it is the shipping box's**, length × width × height in inches turned into cubic metres, with the decimals Odoo keeps (4, read from Odoo at every run). So it travels WITH the box: in `odoo-push` asking for `box` asks for `volume` too (the function adds it — the app sends nothing new, so a page opened before the change sends it as well), and `fields: ["volume"]` sends it alone. No whole box in the PIM → no volume is sent and Odoo's stays (`no_volume`); a box too small for Odoo's decimals is not sent as 0; if Odoo's setting ever turns its volume into cubic feet the send is held (`volume_unit`), never converted. A product the PIM creates in Odoo gets it. Until that day it was not written: Odoo had filled it by itself only where the box had been empty, and elsewhere kept numbers typed for older boxes (K-141B 0.05 for a box of 0.0165; B-123S 0.03 for 0.0069).
    - **Deployed, first load sent and the field locked in Odoo the same day, each at the user's word** ("Desplegar, enviar la carga inicial y ayúdame a bloquear el campo en Odoo"). `odoo-push` **v11** (the Management API's count; v10 was the source of commit 0bc1b2d). Tried first on the dev machine as a dry run with every write refused there: of the 378, 194 already held the box's volume, 168 would change, 5 have no whole box (A-920, A-802B / C / N, S-405T), 5 are not in Odoo and 6 are archived there; asking for `box` alone gave the same 168 with no box to change; the other fields changed nothing. The same dry run through the deployed function gave the same numbers. **First load: 168 volumes** — K-141B alone first (0.05 → 0.0165, read back from Odoo with its box and weight as they were), then the other 167 in batches of 50, all kept; Odoo's values before are in that run's five Activity Log entries (11 products had none). After it: a dry run with nothing left to send, and straight from Odoo 362 of 362 products with a whole box hold the box's volume. The 5 without a whole box keep Odoo's number (A-920: none).
    - **Odoo's screen locks it too.** The view `product.template.form.pim.readonly` (#5248, made with the user's Odoo login on 2026-11-11) makes a "Managed by PIM" product's Odoo Description, Model Name, UPC, Brand, Category, HS Code, Country of Origin, Color, Material, Finish, Shipping Weight and the three sides of the box read-only; **Volume was added to it that day** through the API with that login (one more `xpath` on `//field[@name='volume']`, the same `readonly="x_managed_by_pim"` and a help text; the rest of the view untouched, and the Products form read back with the 15 fields locked). The lock is the screen's: the API still writes. A change to that view is a change to Odoo's screen — only at the user's word, the view read first and kept before it is written.
  - **Product weight** (`attributes.product_weight_lb`) → Overall Product Weight; **Model Name** (the `model_name` column) → Model Name; **Country of Origin** → Country of Origin (Odoo's country of that name); **HS Code** (`attributes.hs_code`, the USA one) → HS Code, which in Odoo is a CLOSED list of 17 codes: a code it lacks is held (`hs_not_listed` — 26 products on 2026-11-11: 7324.10.0010 on 20, 3924.10.4000 on 4, 8307.10.3000, 3405.90.0000; Pedro adds the option in Odoo, the hourly retry then sends it). User, 2026-11-11: "SI" to each. Obsolete → Odoo's Discontinued: "NO".
  - **Main image** (the product's `is_primary` image) → the product's picture in Odoo, sent whole whenever asked (two pictures cannot be compared cheaply): [media.js](src/features/media/api/media.js) asks when the main image is set or first uploaded. The first load went only to the 19 products Odoo had no picture for (17 sent; A-911 and K-153S have no main image in the PIM) — the 348 pictures Odoo already had were not replaced.
  - **Marketplace ids** (`ids`; user, 2026-11-11: "SI … hazlo todos, incluso aquellos que no estén") — the Aliases tab's ids go to Odoo's own id fields when one is added ([aliases.js](src/features/products/api/aliases.js) `addAlias` / `importAliasList`): Home Depot CA → Home Depot.ca SKU, Rona → Rona.ca SKU, Lowe's US → Lowe's SKU, BB&B / Overstock → BB&B.com SKU, Best Buy CA → Best Buy.ca SKU, the Amazon seller SKU → ALIAS AMZ CAN / USA and its ASIN → ASIN Amazon.ca / .com. An id the PIM lacks is left as Odoo has it, and one removed in the PIM stays in Odoo. The first load wrote 1,382 ids on 355 products: most of Home Depot CA / Rona / Lowe's were the same number stored as "1001585648.0"; 15 Lowe's and 22 Amazon CA seller SKUs really differed; **BB&B changed its form on all 229** (Odoo held the base number "37354536", the PIM's is "37354536-000-002" — the user's call over the advice to ask Pedro first; the values before are in that run's Activity Log entries) and 110 more were filled. Only in Odoo, never taken by the PIM: Wayfair SKU (332), Amazon.com ASIN / alias (240 / 259), most Amazon CA ASINs (232). Not mapped: Walmart (Odoo's Walmart SKU is empty and does not say which country), Home Depot US, Lowe's CA, Menards, Costco (no Odoo field).
  - **General Title** (`attributes.general_title_en`) → **Odoo Description**, on every product (user, 2026-11-11: "el Odoo description será el general title de nosotros"; 2026-11-12: "en General Title va en Odoo Description"). **"Odoo Description" is the product's `name`**: Odoo's product form shows that field under that label (`<field name="name" string="Odoo Description"/>`), and the API calls it Name — asked "General Title or Name?" the user chose the first, and corrected it within the hour, so always say "Odoo Description" to him. It also fills Odoo's own General Title field (`general_title`). Until 2026-11-12 the name followed only on products the PIM itself created in Odoo (table `odoo_created_products`, [20261111_odoo_created_products.sql](supabase/migrations/20261111_odoo_created_products.sql); "NO, no todavía" for the others on 2026-11-11).
  - **"Managed by PIM"** (2026-11-12): Odoo's products have a mark of that name (`x_managed_by_pim`, a Studio field of that day: "Checked when PIM manages this product. Locks the PIM fields" — on a marked product Odoo's form locks Odoo Description, Model Name, UPC and Brand and says they are edited in the PIM). At the user's word ("márcalos todos los que están en PIM") it was set on the 367 PIM products Odoo has active; the API still writes on a marked product. Until 2026-11-22 `odoo-push` never set it — not on the products it created either (user, 2026-11-12: "No, solo los que hacen match con los que hay en PIM"). **Since 2026-11-22 every product the PIM is connected to gets it** (user: "los productos que estén conectados deben activar el botón de Managed by PIM y llenar los campos") — see "Every hour the PIM goes over every product" below. Not marked, because they are not connected: A-931, A-933, the three drains Odoo spells with a dash and the six kits archived there.
  - **Brand, Category, Color, Material, Finish** (user, 2026-11-12: "otra cosa que PIM le enviará a Odoo"; `odoo-push` v7):
    - **Brand** (`brand`) → Brand (`product_brand_id`, Odoo's own brands: a brand Odoo lacks is held, `brand_unknown`). **Category** → Product Category (`categ_id`) by `odooCategory` in the function — the PIM's category, an accessory by its kind — which gives all 367 the category Odoo has; it is sent when the Category or the Product Type changes (a category Odoo lacks: `category_unknown`). In Odoo the category also sets accounts and routes.
    - **Color** (`color`) → Color, **Material** (`material`) → Material, **Finish Type** (`attributes.finish_type`) → Finish. Odoo's Finish holds the TYPE of finish (Matte, Brushed, Glossy…), so it takes the PIM's Finish Type, not its Finish ("Brushed Stainless Steel"): user, "Finish Type". The three are lists of their own in Odoo (`product.color` / `.material` / `.finish`, name + code) and a product can hold several: **the product takes the PIM's word and only that one, and a word Odoo's list lacks is added to it** (user: "Tal cual el PIM" — Gray next to Odoo's Grey, Granite Composite next to its Composite Granite; the code is the word in lower case with underscores; the old entries stay unused for Pedro to archive). A word is matched whatever its capitals.
    - **First load, the same day** (user: "Pues sí, envíalo por favor"): on the 367 products both have, Brand and Category were the same on all; sent to 331 products — Odoo's General Title field 241 (11 were empty), Color 204, Material 53, Finish 130 — with 14 words added to Odoo's lists (colors Gray, Brown, Mixed, Black and Gold, Not Applicable, Black and Silver; materials Granite Composite, Wood fiber composite, Brass and H-02's three-part text; finishes Honey-toned, Dura-Tek, Not Applicable, Braided); then Odoo Description on 365 (every name differed: "Stainless Steel Colander" → "16" Stainless Steel Over the Sink Colander"). A-931 and A-933 have no General Title. Odoo's values before are in those runs' Activity Log entries. A comparison after each: nothing left to send.
    - A run about some fields no longer rewrites the reason of what was already waiting for others (v8: the 26 HS codes had lost theirs and Settings grouped them under "Odoo gave an error").
  - **A new product** — "Create it in Odoo too" on the New Product dialog (new and clone) and on the import preview, ticked by default and shown only while the switch is on (`create: true`). Created with what all 367 PIM products in Odoo share (Goods, tracked in inventory, Units, no company) plus category (`odooCategory` in the function: the PIM's category, the accessories by kind — it reproduces the category of all 367), brand, Model Name, General Title (also the Name; else the Model Name, else the SKU), UPC and shipping box. Route, bill of materials, vendor, cost, taxes and reordering stay with operations. Never a second copy: a SKU Odoo has (active or archived) is not created, nor one it has under another spelling (D-701H-2 for D-701H2) unless that spelling is a PIM product of its own (A-906 / A906). **No product has been created for real yet** (only dry runs): the first tick is the first run. Products born in Odoo (106 since June that the PIM lacks, by Andre / Roberto / Pedro) are never brought into the PIM.
  - **One way only: PIM → Odoo, never Odoo → PIM** (user, 2026-11-11: "el flujo es solo PIM to Odoo, nunca Odoo to PIM"). Product data is never copied from Odoo into the PIM — not as a sync, not as a one-off load, not even where Odoo's value looks right: report the difference, the user corrects the PIM, and it travels. Odoo is read to compare, to see whether a SKU exists there, and for the stock (`product_inventory`: inventory, not product data).
  - **The switch is `app_settings.odoo_push.enabled` (Settings → Odoo)**, built off and **turned ON by the user on 2026-11-11**: it is the user's — never change it for a test or a load without reading it first and leaving it as it was (that day a load "restored" it to off while the user had just turned it on; put back within a minute). While it is off nothing is sent; `dryRun: true` compares and writes nothing whatever the switch says.
  - The product page's Save and the spreadsheet import call `sendToOdoo(skus, { fields })` ([odooPush.js](src/features/odoo/api/odooPush.js)) with the fields that save changed (`changedOdooFields(before, after)`) — beside the save, with a progress card ("Odoo") saying what Odoo took or why not. The save never waits for it and never fails for it. The other two writers of those fields send too, from the names of what they wrote (`odooFieldsOf`): the catalog's bulk edit (`bulkUpdateProducts` — Country of Origin) and "apply to variants" (`PropagateVariantsDialog`), found missing on 2026-11-11 when the user asked whether everything was automatic. A change made outside the app (a script, SQL) sends nothing. A new field that travels goes in `changedOdooFields`, `odooFieldsOf`, the function's `plan()` and this list.
  - The edge function `odoo-push` (`{ skus (≤ 100), fields?, create?, dryRun? }`, a signed-in admin / editor or the service role — the cron's x-cron-secret only for `retry` —, `verify_jwt` off since v4, self-contained; `fields` left out = `["upc"]`, for pages opened before the box was added) reads the values from the database itself. It never writes to an archived product, never empties a field (what the PIM lacks is left as Odoo has it: `no_upc` / `no_box` / `no_weight` and the other `no_<field>`, said on the card, not failures), sends only a valid UPC and a whole box (`incomplete_box`), and holds back a UPC another active Odoo product has (`taken`), a SKU Odoo lacks (exact spelling, then ignoring case, the dash kept) or has twice. What it writes it reads back: only what Odoo kept counts as sent. One Activity Log entry per run, target `odoo`, with Odoo's values before (Odoo keeps no history of these fields).
  - **What waits for Odoo is tried again every hour** (user, 2026-11-11: "Reintento cada hora … SI"): a send held because the product is not in Odoo yet, is archived there, has two records, its UPC is on another product, its HS code is not on Odoo's list or Odoo gave an error is noted in `odoo_pending` (sku, fields, reason — [20261111_odoo_pending.sql](supabase/migrations/20261111_odoo_pending.sql)), and the cron `odoo-push-retry-hourly` (:55, `{ retry: true }`) sends it when it can and drops the row. Settings → Odoo lists them ("Waiting for Odoo"). What the PIM itself lacks never waits there: saving it sends it. 37 on 2026-11-11: the 26 HS codes, the 6 kits archived in Odoo, the 3 SKUs Odoo spells with a dash, A-931 / A-933.
  - **Every hour the PIM goes over every product, on top of the saves** (user, 2026-11-22, asked how often each side travels: "lo de PIM a Odoo debería ser cada hora también, los productos que estén conectados deben activar el botón de Managed by PIM y llenar los campos"). Until that day only a save sent, and a product nobody edited was not touched.
    - **The function's `sync` mode** (`{ sync: true, offset }`, the cron's x-cron-secret, the service role or an admin / editor): the PIM's products, 100 from `offset` by SKU, each compared with Odoo on everything that travels but the picture (`SYNC_FIELDS`: two pictures cannot be compared cheaply — the main image still goes only when it changes), and what differs is written, by the same rules as a save (nothing emptied, a whole box, a valid UPC that no other Odoo product holds, the HS list…). So a product made in Odoo by hand after the PIM had it gets its fields within the hour, and a value somebody changed in Odoo goes back to the PIM's. It never creates a product. It says nothing in the Activity Log unless it changed something ("(Hourly sync) …", with Odoo's values before).
    - **Cron `odoo-push-sync-hourly`** (`5-14 * * * *`, [20261122_odoo_sync_and_schedules.sql](supabase/migrations/20261122_odoo_sync_and_schedules.sql)): one call a minute from :05 to :14, each asking for the page of its minute (:05 the first 100, :06 the next…), so up to 1,000 products, one page at a time — two pages at once could add the same word to one of Odoo's lists. A page past the last product answers at once. More than 1,000 products: widen the minutes. The hourly retry (:55) stays for what waits (it also carries a picture that was refused).
    - **"Managed by PIM" is ticked on every connected product**: one active Odoo product with the PIM's SKU (exact, then ignoring case, the dash kept). The function adds `mark` to whatever was asked — a save, the retry, the sync — and a product it creates in Odoo is born with it (the earlier "no" for created products was replaced by that day's word: a created product is connected). It is never unticked, and if Odoo no longer has the field (`x_managed_by_pim`, a Studio field) nothing is done about it and everything else still goes.
    - **Checked that day** (`odoo-push` v12): on the dev machine as dry runs with every write refused there — the sync over the 378 in 11 s with nothing to change (321 the same, 57 held for the known reasons), a product read as not marked ("Managed by PIM on (it had off)" on a save, on a create-only call and on the sync), an Odoo without the field (nothing about the mark, the rest as before), a create (the mark in its values) and the volume as before; the same dry run through the deployed function, page by page (same numbers); the cron's own command run once (200, `sync: true`). All 367 connected products already had the mark, so the first passes had nothing to write.
    - A load by hand is still possible (the function takes any list of SKUs; scratchpad recipe: dry run → keep the before-values → one product → the rest → dry run again).
  - **First loads, 2026-11-11.** UPC (user: "Actualiza esos tú, para que queden iguales a PIM"): 53 products through the function (45 stored in Odoo as "840994001412.0", 6 empty, A-801C, A-801N), B-124G and B-124N by the user's own saves. Shipping box and weight (user: "ENVÍA TODA ESA VAINA", after an Excel of the differences — only 178 of 367 had both the same; Odoo held round carton sizes like 16 × 10 × 6 and 30 × 14 × 7 where the PIM has the product's own box): 185 products, 181 boxes and 98 weights, the PIM's values as they are. Product weight, Model Name, HS Code and Country of Origin (user: "SI" to each): 124 products — 62 weights, 74 model names, 25 HS codes, 9 countries. After each, a dry run or a comparison straight from Odoo: everything the PIM can send is the same. Still apart: D-701H2 / D-702E2 / D-702H2 (Odoo spells D-701H-2…), the six S-828 / S-831 kits (archived in Odoo), A-931 / A-933 (not in Odoo, no UPC), and what the PIM lacks — A-920 (no box, no weight), A-802B / A-802C / A-802N and S-405T (two of the three sides).
- Exporters read yes/no certifications with `isYes` ([templateFiller.js](src/features/syndication/exports/templateFiller.js)): true, "Yes" or the mark's own name ("ADA Compliant", "cUPC Certified"); a stored "No" / "Not Certified" / "Does Not Apply" is a no. BB&B (features) and Home Depot USA (Certifications and Listings) counted ANY filled value as yes until 2026-10-06.
- The spreadsheet import ([buildImportRows.js](src/features/import/lib/buildImportRows.js)) treats the PTD's "Ask Technical Team" placeholder as empty (`EMPTY_VALUES`; the 253 already stored were cleared 2026-10-06) and maps Warranty Length "Limited Lifetime" to the dropdown's "Lifetime" (`VALUE_CANONICALS`).
- **The PIM's own Excel is sectioned** (user, 2026-10-15: "estiliza… seccionalo"). The Import page's blank template and the catalog's PIM export are the same layout ([sectionedXlsx.js](src/features/import/lib/sectionedXlsx.js), `templateLayout` in [importSchema.js](src/features/import/lib/importSchema.js)): row 1 a colored band per section (`SECTIONS`: Identity, Pricing CAD / USD / Wayfair Canada, Physical, Sink, Faucet, Accessory, Dimensions, Shipping, Master Carton, Trade & Warranty, Certifications, Content EN / FR, Retailer IDs), row 2 the headers, both frozen with the SKU column and filtered. The export is ONE .xlsx with a sheet per category (it was a CSV per category in a ZIP), the template one sheet; numbers are numbers, UPCs text. The export (selected products or the whole catalog) ends each sheet with an "Images (links)" section — Image 1 = the main image, then in their order, clickable (user, 2026-10-16); the import skips those columns (`IMAGE_LINK_RE`), images are uploaded on the product page.
  - Every category's vendor columns plus `ALWAYS` (prices and costs at Blue / Orange / Purple, Color, Finish Type, Manufacturer, the warranties, keywords, marketing copy, master carton, both HS codes…) and its `BY_KIND` extras, each column named by its field's label (typos fixed — "External Length", "Country of Origin" — the old names stay aliases) and ordered by `SECTION_FIELDS`. A vendor column no field reads ("Family #", "BM Dealer Cost") is left out.
  - **The bulk upload changes prices and costs** (user, 2026-10-15): `PRICE_DEFS`, type `money` ("$1,234.50", "1 234,50"). An empty cell never clears a value. A description whose words did not change keeps the PIM's HTML formatting (`importProducts`), so an export re-imported as is changes nothing — verified on all 378 products (only leading / trailing spaces get trimmed).
  - **Every hand-typed column checks its input** (user, 2026-10-15: "todo donde el usuario deba meter algo manual … drop down", against typos) — `excelValidationFor(header, kind, suggestions)` in importSchema.js:
    - a fixed list where the page has one (`dropdownFor`: fieldOptions.js, countries, brands, `CATEGORY_LABELS`, Yes / No for a yes / no field; Installation Type on sink sheets and Overall Shape on faucet sheets only, as on the page);
    - else the values the catalog already uses (`loadSuggestionLists` in [suggestionLists.js](src/features/import/api/suggestionLists.js): the page's open fields from [suggestFields.js](src/features/products/lib/suggestFields.js), every other text field of the import, and the SKUs of the type for Strainer Model / Compatible Deck Plate / Drain Assembly); a field nobody has filled yet has no list until it has values;
    - numbers (prices, sizes) must be numbers and a UPC 12 digits only (custom formula; its check digit is checked by the import preview and the database) — these REFUSE anything else (user: "solo número, no letras ni símbolos"), as does a list made only of numbers: the counts and standard sizes are lists in fieldOptions.js (`BOWL_COUNT_OPTIONS`, `HANDLE_COUNT_OPTIONS`, `HOLE_COUNT_OPTIONS`, `PIECE_COUNT_OPTIONS`, `DRAIN_HOLE_COUNT_OPTIONS`, `SPOUT_ROTATION_OPTIONS`, `SINK_RADIUS_OPTIONS`, `MAX_FLOW_RATE_OPTIONS` — the number only, the field is in GPM; Gauge), dropdowns on the product page too. A list of numbers holds numbers on "PIM Lists", so a typed 2 matches. S-202XTG's radius 83 mm is off its list (kept);
    - free text only for what is one product's own: SKU, Model Name, titles, descriptions, bullets, keywords, care / warranty texts, HS codes, URLs, retailer ids, and the "a; b" lists.
    - The lists sit on a hidden "PIM Lists" sheet behind `PIM_List_N` names (the import skips hidden sheets). Every check only WARNS, as the page keeps a stored value that is off its list.
    - The six values that were off their list on 2026-10-15 were fixed that day (16 products, audit rows): UPC Certified "Not certified (will be cUPC in 2025)" → "No" (S-831W*), Spray Type "3 Modes" → "Pull Out" (K-140*), Warranty "Limited Warranty" → "Limited" (H-03/06/07), Basin Split "60 / 40" → "60/40" (C236L), Manufacturer "Jase" → "Stylish International Inc." (K-153S); K-145's Spout Type "Foldable" joined the list.
  - The parser (`parseSpreadsheetFile(file, { scoreHeader, allSheets })`) reads every visible sheet and takes as the header row the best of the first ten rows by `scoreImportHeader`; rows show "Sheet · row". The promo files still read the first sheet's first row. Old CSV / PTD-style files import as before.
  - **The import page stays fast with any file** (user, 2026-11-11, after uploading a Full Report: "la página se puso super lenta" — 13,438 rows drawn at once, 26 s to open and 2.5 s to open one row):
    - **Only product sheets are read.** `isDataSheet: isProductSheet` — a sheet whose header lacks SKU, Brand or Category (the three every row needs) is another kind of table and is left out and named ("Sheets not read: …") when the workbook has a product sheet; a file where no sheet has them is read as before, so its rows say what is missing.
    - **The preview is paged** (25 rows, the shared Pagination) and its counters — rows / new / updates / unchanged / with errors — are the filters of the rows below (pressed again: every row). The columns the import does not read take one line ("79 columns not imported…", the rest behind "Show all"). The table scrolls inside its own `relative`, focusable box on a phone.
  - **An import writes only what changes** (user, 2026-11-11: a report uploaded back must update "aquellos datos nuevos"). [importChanges.js](src/features/import/lib/importChanges.js): `rowChanges(row, product)` compares each value of a row with the product's (`fetchProductsForImport` reads it whole) and `updatePatch` sends only the fields that differ — attributes merged into the product's own, a dimension group axis by axis, so an axis the file lacks is kept. A row that changes nothing is **Unchanged** and is not written (no update, no Activity Log, nothing to Odoo); the preview's row shows its changes ("MAP CAD (Blue): 787 → 788") and the button counts new + changed ("Nothing to import" when there are none).
    - Two values are the same when they read the same (`sameValue`): spaces around or doubled do not count, "65.0" = 65, Yes = true, a list is its items whatever separates them, a description is its words whatever its formatting (so one whose words did not change keeps its HTML), bullets are whole sentences. An empty cell never reaches the comparison: it clears nothing.
    - Checked that day with every write captured: the six reports and the PIM export of the whole catalog (17 sheets) uploaded as downloaded → 378 unchanged each, also after being opened and saved by Excel itself; a few edited cells → only those products and only those fields.
- **Full Report** (user, 2026-10-15: "un reporte completo en un solo excel con toda la información de todos los productos"): the Catalog header's "Full Report" button (every role — it only reads) downloads `Stylish_PIM_Full_Report_<date>.xlsx` from [pimReport.js](src/features/syndication/exports/pimReport.js), loaded on click (~8 s for 378 products).
  - **Summary**: totals, by category, by workflow status, the 25 most-missed completeness fields.
  - **All Products**: one row per product, by category then SKU. It has every import field in the PIM Excel's sections, then PIM Completeness (the Listing Health score and each gap's reason), Stock, Images & Documents, Websites & Marketplace IDs, Web Store Flags and "Other / Older Fields" (columns and attributes from old imports that the app no longer reads). A column nobody fills is left out.
  - **Media**: one row per file. **Marketplace IDs**: aliases, Amazon links and Wix links, one row per ID. **Notes**.
  - **The button opens the list of reports** (user, 2026-11-11: "que permita elegir si full report de precios, de imágenes, de atributos y así pues, y lógicamente el full completo"; `ReportMenu` in Catalog.jsx, [pimReportKinds.js](src/features/syndication/exports/pimReportKinds.js) — plain data, so the menu needs no report code): **Complete** (the workbook above), **Prices**, **Attributes**, **Content**, **Images & documents**, **Marketplace IDs**. A report by subject is the same product sheet with that subject's sections (`sections`), after the four columns every row starts with (SKU, Model Name, Brand, Category), plus its own extra sheet (Media / Marketplace IDs) and short Notes; it keeps every field of its sections, filled or not, so an empty price can be typed in (the complete one still leaves out a column nobody fills) and reads only the tables it shows. Files: `Stylish_PIM_<Prices|Attributes|…>_Report_<date>.xlsx`.
  - **Every report can be uploaded back in Import Products** (user, the same day: "el full report debería servir para importar también … y que actualice aquellos datos nuevos"). The PIM's own fields keep the import's column names; every other column (completeness, stock, counts, links, IDs, web store flags, older fields) is read-only because the import does not know its name — `readOnlyHeader` tags any that it would (an older attribute called `color` was read as the Color column: untouched, the report would have rewritten the Color of 11 products and one Product Care text). The image links keep "Image N", which the import knows and skips. Images, documents, aliases and Amazon / Wix IDs are not changed from a file (the product page does that): the Images report is to read.
  - It uses the same writer as the PIM Excel: `buildSectionedWorkbook` also takes per-column `formats` (money / percent / link, real hyperlinks) and report pages (`blocks`: stacked tables, no gridlines).
  - A new import field shows up in the report by itself; a new table the PIM keeps per product goes in `loadReportData`.
- **The obsolete mark** (user, 2026-10-16: "marcar un producto obsoleto … que no sea el status"; "solo el botón obsolete or not"): `products.is_obsolete`, only obsolete or not ([20261016_products_obsolete.sql](supabase/migrations/20261016_products_obsolete.sql); a first version's date / replacement / note were dropped the same day — when and who is in product_history and the Activity Log). A mark of its own, apart from `workflow_status`: it changes NO listing, channel push, marketplace file, promotion price, promotion automation, price alignment or exclusion — a product can stay Ready to Sell while its last units sell. Never make an exporter, a push or a promo generator read it.
  - The `products_obsolete_mark` trigger turns a null into false (a bulk import's empty cell in a batch insert).
  - Shown as the `ObsoleteBadge` pill (the theme's inverse colors) in the product page header, under the model name in the catalog row (`compact`), in the top search's results and Recent list (read fresh, `obsoleteAmong`) and in a promotion's table; a corner ribbon "OBSOLETE" across the product page photo; the catalog's "Obsolete" filter (Yes / No, `?obsolete=`). Obsolete products are always listed, never hidden by default.
  - Set on the product page (Overview → Identification: the Obsolete switch), in bulk (selection bar → Obsolete ▾, `setProductsObsolete`, one Activity Log entry; a partial failure says how many were done and a rerun skips them) and from the PIM's Excel (Status & Dates: Obsolete Yes / No, an `optional` def so an empty cell never counts as missing). Every spreadsheet import now writes one Activity Log entry (`logImport` in importProducts.js: created / updated, and the marks it set or removed).
  - Per variant (VARIANT_DISTINGUISHING: offered to the family after a save, unchecked); a clone is never obsolete (CLONE_EXCLUDE).
  - PIM completeness leaves obsolete products out like archived ones: the Listing Health panel (it says "N obsolete products left out"), `takeSnapshot`, the daily `health-refresh` snapshot and the Full Report's summary (Totals "Obsolete products", an Obsolete column by category); All Products keeps every product's own score. Analytics: a category with nothing left to score drops out instead of freezing, a product that left the score is listed apart ("No longer scored") and closes no gap.
  - Promotions warn, never block: the new-promotion form lists the obsolete SKUs, and a promotion's table has an "N obsolete" chip that filters them.
  - `formatDate` reads a bare `YYYY-MM-DD` as that local day (a DATE column used to show the day before in Eastern time); the import drops an impossible ISO day (2026-02-30) like any unreadable date instead of failing the batch.
- **Master carton** (user, 2026-10-15): `attributes.master_carton_quantity` (units per carton), `master_carton_dimensions_in` {length, width, height} and `master_carton_weight_lb`, shown in the product's Shipping section under "Master Carton — pallet shipments only". They apply ONLY when a product ships on a pallet (LTL), never to parcel shipping — the box a parcel ships in stays `shipping_dimensions_in` / `shipping_weight_lb`. For sinks and porcelain the user's list gives the full pallet (48 × 40 in). Per variant (never propagated); the import maps "Quantity / Master Carton" and "Master Carton Length / Width / Height / Weight". No exporter reads them yet. Loaded the same day from the user's list for 304 products (23 of its SKUs are not in the PIM: A-03, A-901RD, A904, B-104NC, B-111C/G, B-121C, B-122NC, H-01/04/05, H-450C, K-107B/N, K-137N, K-143P, S-315XG, S-315W, S-316W, S-504XG, S-601WN, S-601WNK, S-639WK).
- **The product page's in / cm switch is a unit SYSTEM** (user, 2026-10-15): "in" shows inches and pounds, "cm" centimetres and kilos ([units.js](src/features/products/lib/units.js) `weightUnitFor`, `toDisplayWeight`; weight fields take `weight={unit}`). Display only: lengths stay stored in inches and weights in pounds, and editing always shows those.
- `workflow_status` values are centralized in [src/features/products/lib/workflowStatus.js](src/features/products/lib/workflowStatus.js) — add new statuses there only.
- **Kitchen Faucet Combo and Cold Water Tap are categories** (user, 2026-10-08, [20261008_kitchen_faucet_kinds.sql](supabase/migrations/20261008_kitchen_faucet_kinds.sql)): `kitchen_faucet_combo` (the faucet + tap / soap dispenser combos) and `cold_water_tap` (K-142, K-147; the K-145 Asti are **pot fillers** — moved to `pot_filler` on 2026-10-30, user: "esos son pot filler"). They are kitchen faucets for every rule, export and template — [_shared/categories.js](supabase/functions/_shared/categories.js) (re-exported by `src/features/products/lib/categories.js`): `isFaucetCategory`, `isKitchenFaucetCategory`, and `baseCategory()` = `kitchen_faucet` for them, which every channel mapping switches on and `templateAppliesTo` accepts (a template tagged Kitchen Faucet fits them). Listing Health checks them as kitchen faucets. A new kitchen faucet kind goes in that file, never as a `=== 'kitchen_faucet'` check.
  - The same day the user's file moved the 19 colanders, drying racks / mats, dish racks and workstation kits to **Accessory** and S-320T to **Laundry Sink**. The channels still know those accessories as Colanders & Drying Racks: `channelCategory(product)` (same file) reads `colander_drying_rack` for an Accessory whose name says colander / drying rack / drying mat / dish rack / dish organizer / sink basket / accessories kit (a "Cutting Board with Colander" stays a board), and every channel switch, `templateMatchesProduct`, the Wayfair class and attribute rules and the PIM export use it — their templates, classes and mappings did not change. Laundry sinks take the templates scoped to Kitchen Sink (`TEMPLATE_FALLBACK` in templates.js; no marketplace template lists laundry sinks), so S-825H/L/N and C122 got them too.
- **Color and Finish Type** split the Finish (user, 2026-10-08): Color = the `products.color` column (Silver, Black, Black and Gold…; Walmart's color reads it, else the Finish), Finish Type = `attributes.finish_type` (Brushed, Matte, Glossy, Polished, Honey-toned, Dura-Tek). Both are dropdowns in Specifications → Physical Properties ([fieldOptions.js](src/features/products/lib/fieldOptions.js) `COLOR_OPTIONS` / `FINISH_TYPE_OPTIONS`), per variant (never propagated). The Finish stays as it is. All 363 products got them from the user's "PIM - Datos de productos" file that day, with its newer model names and materials ("Granite Composite", "Stainless Steel") and the UPC / HS Code / Country it filled in — the user's own file, loaded at the user's request. On 2026-10-15 the user's "PIM - Missing Items - completed" file filled the 13 accessories without them (DA-01/04, H-02/03/06/07/24, ST-01/07/08, ST03/04/05): Color, Finish Type ("Braided" and "Not Applicable" joined both lists), HS Code where empty, Model name (H-03 now "Overflow Ring", ST-07 / ST03 / ST05 "Basket Strainer") and H-24's Material "Stainless Steel"; their UPC, Category and Country already matched, and the Finish was left as it is.
- The product page edits with dropdowns wherever the value comes from a list (user request 2026-10-06, against typos):
  - Fixed lists live in [fieldOptions.js](src/features/products/lib/fieldOptions.js) (`type="select" options`): Manufacturer, Warranty, Craftsmanship, Sink Shape, Gauge, Bowl Configuration, Basin Split, Drain Location, Handle Style, Spray Type / Activation, every Yes/No and certification field.
  - Open fields (Series, Product Type, Finish, Mounting, Cartridge, Max Flow Rate, Connection Size, Safety Reg. #…) offer the catalog's own values plus "Other…" (`suggest`; the keys are `SUGGEST_COLUMNS` / `SUGGEST_ATTRS` / `SUGGEST_FROM` in [suggestFields.js](src/features/products/lib/suggestFields.js), read by ProductDetail's `loadSuggestions` and the PIM's Excel). The bathroom sink's Drain Location takes the fixed Drain Location list (2026-10-15).
  - Strainer Model and the Compatible Deck Plate # / Drain Assembly # pickers offer the catalog's SKUs of that product type, of the product's own brand (`skusOfType`; ST-03 Stylish, ST03 Azuni).
  - A stored value that is off its list shows as the first option and is kept. A new value goes on the list, not typed into one product.
  - UPC, HS Code, numbers, titles and long texts (care instructions) stay free text.
- SKUs with and without dashes are DIFFERENT brands (e.g. `A-906` vs `A906`), not duplicates. Never merge/delete on that assumption without asking.
- **A family number a product has is never changed by an import or a new product** (2026-11-11). [variantFamilies.js](src/features/products/api/variantFamilies.js) `syncVariantFamilies` (after every import and product creation; `planFamilySync` is its pure plan) only gives a number to products that have NONE: they join their base model's family (S-611X → S-611's) when the base has exactly one, two or more products of a base nobody numbered get a new one, and a product alone or of a base split into several families stays without. Until that day it regrouped every base an import touched from scratch: any file naming K-131N would have merged the family the user made by hand on 2026-08-21 for K-131 G / N / P / S (79) into K-131 CR / GR / NR / SR's (32) and taken B-111N's (66) away, reported only as "5 variant family links re-synced" — found before it happened, with the import's writes captured.
- Mutations log to the audit trail via `logActivity` ([src/features/activity/api/activityLog.js](src/features/activity/api/activityLog.js)).
- In PIM ↔ marketplace discrepancies, the PIM is the source of truth: fix diffs by pushing from the PIM, not by copying channel data in.
- Promotion prices (user rules 2026-09-30, replacing the 2026-09-28 "levels only" rule): **an uploaded file's prices ARE the promotion** ("no el orange obligatorio" — A-02: file 79, not the Orange 77), and **the USA and the Canada promotions are independent lists** (each market's list is the SKUs of its own file). **Monthly promotions by market (user rule 2026-10-02, [20261002_promotion_prices_us_monthly_orange.sql](supabase/migrations/20261002_promotion_prices_us_monthly_orange.sql)):** Canada = what the file says over the Orange price (a product the file gives no price takes Orange); USA = the ORANGE price over the file (a file price only fills a product without an Orange price), USA costs the same way. Flash deals and special events keep the file-first rule for now ("después vemos") — confirmed for peak season events on 2026-11-13, when their form got the price files ("que el sistema respete los precios que se suban en el template", below). `promotion_prices` stores the given values and the membership — `in_ca` / `in_us`, `set_price_cad` / `set_price_usd`, `set_costs` — and the trigger ([20260930_promotion_prices_file_first.sql](supabase/migrations/20260930_promotion_prices_file_first.sql)) keeps the columns every reader uses: `promo_price_cad` / `_usd` = the given price, else the level's, **only for that market's members (NULL otherwise)**; `promo_costs` = level costs overlaid with the given ones, each market's slugs only for its members. Only a SKU-only list (flash deals, special events, a monthly list pasted without prices) takes everything from the level: monthly promotions and **flash deals at Orange**, **special events at Purple** (`KIND_LEVEL` / `promotionLevel` in [promotions.js](src/features/pricing/api/promotions.js), in step with `promotion_level_of()` in SQL); a level change in Pricing refreshes those rows, never a file's price. `promotionMembersFor(promotion, channelKey)` returns only the channel's market list (`channelMarket`, `onMarketList`); importing a market file into a promotion (`addFileToPromotion(promo, rows, market)`) REPLACES that market's list (SKUs not in the file leave it; a row on neither list is removed). Generators use the promotion's row first and the level only to fill a gap; the one exception: **Bed Bath & Beyond and Overstock take flash deals at Purple** (`levelByKind` in [promoChannels.js](src/features/pricing/lib/promoChannels.js)). Never hardcode `kind === 'monthly' ? 'orange' : 'purple'`. The per-market lists and file prices of promotions loaded before 2026-09-30 were overwritten on 2026-09-28 — their market files must be imported again. A promotion's dates follow each market's calendar (USA the 1st to month end, Canada the first Thursday to the day before the next) UNLESS it has custom dates, and **custom dates are per country** (user rule 2026-09-30 — October: Canada Oct 1–21, USA Oct 1–31): a market's own pair (`ca_starts_on` / `ca_ends_on`, `us_starts_on` / `us_ends_on`, [20260930_promotion_market_dates.sql](supabase/migrations/20260930_promotion_market_dates.sql)) rules that market, else the promotion-wide pair (`starts_on` / `ends_on` — flash deals and special events, the portal's dates), else the calendar. They rule every channel of that market — automations, API sends and files (user rule 2026-09-29: what Jessica sets in the dates is what happens). The card edits a monthly promotion's dates per market (`updatePromotionMarketDates`, which writes both markets' pairs and keeps Canada's days on the wide pair for readers that only know it). Everything goes through `promoWindow()` (src/features/pricing/lib/promoCalendar.js and its Deno twin in functions/_shared) and selects the date columns (`PROMO_DATE_COLUMNS`); never compute a window elsewhere or read `starts_on` / `ends_on` directly.
- **Pricing's tabs** (user, 2026-11-09; drawn on a rule like Listing Health's channels, the pill track was the page's own gray and the tabs looked adrift): **Calendar** (2026-11-16, the tab the page opens on — next bullet), Monthly Promotions, Flash Deals, **MK Events** (`kind = 'mk_event'`: a copy of the flash deals, Orange level, with Bed Bath & Beyond and Overstock at Purple — `levelByKind` in promoChannels.js and pageStores.js), **Peak Season Events** (the special events renamed, Purple; the key stays `special`; a note on the tab names Way Day, Prime Day, Black Friday, Boxing Day), **Coupons**, Price Alignment. A new deal kind goes in `KIND_LEVEL`, `PROMOTION_KINDS`, `KIND_NOUN`, `promotions_kind_check`, `DEAL_KINDS` (expectedPrices.js) and the audit's `kind=in.(…)`. In the EOS ScoreCard an MK event counts with the flash deals (`flash_count`, `flash_skus`, `deal_count`), so moving a deal between those two tabs changes no number.
- **Pricing's Calendar** (user, 2026-11-16, showing the team's Asana board "Promotions + Events Calendar Q4 2026": "¿podríamos replicar algo así para las promociones? ya que son muchísimas" — "Sí, constrúyelo así, hazlo bien bonito"). The first tab of Pricing and the one it opens on ([PromoCalendar](src/features/pricing/components/PromoCalendar.jsx), [promoCalendarItems.js](src/features/pricing/lib/promoCalendarItems.js)). **Nothing is typed for it**: it draws what the other tabs hold — monthly promotions, flash deals, MK events, peak season events and coupons — by their own days (`promoWindow`, so a monthly promotion has its days per country; one ended early stops the day it was ended, one ended before it began is not drawn). It does not hold the follow-up tasks the Asana board also has. The tab takes the page's whole width (`max-w-none`; the other tabs keep `max-w-5xl`, on the same left edge), and its month, view and filters are kept by the page while another tab is visited.
  - **Two views, one switch.** *Month* (the default, the one the team already used): a week per row, Sunday to Saturday; each promotion shows on the day it STARTS (a filled chip with its kind's color on the spine) and on the day it ENDS (a dashed chip with a flag) — never as a bar across the days: on Nov 13–15 fifteen deals ran at once and a deal lasts twelve days on average. Three chips at most, then "N more". At the foot of each day a strip says what is RUNNING: as tall as the day is busy against the month's busiest, a segment per kind; the day's count opens its list (the shared Dialog: Starting, Ending, Also running). *Timeline*: a row per marketplace under its country, a bar from the first day to the last, laid in lanes where two overlap; a bar that goes on before or after the month ends square on that side, its name follows the sideways scroll, and each country's band is also the ruler of days. A monthly promotion is on every marketplace of the country but the Stylish stores; a deal nobody gave a marketplace sits on "No marketplace yet".
  - **Filters:** the legend's five kinds are switches with the month's count of each, Country (All / Canada / USA) and Marketplace. **A promotion that is over is faint** (user, the same day: "más tenues o con transparencia, que se note la diferencia entre las activas y las no activas"): no fill, a hairline around it, lighter text in the regular weight and its kind's color dimmed on the spine (`OVER` / `OVER_END`, `spineOver` / `iconOver` / `dotOver` in `LOOK`) — in the month's chips, the timeline's bars and a day's list; the strip of a day that is past is dimmed too. The text is a lighter color (`text-on-surface-variant/80`, 5:1), never the whole chip made see-through: an `opacity` on a faint chip's country tag fell under 4.5:1 and was taken off. One that is running or still to come keeps its full color; today is the brand red.
  - **A click opens the promotion's own card on its tab** (`openFromCalendar` in PricingPage: the tab of its kind and `openDeal`; a coupon opens on Coupons, `initialOpenId` on CouponsTab) — from a chip, a bar or a row of a day's list.
  - **A SKU can be looked for** (user, 2026-11-19: "un filtro para buscar en qué promociones un SKU fue ingresado … no sé cómo identificarlos entre tantas promociones"). "Find a SKU…" on the title's row ([PromoSkuFinder](src/features/pricing/components/PromoSkuFinder.jsx)) is the top bar's own product search — by SKU, name or a marketplace's id (`useProductSearch`) — as a combobox; picking a product is the filter (`state.sku` = `{ sku, name }`, kept by the page like the month and the other filters). The calendar is then made only of the promotions and coupons whose list holds that product, in both views and in every month, under the other filters (`loadSkuPromotions` in [promoSku.js](src/features/pricing/api/promoSku.js): its rows of `promotion_prices` and `coupon_products`; `itemsOfSku` in promoCalendarItems.js). Nothing is written.

Chips, not rows: the list sits over the calendar and a product can be in nine at once. A product switched off for a marketplace its running or coming promotions go to gets one line ("… is switched off for Walmart USA: no promotion reaches it there", `switchedOffFor`) — the calendar still shows the promotion, since the product is on its list. No prices here: a marketplace can take another level than the row's (Bed Bath & Beyond, the Stylish stores); the card has them.

The list of products is drawn outside the card (the card is a container, which holds a fixed box inside itself, and clips) and inside `<main>`, not `<body>`, where axe found it outside every landmark. CSS anchor positioning was looked at with the `modern-web-guidance` skill and left: Firefox does not have it.

A new kind takes its three tokens there, its entry in `CALENDAR_KINDS` and its classes in `LOOK` (written out in full: Tailwind makes only the classes it can read).
  - **On a phone** the month keeps its grid with a dot per start (filled) and per end (a ring) and the day's list one tap away; the timeline's name column is narrow, it scrolls sideways inside its own focusable box and opens with today in view.
  - **A pass with the `modern-web-guidance` skill** (user, the same day: "aplícale ese skill … a ver si sugiere corregir algo"; the skill is the user's, in `.claude/skills/modern-web-guidance`). Applied: **the headers stick** — the month's weekday row and, in the timeline, each country's band with its days stay at the top of the page while their rows go by (the card is `overflow-clip`, never `overflow-hidden`, which would make it a scroll box and stop them; the month's table is `border-separate`; the timeline is a scroll box only where the month does not fit — `fits`, a ResizeObserver — and takes `tabIndex` only then); **the card answers to its own width** (`@container` on the card, `@2xl:` / `@5xl:` in the place of `md:` / `xl:`); **a day's list shows every name whole** (it is the way to the full text by keyboard and on a phone, where a chip's `title` cannot be reached); a bar's focus ring is drawn inside it. Looked at and left: the browser's customizable select (it would be the one select of the app that looks different, and not every browser has it), Temporal for the days (it needs a polyfill on Safari; the days here are plain strings read at noon UTC, which do not drift), hints that a box scrolls sideways (the scrollbar is there), and two things that belong to the whole app, not to this tab — the shared Dialog as a native `<dialog>`, and `<meta name="color-scheme">` in index.html.
