- **A promotion's name can be changed on its card** (user, 2026-11-11: "habilita la opción de cambiar nombre de promociones, tanto monthly como todas las demás"): the Name row (`PromoName` in PricingPage.jsx, `renamePromotion` in promotions.js) — every kind and every status, ended ones too, for editors. The name is a label: files, sends and automations go by the promotion's id, dates and lists, so a rename changes nothing else; the Activity Log keeps the name each entry was written with, and the rename's own entry carries both. An empty name is refused. Coupons have no rename yet.
- **A peak season event takes a price file per country, like the monthly promotion, and its prices are the event's** (user, 2026-11-13: "Colocar lo de subir template al igual que en Monthly dentro de Peak Season Events y que el sistema respete los precios que se suban en el template"). Only that kind: flash deals and MK events keep their one SKU box.
  - **New peak season event → Source: Upload file / Paste list** (`takesFiles` / `fileMode` in `NewPromotionForm`). It opens on Paste list, the SKU box it always had; Upload file shows the monthly form's two boxes — Canada file, USA file, each with Download template (the same templates: SKU, PROMO MAP, the promo costs) — and the portals are still picked. Either file alone creates the event. An event for the Stylish stores has no Source: it takes its own discount on the MSRP (`createPromotionFromFile` refuses it too).
  - **The file decides, on every marketplace of that country.** `createPromotionFromFile({ …, kind: 'special', marketplaces })` writes each row with its country's list (`in_ca` / `in_us`), the price the file gives (`set_price_cad` / `set_price_usd`) and its costs (`set_costs`); the trigger `promotion_prices_from_level()` takes the file first for every kind but monthly — `coalesce(set_price, the Purple level)`, costs the level's overlaid with the file's — so what a row leaves empty comes from Purple, and a later change of the Purple level in Pricing moves only the prices that came from it. Every reader already takes the promotion's row first: the marketplace files (Walmart Canada's deal file, Lowe's, Rona, Home Depot, Amazon, Menards, Wayfair…), Price Alignment's expected price, and `sinksdirect-deals`, which puts that price on the Sinks Direct stores.
  - **Checks, as on the monthly form.** A file is taken whole or not at all (`parsePromoFile`, `checkMarketList` with the event's kind: the other country's file, an unreadable price, a SKU twice, a promo price above the regular MAP, a promo cost not under the promo price). Warnings, never a block: SKUs the PIM lacks, no price in the file and none on Purple, a price that lowers nothing or goes more than 40 % under, rows left out, stock and obsolete products — and what is about the portals (`checkDealList` with `lists`, each portal on its own country's list; `dealWarnings(…, { fromFile })`): switched off for a portal, another deal on those days, **a portal picked whose country has no file** ("No USA file: Lowe's USA gets no product…") and a file whose country has no portal.
  - **On the card** nothing was missing: Import list (every kind) uploads or replaces a country's file or list after a review of what joins, stays and leaves, and its prices are kept the same way. **Edit SKUs edits the list of the tab shown whenever the two countries hold different products** (`listPerMarket`: a monthly promotion, or an event made from files) — the import dialog's edit, so the other country's list and the file's prices are not touched; a deal with one list for both keeps its inline editor.
  - **Checked 2026-11-13:** the trigger on a peak season event's rows inside an undone transaction (a Canada-only row at the file's 111.11 with its file cost over Purple's and no USA price; a row in both files at Purple in CAD and the file's price in USD; the Purple level lowered by 1: the file's price stayed, the level's followed); the form in a browser with a stand-in sign-in, live reads and every write captured — the other country's file and a price above the MAP refused, a Canada file of four rows read, the missing USA file and the SKU the PIM lacks said, and Create sending the event (kind, days, portals) with its three rows at the file's prices and costs, Canada only, and one Activity Log entry; a flash deal without Source, a monthly promotion opening on its files, a Stylish event without Source — at 1440 / 390 in both themes, axe's only finding the top bar's account button, no sideways scroll. **Not run for real:** no event was created (the writes were answered by the test).
- **A monthly promotion's pasted list is per country** (user, 2026-11-11: Jessica's December list — "no la dejaba escoger el país", and "en Canadá agarró 71 productos pero en USA solo 40"). The one box put the same SKUs on both lists, and 31 of the 71 had no Orange price in USD, which nothing said.

  - **The card's "Import list"** (it was "Import file") also takes a pasted list for the market chosen: `marketListRows` → `addFileToPromotion(…, { source: 'list' })`. It IS that market's list — the SKUs not pasted leave it, a product already on it keeps the price its file gave, the other market is not touched. A file or a list with no SKU of the PIM is refused instead of emptying the market's list.
  - **"Edit SKUs" is on every kind** (user, 2026-11-12: "agregar o quitar SKUs … tanto en monthly como en todos"; not on an ended promotion). On a deal it edits the list in the card, as before. On a monthly promotion it opens that same import dialog for the country of the tab shown (`edit` on `ImportPromoDialog`: no market to pick, no file), with the country's SKUs written out to add or delete lines; "Review the changes" shows what joins, stays and leaves before "Replace the <country> list" — the pasted list's own path, so a product that stays keeps its price and what follows a list change (Best Buy, Walmart Canada, the Sinks Direct stores) follows this one too.

The new-deal form's warning about products already in the monthly promotion (rule of 2026-09-22, when a deal and the monthly promotion were both at Orange) named every such product, also under an event at Purple, which lowers them. `monthlyOverlap` now gives each product's country and monthly price, and the form names a product only when the deal's price on a portal picked is not under it (same price or higher); a Stylish-store event is never named (those stores take no monthly promotion).
  - **Before creating**, the form warns (never blocks) about the SKUs the PIM does not have and, per market, the products that will have no promotion price — none in the file and none on the level in that currency (`priceCheck`). On 2026-11-11 143 of the 371 products with a USD MAP had no Orange USD price (4 had no Orange CAD). Stock is checked per market on that market's own list. What the form reported stays on the page once it closes (`notice`).
  - **On the card** a market's tab counts its list, the rows its table shows (it counted the rows with a price: a table of 71 under "USA 40"), and the "N without a promo price" chip filters the products no channel of that market can take until they get their price on the level.
- **The promotion tables sort by their headers** (user, 2026-11-12: "al hacerle click al header … mayor a menor o menor a mayor … en todas las pantallas que salga eso"). A click on a column's header sorts by it — numbers high to low, text A to Z —, a second click turns it round, a third gives the list's own order back; a row with nothing in that column (no price, stock not tracked) goes last either way, and the order works on the rows the chips left. [tableSort.js](src/features/pricing/lib/tableSort.js) (`nextSort`, `sortRows`, `ariaSort`) and [SortButton](src/features/pricing/components/SortButton.jsx), the catalog's own header button and arrows; the `<th>` carries `aria-sort`.
  - **A promotion's product table** (`SortTh` in PricingPage.jsx; every kind, the Stylish event's too): SKU, Promo MAP / MSRP, Regular MAP / MSRP, Stock, Sold 90 d and each cost column. **The History table** of Flash Deals, MK Events, Peak Season Events and Coupons (`HISTORY_SORTS` in DealsOverview.jsx, the one table all four tabs draw): name, marketplaces, dates, days, SKUs, status. The order is the card's own and is not kept when it closes.
  - The arrows made the product table 48 px wider than its box (the last column cut, the SKU on two lines): its cells are tight (`px-1.5 last:pr-3` since the checkbox column of 2026-11-26, which had left it 20 px over at 1440; `px-2 first:pl-3 last:pr-3` before) and the SKU cell `whitespace-nowrap` — keep them when adding a column. Not sortable yet: Price Alignment's problems table.
- **A promotion's products are ticked in its table, and the ticked ones can be removed** (user, 2026-11-26: "Check box para seleccionar los productos en las promociones y poder eliminarlos o lo que sea"). Every kind and every status, every role: a box on each row and one in the header (`selected` in PromotionCard, the shared [Checkbox](src/components/ui/Checkbox.jsx), which took an `indeterminate` state that day — a dash while only some rows are ticked, read as "mixed").
  - **The header's box ticks or unticks the rows SHOWN**, so the filters choose: the "N out of stock" chip, then the header's box, is every sold-out product. The ticks stay while the list is filtered or sorted; the bar says how many the filters hide ("8 selected · 3 not shown by the filters") and those count like the rest. They are emptied when the country's tab changes and when the list is loaded again.
  - **The bar** (`SelectionBar`) sits at the foot of the table's own scroll box (`sticky bottom-0 left-0`: it comes up over the rows, so no row moves from under the pointer, and stays in view while the list scrolls, sideways too): the count, **Copy SKUs** (the ticked ones, one per line), **Remove** — editors, and not on a promotion that is over — and Clear. A ticked row is gray (`bg-surface-container-high`), not the catalog's primary tint: here that pale red could not be told from an out-of-stock row.
  - **Remove is what Edit SKUs does when those lines are deleted**, without the list being written out ([promotions.js](src/features/pricing/api/promotions.js) `removePromotionSkus`). Where each country has its list (a monthly promotion, an event made from a file per country) the button is "Remove from the Canada list" / "… USA list": a product still on the other country's list keeps its row, without this country's file price and costs, and one on this list only loses its row. On a deal with one list the product leaves the promotion. Only those rows are written, so the products that stay keep the prices their file gave them. One Activity Log entry, which keeps what each removed product held (lists, file price, file costs) to put one back as it was.

Keep at least one." / "… Keep at least one — or delete the flash deal"). An emptied Canada list would send Best Buy nothing, and the discounts already scheduled there would stay.

- **Pricing validations** (user, 2026-11-11: "hay que meter validaciones en el módulo de pricing … hazlas todas"). Three kinds: what STOPS a save, what only WARNS (the person can go on), and one confirmation.
  - **A product's prices.** [priceRules.js](src/features/products/lib/priceRules.js) `priceProblems` and the database's `price_problem()` / trigger `products_valid_prices` ([20261111_products_valid_prices.sql](supabase/migrations/20261111_products_valid_prices.sql)) are the same rule in the same words: a price or a cost is more than 0; the Orange and the Purple MAP are never above the Blue MAP of their table (Canada CAD, USA USD, Wayfair Canada USD, and Costco Canada USD since 2026-11-23); a cost is under the MAP of its own level. Only what a save sets or changes is checked (a rule fires when one of the columns it compares changes); none of the 378 products broke any that day. The product page's Pricing tab lists them while editing and Save stops; the import preview marks the row (`flagPriceProblems` in [priceCheck.js](src/features/import/lib/priceCheck.js)). `priceNotes` never stop a save: a level equal to Blue ("lowers nothing" — 8 products' Orange USD that day), Purple above Orange, MAP above MSRP, more than 40 % under Blue (`BIG_DISCOUNT`; the deepest levels that day were 10 % Orange and 20 % Purple). Change the rule in the database, never only in the browser.
  - **A price that cannot be read is said, never guessed** ([money.js](src/lib/money.js) `readMoney`): "39O" was read as 39 by the spreadsheet import and dropped without a word by the promotion file (the product took its level's price). Now it is an error on the import's row (`"39O" is not a price`) and refuses a promotion file.
  - **A promotion file is taken whole or not at all** (`parsePromoFile`'s `problems`, then `checkMarketList` in [promoChecks.js](src/features/pricing/lib/promoChecks.js)), in New promotion and in Import list: the other country's file, an unreadable / 0 / negative price, a SKU on two rows (the last row used to win, or the database failed), a promo price above the regular MAP, a promo cost of the market's own currency that is not under the promo price — refused, the rows named. Warnings: a promo price that is not under the regular MAP, one more than 40 % under it, and the rows with a SKU and no value (left out as before, now named).
  - **Import list into a promotion reviews first** (`ImportPlan`, `planMarketImport`): nothing is written until the person has seen how many products join, stay and LEAVE that market's list and pressed "Replace the <market> list". A wrong file of 5 SKUs used to empty the list at once, take the promo off Walmart Canada and send Best Buy again.
  - **Flash deals, MK events and peak season events** warn portal by portal (`checkDealList`: the new-deal form and Edit SKUs; products by `loadCheckProducts`, other deals by `overlappingDeals` in [api/promoChecks.js](src/features/pricing/api/promoChecks.js)): SKUs the PIM lacks, no price of the deal's level in the portal's currency (Beyond's flash deals: Purple), switched off for the portal, Azuni on a Stylish store, a level that lowers nothing or goes more than 40 % under, products already in another deal on the same portal and days; and days that are already over. Until then only the monthly form looked at prices. That day the Sinks Direct USA Flash deal W46 had 64 of 80 products without an Orange USD price and Lowe's Black Friday Week 22 of 69 without Purple USD (143 products have a USD MAP and no Orange / Purple USD).
  - **One monthly promotion per month** (ended ones aside): `monthlyOfPeriod` in promotions.js — the form says it as the month is picked, every create function refuses — and the unique index `promotions_one_monthly_per_month` ([20261111_promotions_one_monthly_per_month.sql](supabase/migrations/20261111_promotions_one_monthly_per_month.sql)). The nightly automation takes one promotion per market and day (the active one, else the newest) and left a second one out without a word.
  - **Coupons**: a money amount as much as the product's MAP or more is said once (`overPrice` in `reviewCoupons`); pressing Create coupon again creates it.
  - **A promotion's days are never chosen before today** (user, 2026-11-20: "que PIM no deje escoger fechas anteriores a la actual … si hoy es 20, no podemos escoger 19, ni 10 ni nada, solo del 20 en adelante"). One rule, [promoDays.js](src/features/pricing/lib/promoDays.js) (`pastDayProblem`, `pastDaysProblem`, `pastMonthProblem`, `firstMonth`; today is the Eastern day), in every place of Pricing where a day is picked or read: the new-promotion form (flash deals, MK events, peak season events, a monthly promotion's custom dates), the dates on a promotion's card (a deal's pair, a monthly promotion's days per country) and coupons — the form and the template, where such a row stops the file. The pickers start today (`min`), the form says it as the day is typed and Create stays off, and the saves refuse it in the same words (`assertKindDates`, `updatePromotionDates`, `updatePromotionMarketDates`, `couponProblem`). **Only a day that is being chosen is checked**: a day the promotion already had stays — a deal that began last week can still get a new last day, while moving its first day to another past day is refused — and "Back to calendar" chooses no day. **A monthly promotion on its calendar picks a month**: one that is over in both countries is refused (it was a heads-up), the month running today is not — its days began before today and nobody chose them. Until that day days already over were only a heads-up ("a deal can be written down after the fact"): a deal or a coupon recorded late now starts today. In the app only: the database refuses nothing, so the automations and the rows of the history are not touched. The History's range filter (DealsOverview) still takes any day. Checked that day: 21 cases of the rule on made-up days; in a browser with a stand-in sign-in, live reads and every write captured — a new flash deal (yesterday typed: the message, Create off), a new monthly promotion (October refused, custom dates from yesterday refused), "Black Friday Cyber Monday BB&B / OVSTK" (Nov 9 – Dec 6: its last day to yesterday and its first day to another past day refused with nothing sent; its own first day kept with a later last day: sent), November's days per country (Canada's last day to yesterday refused, a later one sent with the past first days as they were), a coupon and the coupons template (the row named) — at 1440, and at 390 in the dark theme: axe's only finding the top bar's account button, no sideways scroll.

- **Coupons** ([CouponsTab](src/features/pricing/components/CouponsTab.jsx), tables `coupons` / `coupon_products`, [20261109_mk_events_and_coupons.sql](supabase/migrations/20261109_mk_events_and_coupons.sql)): Amazon, Wayfair and, since 2026-11-13, the Sinks Direct stores (user: "Coloca a Sinks Direct aca tambien. Tanto Canada como USA" — `wix_sinksdirect_ca` / `wix_sinksdirect_us`, the stores' promotion channel keys; `COUPON_MARKETPLACES` in couponTemplate.js and the constraint `coupons_marketplace_check`, [20261113_coupons_sinksdirect.sql](supabase/migrations/20261113_coupons_sinksdirect.sql)), Canada or USA, by percentage (one for the whole coupon, `coupons.discount_value`) or by money (per product, `coupon_products.discount_amount`; user, the same day) (each row is one marketplace, each portal having its own coupon: the form picks several and creates one per marketplace), several running at once, each with its products. The EOS ScoreCard counts them in "# of Coupons" (`coupon_count`, Amazon and Wayfair, both countries) and their products in "# SKUs in Coupons", the row above it (`coupon_skus`; user, 2026-11-13: "otro item de # de skus en cupones"): the products of the week's coupons on that marketplace as the Coupons tab lists them, a product of two coupons counted once — both in `scorecardLive.js`, so the crons and an editor opening the tab fill them alike. Created one by one or from the template ([couponTemplate.js](src/features/pricing/lib/couponTemplate.js): one row per product; rows with the same name and marketplace are one coupon; a row it cannot read stops the file; rows named EXAMPLE are skipped). A product not in the PIM or switched off for that marketplace is left out and listed. **A record in the PIM only:** nothing is sent to Amazon, Wayfair or a Sinks Direct store and no expected price changes.
  - **A Sinks Direct coupon is informative too: nothing goes to Wix** (user, 2026-11-13: "dejaremos Sinks Direct como los demás, es decir, solo informativo, no afectará nada a Wix"). That morning he had asked whether a coupon made here could be created on the store too; it was built (an edge function that created, switched off and deleted the store's coupon, a form with Wix's own fields) and taken out again the same day at that word, before any coupon was made with it: the function was deleted and its columns dropped. Do not offer it again unless he asks. What was learned, should he ask: Wix's Coupons API (`/stores/v2/coupons`; one permission, Manage Coupons, which the PIM's key has on both stores) takes ONE code (unique per store, 20 characters at most) with one kind of discount, for the whole store, one product or one collection — never a list of products — and its days as milliseconds; the team makes the stores' coupons by hand in Wix (SAVE10 on both, "25% Off in selected products" on a collection of Sinks Direct Canada). The EOS ScoreCard's Sinks Direct tab has no coupon rows, so a Sinks Direct coupon is counted nowhere yet.
- **The Stylish stores take promotions through special events only** (user, 2026-11-09: "pueden trabajar en porcentajes o por precio, y debemos integrarlo en los special events"). `wix_stylish_ca` / `wix_stylish_us` are portals of a special event (`kinds: ['special']` in promoChannels.js; never monthly or flash), and **such an event goes only to them** (user, the same day: "no vaya a ser que elijan stylish canada y luego otro portal como walmart"): picking one switches every other portal off and the reverse (`PortalPicker`; the two stores can go together), and the constraint `promotions_stylish_alone` refuses the mix in the database. The event says how in `promotions.stylish_discount` ([20261109_stylish_event_discounts.sql](supabase/migrations/20261109_stylish_event_discounts.sql)), edited in the new-event form and on its card ([StylishDiscountFields](src/features/pricing/components/StylishDiscountFields.jsx)): `{ mode: "percent", percent }` or `{ mode: "price", prices: { ca: { SKU: price }, us: … } }`.
  - **The rule** ([_shared/stylishDiscount.js](supabase/functions/_shared/stylishDiscount.js), the user's words: "después [del] 10% off se divide entre 0.3 … ese 27$ queda en 90$"): an approved customer (registered on the website, sent the code by email) buys with the coupon DEAL70%, 70% off at checkout — Wix's own configuration, not editable from the PIM (`CUSTOMER_OFF`); a shopper without the code pays the store price —, so MSRP 100 → the customer pays 30 → 10% off = 27 → 27 ÷ 0.3 = **90 on the store**. By percentage that equals that % off the MSRP (Wix `PERCENT`); by price the person gives what the customer PAYS per product and the store price is that ÷ 0.3 (Wix `AMOUNT` = MSRP − store price). Saving refuses a SKU the PIM lacks or a store price not under the MSRP (a MAP typed by mistake). A product given a price joins the event's list.
  - **It goes as a Wix discount**, so the base price stays at the MSRP: Price Alignment and Listing Health keep comparing the MSRP (`promoAware: false`), and no other push touches it.
  - **The event's card shows what the store takes** (user, 2026-11-11: "ahí solo el MSRP y el promo MSRP"): its table is SKU, **Promo MSRP** (the store's price with the discount — `discountFor`, the very number stylish-event-discounts sends; the cell's tooltip says what an approved customer pays), **Regular MSRP**, Stock and Sold 90 d. No MAP and no cost columns (`stylishEvent` in PromotionCard): it showed the Purple MAP against the Blue MAP and the Rona / dealers / Wayfair costs, which no Stylish store uses. A product the discount cannot reach (no MSRP, no price given, Azuni) shows — with the reason and counts in the "N without a discount" chip. Checked on "Early Black Fridays" (10% off, 22 products): the table's 22 numbers are the 22 store prices sent and read back from Wix (K-146N 530 → 477).
  - **It goes on and off by itself:** the edge function `stylish-event-discounts` (`verify_jwt` off, x-cron-secret or service role; cron `stylish-event-discounts`, `4-59/5 * * * *`, body `{ cron: true }`, honours `promo_automation.enabled` / `wix`) works out each product's discount today and changes only what differs from table `stylish_event_discounts` (site, sku; written BEFORE the Wix call with `confirmed` false, so a run cut short leaves nothing untracked). An event starting, ending, marked ended, a product leaving its list or a new percentage all take that path; overlapping events: the later start wins; Azuni products are skipped. Wix's ~30-changes limit: a run stops after ~100 s and the next goes on. Test with `{ dryRun: true, asOf: "YYYY-MM-DD" }`; the last run's report is `app_settings.stylish_event_discounts_run`. Checked 2026-11-09 by dry run only (start, price and percent, MSRP refusal, take-off, brand rule): no discount has been written to Wix by it yet. The open-box SKUs' 20% / 40% on these stores (278 on that day) are the store's own and are not touched.
- **A flash deal, MK event or peak season event made for a Sinks Direct store goes on the store by itself** (user, 2026-11-11: "que apenas se genere el deal … se suba dependiendo de la fecha; si se pone la fecha de hoy, se envía de una vez"). Until that day only the monthly promotion reached Sinks Direct: Jessica's four deals for Nov 11–16 showed "Running now" and "Sinks Direct — Starts Nov 11" while nothing had been sent.
  - **The edge function `sinksdirect-deals`** (`verify_jwt` off; x-cron-secret, service role or a signed-in admin / editor; cron `sinksdirect-deals`, `1-59/5 * * * *`; honours `promo_automation.enabled` / `wix`) works out the deal price each product should carry on `sinksdirect_ca` / `sinksdirect_us` TODAY — the very expected price of Price Alignment (`loadExpectedPrices` in [_shared/priceAlignmentReport.js](supabase/functions/_shared/priceAlignmentReport.js), which takes a `today` for dry runs: a deal running by its dates with that store among its portals, the later start winning, only its members with a price in that currency) — and changes only what differs from table `sinksdirect_deal_prices` (site, sku; [20261111_sinksdirect_deal_prices.sql](supabase/migrations/20261111_sinksdirect_deal_prices.sql); written BEFORE the Wix call with `confirmed` false). A deal starting, ending, ended early or deleted, a product leaving its list, new dates or portals all take that path.
  - **How each store takes it** (as promo-apply does the monthly one): Sinks Direct Canada = the PIM's sale fields (`on_sale`, `sale_price_cad` = the deal's price) and Wix shows MAP with a discount; Sinks Direct USA = the price itself, discount 0. **When the deal is over** the product goes back to the running monthly promotion's price if it is a member, else to MAP (Canada: the sale fields too), and its row is deleted. A deal price not under the MAP, a product without a MAP or not on the store is skipped and named in the report.
  - **A deal price that something else pushed over is put back within 5 minutes**: `wix-push-product` stamps `wix_links.synced_at` on every push (a product's "Push <store>", which sends MAP to Sinks Direct USA; Price Alignment's fix, which on Sinks Direct Canada lowers the base price and leaves the discount on top; promo-apply at a monthly boundary), and a stamp newer than the row's `applied_at` sends the price again; on Sinks Direct Canada the PIM's sale fields are compared too.
  - A change Wix refuses (404: the link points at a product Wix no longer has) is tried again after 15 minutes, 30, an hour… up to 12 hours (`attempts`, `next_try_at`; `{ force: true }` tries now); the Activity Log hears a refusal the first time only. One run at a time (`app_settings.sinksdirect_deals_lock`). Test with `{ dryRun: true, asOf: "YYYY-MM-DD" }` (the plan, nothing written), `{ sites: [...], limit: N }` for a part; the last run's report is `app_settings.sinksdirect_deals_run`.
  - **The store is read again after a change** (2026-11-13): a run that put prices on a store or took them back asks `health-refresh` for that store's snapshot (see "A deal price seen on Best Buy or Walmart is followed by the channel's snapshot"), so Price Alignment shows the deal within the minute instead of at the next daily snapshot.
  - **The Pricing page sends at once** ([sinksDirectDeals.js](src/features/pricing/api/sinksDirectDeals.js) `sendSinksDirectDeals`, `sendDealsNow` in PricingPage): creating a deal that runs today, Edit SKUs, new dates or portals, Import list, End and Delete call the function until nothing is owed, on the action's own progress card. The deal card's Sinks Direct row reads the table ("78 on the store", "Starts Nov 20", "Taking back 12", "Back at regular prices"). **"Apply to store pricing" and "Push to Wix" are for monthly promotions only** (they work in CAD on Sinks Direct Canada: on a USA deal they would put Canada's sale prices on the Canada store), and **ending a deal only marks it ended** — `endPromotion` used to clear `on_sale` on every member, which took November's price off the products of any deal.
  - First run, 2026-11-11: 138 prices in 29 s with no rate-limit answer (78 on Sinks Direct Canada, 60 on Sinks Direct USA), each read back from Wix at the deal's price. Not sent: A-902BK and S-833WH (Sinks Direct USA no longer has them, their `wix_links` rows are stale), ST-07 (not on that store), and the 64 products of "Sinks Direct USA Flash deal W46" without an Orange USD price. The way back was rehearsed on four products outside the deals (rows added by hand, the store's price unchanged), and both ways of being pushed over were tried. The first real end is Nov 17: check `app_settings.sinksdirect_deals_run` and that the table is empty.
- **Deals on Best Buy and Walmart: `channel-deals`** (user, 2026-11-13, after asking "para flash deals y los demás, ¿están corriendo las conexiones de Walmart y Best Buy?" — they were not: only the monthly promotion is sent by `promo-apply`, and no deal had ever gone to either through the API — "Haz que se haga eso con todos los que tengamos conexión"). The edge function `channel-deals` ([index.ts](supabase/functions/channel-deals/index.ts)) puts the price of a flash deal, MK event or peak season event on **Best Buy Canada, Walmart Canada and Walmart USA** the day it starts and takes it back when it ends, the way `sinksdirect-deals` does on the Sinks Direct stores. **Live since 2026-11-13**: the cron `channel-deals` was created that morning at the user's word ("crea la tarea programada"). Its first run (10:13 Eastern) sent what was left of the two deals running that day — 105 discounts to Best Buy ("Early Black Friday Deals Wk 3", import 41534503 complete, no line refused) and 39 promotions to Walmart Canada ("Walmart Ca Flash deal Week46", all taken, none of them had a promotion in its way), 20 s in all — and the run after it saw them there: 107 of 107 offers with the discount on Best Buy, 41 of 41 promotions on Walmart. **What it must do, in the user's words that day:** a deal goes OVER the monthly promotion — the monthly promotion comes off that product if that is what it takes — and when the deal ends the product goes back to the monthly promotion ("solo necesito que me confirmes eso, tanto en Best Buy como en Walmart Canadá"; explain it to him in those words, not with the mechanics). Before the cron, a test of four products at the user's "ENVIA LA PRUEBA": Best Buy C122 (341, regular 363) and S-509XG (378, regular 402), import 41534163 complete with no line refused, read back on Best Buy with the discount from Nov 13 to Nov 18 and their stock as it was; Walmart Canada B-124S (147, was 157) and C228L (740, was 787), read back on Walmart as REDUCED promotions to Nov 16 23:59:59 Eastern.
  - **What a run does.** For each channel it takes the deal price each product should carry TODAY — the very expected price of Price Alignment (`loadExpectedPrices`: a deal running by its dates with that channel among its portals, the later start winning, its members of that country with a price) — and changes only what differs from what is there. A deal starting, ending, ended early or deleted, a product leaving its list, a new price or new dates all take that path. Table `channel_deal_prices` (channel, sku; [20261113_channel_deal_prices.sql](supabase/migrations/20261113_channel_deal_prices.sql)) holds what the PIM put where, written BEFORE anything is sent. Left out and named in the report: a product switched off for the channel, not listed there, without a regular price, or whose deal price is not under it.
  - **Best Buy** — an offer holds ONE discount. The deal's goes on for its days (`bbDiscount`, the line carrying MAP Blue and the live quantity, as `promo-apply` sends the monthly one) and takes the monthly promotion's place; when the deal is over the monthly promotion's discount is sent again for the products in the one running, for what is left of its days. An offer already selling at the deal's price under another discount (the monthly promotion at the same price) is left as it is (`mode` found). **An offer can also hold a discount scheduled for later** — next month's, sent when its list was loaded: 59 of that deal's 105 offers held December's (Dec 3 → Dec 30, or → Jan 6), seen on the first real send. The deal's takes its place too, so what the offer held is noted in the row (`before`: price, first and last day) and goes back when the deal is over, if it is still to end and the product is not in the running monthly promotion (`promo-apply` sends the next monthly promotion again to every member the day before its window anyway). **Best Buy keeps a discount's days seven hours behind UTC all year** (Oct 22 is stored 2026-10-22T07:00:00Z and Dec 3 is 2026-12-03T07:00:00Z): the Pacific day in summer, an hour off it in winter — read with the Pacific time zone a December start came out a day early (`BB_DAY_MS`). The live offers are read every run, so a discount something else sent over the deal's is put back, and a row is confirmed only once Best Buy shows the discount (price and last day — Best Buy keeps whole Pacific days, `pacificDay`).
  - **Walmart** — **a SKU cannot hold two promotions on the same days** (Walmart's own rule, read in its documentation that day: "The entered promotion dates range is overlapping"; Canada: "it will result in an error"), and the days of a promotion that has started cannot be changed. So the deal is CARVED out of what is there ([_shared/walmartPromoPlan.js](supabase/functions/_shared/walmartPromoPlan.js), `planDeal` / `planBack`, pure): each product's promotions are read from Walmart (`GET /v3/promo/sku`), what touches the deal's days is deleted, the deal's promotion is created for its days, and what a deleted promotion still had after the deal's last day is created again at its own price (the monthly promotion's rest) — so Walmart goes back to the monthly price by itself. A product already at the deal's price is left alone; a deal that is over takes off only a promotion the PIM created (`mode` created, never one it only found), and puts the monthly price back when the product is in the one running and nothing gives it. Canada goes by the PRICE_AND_PROMOTION feed (a Delete feed, then a Create feed — between the two a product in the monthly promotion sells at its regular price for some minutes), the USA by one call per product and promotion (`processMode` DELETE / UPSERT). Up to 40 products a run and market (each is a read of Walmart); a row on record is read again once a day, and when the promotion that carried its price ends.
  - **Running it.** Cron `channel-deals` every 10 minutes (`3-59/10 * * * *`, created 2026-11-13 by copying the `sinksdirect-deals` job's command); `verify_jwt` off; x-cron-secret, service role or a signed-in admin / editor. It honours `promo_automation.enabled` and each channel's own switch (`bestbuy`, `walmart_ca`, `walmart_us` — the same ones as the monthly promotion's). `{ dryRun: true, asOf?: "YYYY-MM-DD" }` gives the plan and sends nothing; `{ channels: [...], limit: N }` a part; `{ force: true }` does not wait. The last run's report is `app_settings.channel_deals_run`, and a run that changed something writes the Activity Log. One marketplace failing does not stop the others; a change a marketplace refuses is tried again later each time (20 minutes, 40… up to 12 hours).
  - **On the deal's card** the Best Buy and Walmart rows read the table while the automation is on (`channelDealsLive`, [channelDeals.js](src/features/pricing/api/channelDeals.js)): "Starts Nov 19", "41 on Walmart", "3 on their way", "Taking back 12", "Back at regular prices" (`dealHow` in promoChannels.js); Walmart's Schedule button is then not offered on a deal (its Generate, the Seller Center file, stays).
  - **Import list sends Best Buy only for the monthly promotion or a deal with Best Buy among its portals** (`onImported` in PromotionCard). Until 2026-11-13 it sent it for every promotion: on a Walmart-only flash deal it would have scheduled its 41 products on Best Buy for the deal's days — found with every call captured, before it ever happened for real.
  - **Most runs do nothing and cost a few small reads** (user, 2026-11-13: "no quiero que la aplicación se ponga lenta", and why would it run every 10 minutes for a deal made for the 15th — "debería saber que tiene que correr es el 15"). The cron is only the clock: every 10 minutes the function asks the database whether there is anything to do, so a deal goes up minutes after its first day begins, one made or changed for today within 10 minutes, and a refusal is tried again. It runs on the server, never in the browser. Three levels:
    - **No deal runs TODAY on these channels and nothing is on record** → the run ends after four small reads (the state among them, for a snapshot still owed). A deal made for another day asks nothing of Best Buy or Walmart until its day.
    - **Else what the PIM wants today is worked out from the database alone** and kept as one short text per channel (`signature`: today, each deal product's price, regular price, deal and days, the monthly promotion's members, what was left out). A channel is LOOKED AT — Best Buy's offers read (5 calls), Walmart's item list and promotions — only when that text changed since its last look (a deal started, ended, was made, changed or deleted, a product joined or left, a price or an exclusion changed, a new day), when something on record is due (sent and not seen yet, refused and to be tried again, a Walmart price to read again once a day), or when the look is old: 30 minutes on Best Buy (a discount something else sent over the deal's is put back within half an hour — it was every run), 2 hours on Walmart (`LOOK_EVERY_MS`). `app_settings.channel_deals_state` holds, per channel, that text and when to look next; without it every run looks. A dry run, `force` and `limit` always look, and a run that looked at nothing writes nothing (the last run's report keeps the last look).
    - Measured that day from the dev machine, with both deals on record: a look = 15 database reads + 5 Best Buy calls, 3.0 s; a run with nothing new = 10 small database reads, no call to Best Buy or Walmart, under a second; a day with no deal = 3 reads, half a second (4 since the snapshot step).
    - **Deployed the same morning (v3), at the user's "Despliega"** — until then every run read Best Buy's offers and Walmart's item list while a deal was on. Tried first on the dev machine (the function's own file called the way the cron calls it, three times in a row, every write refused there and its state kept in memory: the first call looked, the next two ended quiet), then seen on the server: the 10:33 run looked (107 of 107 on Best Buy, 41 of 41 on Walmart Canada, under 2 s) and kept its state — Best Buy to be looked at again at 11:03, Walmart Canada at 12:33 —, and the 10:43 run answered `nothing_to_do` with both channels `unchanged` and wrote nothing. Read a run's answer in `net._http_response` (the cron's calls, kept a few hours) when the report has not moved.
  - **A deal price seen on Best Buy or Walmart is followed by the channel's snapshot** (user, 2026-11-13: "hazlo ya"). Price Alignment and Listing Health do not read a marketplace live: they read its last snapshot (`channel_health`), taken twice a day by `health-refresh` (6:00 am and 1:00 pm Eastern in winter) or with "Run fresh analysis". That day Best Buy read 62 % aligned and Walmart Canada 76 % for hours with every deal price in place: the deals went on at 10 am and the report still compared the 6 am snapshot with the price expected today. Now, when a look READS a change on the channel — a deal's discount on the offers, a promotion on Walmart, or either back off — `channel-deals` writes a fresh snapshot (`takeSnapshot`), the same row `health-refresh` and the page write.

Walmart Canada reads 40 promotions a pull, the ones the report would flag first, so a deal of more products takes a second snapshot at the next run.

A run that only takes an owed snapshot looks at nothing and leaves the last run's report as it is.

Two places could still show the morning's error:

When a daily snapshot leaves some unread, `health-refresh` marks Walmart Canada's snapshot as owed in `channel_deals_state` and `channel-deals` takes the next ones every 10 minutes (six in a row at most), so the report is whole within the hour instead of a day and a half later.

A deal reaches a store at an hour nobody knows, so a product's page is read at the first run with the deal in force, again while it does not show the price Price Alignment expects (within its margin; a sold-out or not comparable page is done), and not after the next morning's run: 3 reads at most, then its ordinary pass. BB&B and Overstock deals go live at ~10-11 PM of the start day (`dealStartsAtNight` in pageStores.js), so their first read is the next morning. A product that joins a running deal gets the same turn from that day (`app_settings.where_to_buy_deal_reads`: deal : store : start → SKU → the day it was first looked at). The morning after a deal's last day its pages are read once. What these reads may spend of ScraperAPI goes on top of the day's allowance, and the next pass shares what is left. Test with `{ mode: "deal-reread", dryRun: true, asOf: "YYYY-MM-DD", run: "morning" | "afternoon" }` (a dry run saves nothing, so a deal already looked at reads as new on later dates). "Reload" re-reads the readings and opens nothing. Sinks Direct, Best Buy and Walmart keep their API reads. The Pricing overview and the Dashboard card list them apart ("Read from the store pages"; the card only once a store has prices) and show the API channels first.

The choice stays when another channel is picked, so Below can be walked store by store (a channel with none says so in the table). It only chooses the rows shown: "Fix all" still pushes every fixable problem. The table's box is focusable (`tabIndex={0}`), since a filter with no row leaves it without a link.

  - **It is the report on screen, with everything the PIM knows about each row** — nothing is pulled from the store or computed again. Sheets: **Summary** (the tiles with their share, the problems above / below, what was left out by reason), **Problems** (the page's table: every problem, whatever All / Above / Below shows), **All prices** (the problems and the products at their expected price), **Left out** (each product the comparison leaves out, with its reason) and **Notes** (what each status, reason and column means). A sheet with no row is not written.
  - **A row:** SKU, model, brand, category, UPC, obsolete; the status in its color, Above / Below, the store's price, the expected one, the difference in money and in % (an aligned row can show a small one: the margin); where the expected price comes from — Regular MAP / MSRP or the kind of promotion, with its name and its days in that country (`loadExpectedPrices`' `promoBySku`, only when the row's price IS a promotion's: Home Depot Canada's NLP rule can put a member at MAP); the product's MAP Blue / Orange / Purple and MSRP in the channel's currency; our stock and the units sold in 30 / 90 days in the store's country; and, for a store read from its pages, the page as a link, SKU confirmed, in stock on the store and the page's note.
  - **What was left out is now a list**: the report (`_shared/priceAlignmentReport.js`) returns `leftOut` — `{ sku, reason, … }` with `not_on_store`, `other_product`, `other_currency`, `read_before_change`, `sold_out`, `no_price` — beside the counters it already had (`notOnStore`, `otherProduct`…), which it still equals. Only the Excel reads it, so the functions that share that file (`scorecard-fill`, `sinksdirect-deals`, `assistant`) need no redeploy for it.

From the product's **Aliases** where the PIM keeps one — Home Depot Canada / USA "Home Depot Item #", Rona "Rona Article #", Lowe's "Lowe's Item #", BB&B "BB&B SKU", Overstock "Overstock SKU", Walmart Canada "Walmart SKU" (87 products are listed there under another SKU than ours: A-801B is A-801B-1), Amazon Canada "Amazon Seller SKU" (`amazon_links`) —, from Best Buy's snapshot ("Best Buy Product ID", the id of our offer), else **the code the store's own page showed** when it was read (`where_to_buy_links.page_item_id`, only from a page that is this product's — not a broken link, another product or another option): "Wayfair SKU", Amazon USA "ASIN", Walmart USA "Walmart Item ID", and Home Depot's and Rona's where a product has no alias (their pages show the very number the alias is; Lowe's and BB&B / Overstock pages show another number, so no fallback there). - Price Alignment's expected price (user rule 2026-10-02): a flash deal or special event RUNNING on that marketplace (its `marketplaces` holds the target's `promoChannel`; it runs by its dates like DealsOverview, only "ended" is over early; overlapping deals: the later start wins; on BB&B and Overstock from the day AFTER its start date, `dealStartsAtNight` — they switch at ~10-11 PM of that date, and Black Friday's start date on Monday 2026-11-09 took their scores to 66 and 64 with every page right, user: "eso no puede volver a pasar los lunes") is the expected price of its SKUs over the monthly promo; else the active monthly promo for that market's members; else the regular price ([priceAlignment.js](src/features/pricing/api/priceAlignment.js) `loadExpectedPrices`; the chip says Flash deal / Special event, and Best Buy fixes schedule the discount for the deal's own dates). Walmart Canada shows prices only through a promo, and `walmart-pull-items` re-reads 40 promos per run, so it ORDERS them: first what the report would flag (the carried reading disagrees with what the PIM expects today, unless confirmed in the last hour), then SKUs sent to Walmart (`wm_ca_scheduled_at`) after their last read (a carried promotion that already ended counts as none since 2026-11-13: its old price could be today's expected one, and the product was never read again), then promo SKUs not read in 12 h, then the rest — on 2026-10-02 19 SKUs were flagged for two days on reads older than the Sept 30 re-push / removal while Walmart was right. A promo member read with no promo on Walmart is "Missing promo price" (it used to drop out); a non-member without a promo has no price on file and stays out.
- Wayfair (Canada and USA) promotion files ALWAYS start from the file the person downloads from Partner Home right before and uploads with Fill file (user decision 2026-09-28) — never a copy in Templates: its "Current" columns are Wayfair's live snapshot. Promotions file ([wayfairPromoFill.js](src/features/pricing/lib/wayfairPromoFill.js)): only the promotion's rows are kept; USA = discount 0, WC Wayfair of the level as `$0.00`, B2B 0. Price change ([wayfairPriceChangeFill.js](src/features/pricing/lib/wayfairPriceChangeFill.js), sheet "Pricing", no dates: Wayfair applies it on import) at both ends of every promotion, monthly included: Promo MAP the day it starts (New MAP = MAP of the level, no cost) and Back to Blue the day it ends (New Base Cost = WC Wayfair Blue, New MAP = MAP Blue); at both ends the New MSRP takes the same value as the New MAP (`NewMsrpUSD` / `NewMsrpCAD`, user 2026-10-16: "la G y la I, las dos con el mismo dato MAP" — Wayfair keeps MSRP = MAP), and every price goes as a plain number in the General format (no "$", decimals only when the price has them). Wayfair Canada (checked on its real files 2026-09-28): the base cost is in USD from its OWN levels `cost_usd_wayfair_ca*` (loaded from the user's list, [20260928_wayfair_ca_levels.sql](supabase/migrations/20260928_wayfair_ca_levels.sql); its promo cost slug `wayfair_ca_usd` derives from them), never the Small Online Dealers CAD cost (user, 2026-10-06: Wayfair and SOD are separate — SOD works in CAD on `cost_cad_wayfair_sod*`, a column that keeps its old name and is labelled "WC · Small Online Dealers"; Wayfair Canada works in USD on `cost_usd_wayfair_ca*`, and `wayfair-add-products` sends that USD cost as its wholesale price); the MAP Wayfair shows is in CAD = the Canada MAP levels (`map_cad`, `map_<level>_cad`), and `map_usd_wayfair_ca*` (that MAP / 1.38) only goes where Wayfair keeps a USD MAP too. Both markets set the B2B promo columns to 0 (Wayfair pre-fills discounts there). Menards works the same way (user rule 2026-09-29, monthly promotions and flash deals): its own file is filled at the start (F = H = MAP of the level, G = WC Menards of the level; rows outside the promotion at Blue) and goes back with "Back to Blue" the day it ends — every row at MAP Blue USD / WC Menards Blue ([menardsPromoFill.js](src/features/pricing/lib/menardsPromoFill.js)). Done tasks are stamped in `promotions.file_tasks` (`promotion_mark_task`); `PromoTaskNudge` reminds the channel owner (`app_settings.promo_channel_owners`, Settings → Promotion file owners; a channel without an owner reminds every admin). Back to Blue carries ONLY the products that went out when the promotion started — for Wayfair the union of `file_tasks["<channel>:promo_file"]` and `["<channel>:price_start"]` `.skus` (a product can be in one only), read fresh (`freshFileTasks`) — at the Blue prices read at that moment; every file is generated from the promotion re-read then (`refreshPromotion`), never the page's copy; Menards' own file has formulas on every row, so its rows can't be removed and those of other products also go back at Blue, and the start file — Menards' file, Wayfair's Promo MAP pricing file — is kept 60 days in the private bucket `promo-files` ([20260929_promo_files_bucket.sql](supabase/migrations/20260929_promo_files_bucket.sql), `savePromoFile` / `loadPromoFile`) so Back to Blue is one click ("Generate"); older files are deleted when promotions load (`expirePromoFiles`) and must be uploaded again, the product list stays. A channel joins the reminders by having `priceChange` (and `priceStart` for a start price change) in promoChannels, with its own `taskHow` wording.
- Lowe's USA promotion files ([lowesPromoFill.js](src/features/pricing/lib/lowesPromoFill.js), monthly, flash deals and special events alike — user rule 2026-10-09): **Kitchen Sinks and Kitchen Faucets share one file** (`Kitchen_Sinks_188_Faucets_225`: Offer # 1 the sinks with sub-division 188, Offer # 2 the kitchen faucets with 225, each its own offer name), and every other group has a file of its own — Bath Faucets 225, Bath Sinks 130 (merchant mike.norris), Drains 208 (strainers, drains), Accessories 188 (the rest of the accessories, colanders and racks). An offer name over 50 characters drops the month name first ("10.1-10.31 Monthly Promotion Kitchen Faucets"). Column W **Comments** carries our SKU on every row (user, 2026-10-15) so the file can be checked; the user deletes it before sending.
- Home Depot Canada promotion files ([homeDepotCaPromoFill.js](src/features/pricing/lib/homeDepotCaPromoFill.js)): the NLP workbook's (kitchen sinks, sheet D29K) **A Fiscal Week** = the Launch Week of Home Depot's **FY 2026 Monthly NLP Launch Schedule** (`NLP_LAUNCH_WEEKS`; user 2026-10-16: the monthly table, not the off-cycle one) whose NLP start is nearest the 1st of the promotion's month (a flash deal / special event: its first day) — October 35, November 40, December 44. Past the table (FY 2027) it stays empty and the summary says so: load the next year's schedule there. The faucets & porcelain Vendor Submission's B Quarter and W Notes ("November Promotion") go by the same month — a monthly promotion is its own month even when its Canada window opens the month before (November's opens Oct 22; it read "October Promotion", Q3, until 2026-10-16). Our SKU goes on every row (user, 2026-10-16): the NLP workbook's Q Notes = the SKU; the Vendor Submission keeps W Notes = "November Promotion" only and takes the SKU in X, the column after it (no header there). The NLP workbook leaves out every product under Home Depot's 5% NLP minimum (its J formula, `ROUNDDOWN((WAS - NLP) / WAS, 4)` < 0.05 — user 2026-10-16) and lists them in the summary; on November 2026's list that was 54 of 93 kitchen sinks. A forecast that comes out 0 (no Canada stock, not tracked, or 60% rounding to 0) goes as 5 in both files (`FORECAST_FLOOR`, user 2026-10-16).
- Amazon Canada / USA promotion files ([amazonPromoFill.js](src/features/pricing/lib/amazonPromoFill.js), the PriceAndQuantity flat file, monthly and deals alike): A = the offer's Seller SKU (Canada from `amazon_links`, USA our SKU), the sale price and the market's window; **column B, Fulfillment Channel Code, stays empty** on every row written (user, 2026-10-14 — it carried FBM since 09-22). Amazon's own example row (ABC123) is left as it is.
- Walmart Canada promotion files ([walmartCaPromoFill.js](src/features/pricing/lib/walmartCaPromoFill.js); the API sends the monthly promotion by itself, the files are for Seller Center by hand, "Generate" on the promotion's card → Marketplaces). The monthly **price & promotion file** (spec "price-mp"): the product's Walmart SKU from Aliases, msrp = price = MAP Blue CAD, action Create, the promo price, the window written in UTC. The **deal file** for flash deals, MK events and peak season events ("DEAL_ITEM.xlsx", Templates purpose Flash deals & events; the dates go in the portal): one row per product, columns found by their headers, the newest upload is the one filled (`promoTemplatesFor`).

  - **Regular Price = the product's MAP Blue CAD, always** (user, 2026-11-13, a few hours after the rule before it: "Regular price debe ser el MAP Blue obligatoriamente del producto, no de la lectura"). For those hours it was the price read from Walmart's API (the latest `walmart_ca` snapshot) with MAP Blue as the fallback — and Walmart's API only answers for a product in a promotion there, whose price it keeps as a plain price: for the 58 products of the running monthly promotion the "regular" price in the file was November's promo price (S-642W 1,123 against a MAP of 1,195). No reading of Walmart is used in this file any more. Promo Price = the promotion's own price for the product (a file's, on an event made from one), else the MAP of the deal's level.
  - A product with no MAP Blue CAD, no promo price, or a promo price that is not under its MAP Blue is left out and named on the page. Checked 2026-11-13 after the change, generated from the page and every row compared with the database: "Canada Boxing Day" 114 rows of 115 members (P-201-2 is switched off there) and "Flash Deals Week 45" 44 rows — every Regular Price the product's MAP Blue, every Promo Price the deal's; the earlier file is still refused.

Flash deals, MK events and peak season events share the one deal file, as before. Do not offer a separate MK events file for Walmart CANADA again unless he asks. **The file he had uploaded was for Walmart USA** (his next message: "El problema es en Walmart USA en MK events"): it was moved there in Templates — see "Walmart USA's MK events go by file" under the deals on Best Buy and Walmart.
- Stock lives in `product_inventory (market, sku)` — a CACHE written only by the `inventory-pull` edge function. **Both markets come from Odoo** (user, 2026-11-10: "quitar eso y cambiar de dónde viene la información. Ahora vendrá de Odoo … de la columna llamada Available for Use"; [20261110_product_inventory_from_odoo.sql](supabase/migrations/20261110_product_inventory_from_odoo.sql)): Odoo's **Calculated Inventory** (Inventory → Reporting), column **Available for Use** = Free to Use (Finished) + Max Buildable Qty from components (`product.product`'s computed `available_for_use` = `finished_free_qty` + `max_buildable_qty`, Odoo's replenishment_report module), read with ONE warehouse selected per market — the user's choice the same day, what can really be shipped: `ca` = **CBG** (Cambridge), `us` = **ATL** (Flowery Branch GA). Not the whole company (Odoo's own marketplace sync reads that: it adds Amazon FBA's AMZ-CAN / AMZ-USA, Irene / Elina / Bonnie and Open Box) and not the screen's "All Warehouses" (both countries together).
  - The screen's warehouse filter is the context key `warehouse_id` (Odoo's own StockReportSearchModel), with both companies allowed as on the screen: a product of the Canada company only reads 0 at ATL (A-801C, A-910, A-913). Checked that day: the API gives the screen's numbers (20 of 20 rows of the user's screenshot).
  - `available` = Available for Use (whole units, never below 0), `on_hand` = Odoo's Quantity On Hand, `warehouses[code]` = `{ on_hand, available, free, buildable, incoming }`, `source` 'odoo'; `dropped_at` stays NULL.
  - **`eta` = when Odoo expects more** (user, 2026-11-11: "llega el…"): the next Expected Arrival of the product's open purchase order lines (ordered, not received yet) of the market's company, with `warehouses[code].on_order` (units), `.expected` and `.late` (the date is already past in Odoo — 26 of 103 products in Canada and 47 of 91 in the USA that day, so Odoo's dates are not kept up). The purchases land in the vendors' own warehouses of that country (Irene-CAN, Elina-USA…), not in CBG / ATL: it is the purchase's date, not the day the units can be sold. Shown by `arrivalText` / `onOrder` in [inventory.js](src/features/pricing/api/inventory.js): the stock chip of a product at 0 ("arrives Nov 27" / "was due Oct 17"), the stock tooltip, the new-promotion warning and the promotion table.
  - A PIM product Odoo does not have (no record, or an archived one) has no row: *not tracked*, never 0 — the report's `notInOdoo`, listed on Settings → Stock. 11 on 2026-11-10: A-931 and A-933 (not in Odoo); D-701H2 / D-702E2 / D-702H2 (Odoo has D-701H-2 / D-702E-2 / D-702H-2 — the dash rule, never merged); S-828WHK / WLK / WNK and S-831WHK / WLK / WNK (archived in Odoo). Matching is exact then case-insensitive; the dash is kept.
  - Guards: Odoo not answering, answering with no product, or with under half of the products a market holds, changes nothing — the last numbers stay and the report carries the reason.

Both are removed: the parser, the upload button, `scripts/gmail-inventory-to-pim.gs`, the Dashboard's "SKUs in the warehouse with no product in the PIM" row. The mailbox script's call (`x-inventory-secret`) is answered "no longer read" and changes nothing; `SHIPSTATION_API_KEY` stays for `shipstation-labels-sync`.

The stock SENDERS (Walmart USA :35, Best Buy :45) are still hourly.

  - **The PIM sends the stock to Walmart USA and Best Buy again since 2026-11-11** (user: "SI QUE LO CUBRA PIM"). Both switches (`walmart_us_inventory`, `bestbuy_inventory`) had been off since 2026-11-10 ("todo lo va a sincronizar Odoo"), but Odoo's own sync still has Walmart off and no Best Buy option at all (only Wix and Wayfair on), so nobody was sending. Both are ON until Odoo covers those two — then they go off again, on the user's word: two senders would fight. Roberto types Best Buy's and Walmart's orders into Odoo by hand (14 and 8 since October), so between a sale and his entry the hourly send carries the stock from before it (Walmart keeps its 2 units of margin, Best Buy none).

A product that sold nothing has no row. Read through [sales.js](src/features/pricing/api/sales.js): the product page's **Sales and stock** card on Overview ([ProductSalesCard](src/features/pricing/components/ProductSalesCard.jsx): stock, on order, sold 30 / 90 days by marketplace, "Stock lasts" = available ÷ the 90-day pace) and the promotion table's "Sold 90 d" column (its "Stock lasts" column was taken out the same day — user: "quitemos esa de stock lasts"; the product's card keeps it). Information like the stock: it never changes a product's own data.
