The one exception: Home Depot USA's Mirakl `quantity` is ALWAYS 1 (user rule 2026-10-01, with the new template "NEW TEMPLATE THDUSA": F WC Blue, H = I MAP Blue, J 1, L "New", O WC Orange, R MAP Orange, U "UPDATE", C "SKU", and M/N, P/Q, S/T the window in the format of the user's filled example, `T00:00:00.000+02:00` … `T23:59:00.000+02:00` — channel `dateStamps`, unless the uploaded template carries example rows of its own; [miraklPromoFill.js](src/features/pricing/lib/miraklPromoFill.js) replaces the template's example rows). Promotions (user idea 2026-09-29): creating one warns about products with 0 units in the markets it reaches (a monthly promotion both, a flash deal / special event its portals' markets — not blocking; "not tracked" is not 0), and a promotion's table tints out-of-stock rows light red with an "N out of stock" chip that filters them.

### Syndication exports (the core domain logic)

`src/features/syndication/exports/` fills marketplace XLSX templates **without altering them**: [templateFiller.js](src/features/syndication/exports/templateFiller.js) edits the worksheet XML in place via JSZip so dropdowns/valid-values/formatting survive (SheetJS would destroy them). Each marketplace exporter (`wayfairExport`, `amazonExport`, `bbbExport`, `menardsExport`) layers its own header detection and row-building rules on top. Wayfair variant grouping: group by `model_name` + dashed SKU root, Finish is the primary axis. Marketplace template files themselves are managed in the Templates page (`marketplace_templates` table + Storage). A marketplace with several purposes (new listing, update, promotions…) asks which file first ([ExportPurposeDialog](src/features/templates/components/ExportPurposeDialog.jsx)); its "N files" is what the selection will download (BulkActionsBar `filesForSelection`, the exporters' own routing — one per template a product resolves to, Lowe's whole set, Menards per category), never the purpose's template count (2026-10-14: Amazon CA said "6 files" for one sink).

A template's formula cells ship with an empty cached value, and Excel shows them blank until it recalculates — and a vendor portal never does. An exporter that fills rows next to formulas writes their RESULT as the cached value (`setFormulaResults`, the `<f>` kept) and calls `recalcOnOpen`.

Home Depot Canada listing ([homeDepotCaExport.js](src/features/syndication/exports/homeDepotCaExport.js), user review 2026-10-05):
- **Basic Data.**
  - **Identity:** B `Stock_Articles`; M vendor `70007082` (VendorData → CAD, so N / BS / BV = CAD; BT / BW = `EA_each`).
  - **Department:** E/F — kitchen sinks + kitchen faucets `29 Kitchen` / `In-Stock Countertops/Kitchen Sinks`; accessories, colanders, racks `26 Plumbing` / `Plumbing Repair`; bath stays `29 Bath` (Faucets / Bath Fixtures).
  - **Descriptions:** G/H ≤ 35 = model + short type + SKU (EN/FR; the SKU keeps the variants apart).
  - **Till receipt:** I/J ≤ 12 = the size `36x18x10in` / `po` (L×W×D; unit dropped, then 1 decimal, if it doesn't fit; faucets `H16in`).
  - **Weights:** Z gross = package; AA net = product, else package − 2 lb unless that is ≤ 1 (then the package's). Both are written with the General number format (`generalStyle`): a whole number reads whole, decimals as they are — the template's first rows are formatted 0.000 (user, 2026-10-14).
  - **Packaging and money:** only L1 is filled; BR = WC Blue (`cost_cad_rona_hd`), BU = MAP Blue (`map_cad`).
  - **Country:** CG = the template's own country list ("VN - Viet Nam", `countryFromList`).
- **Online Core.** E category L3 Workstation for workstation sinks; AP = French warranty; AT = the AA rule; **AU / AV / AW** Assembled Height = the PIM length, Width = width, Depth = height (user, 2026-10-14 — faucets keep their faucet height, round drains their body height and diameter); **AZ** Pick SLA 1; **BA / BB / BC** `CA__Parcel` / `Ontario__Parcel` / `Other__Parcel` for every product (user, 2026-10-14: the lists hang on AY Shipping Method, which reads Parcel for every product with sizes — the old `…Less_than_truckload` values were off that list); row 3's example category is cleared.
- **Add EAN UPC.** D L1-Primary UPC = the product's UPC, as text (same as Basic Data W; the template leaves it empty). The additional UPCs stay empty.
- **Digital Assets.**
  - Images `SKU.jpg`, `SKU_2.jpg`…
  - PDF #1 spec sheet, #2 installation manual, #3 the brand's warranty (`Stylish_warranty.pdf`). Each PDF is ONE file: the `en_fr` one, else English.
- **ECO and HAZMAT.** ECO questions all "No"; HAZMAT C–L "No", O `0`, Q `SOLID`.
- **Untouched.** "Consolidated Data (DO NOT EDIT)".

Amazon listings, Canada and USA (user review of the Canada kitchen sink template 2026-10-06, extended to the USA and to every Amazon category 2026-10-09). [amazonMapping.js](src/features/syndication/exports/amazonMapping.js): `AMAZON_LISTING_RULES` sit on top of `AMAZON_RULES` for EVERY Amazon listing template; `AMAZON_KITCHEN_SINK_RULES` add the sink-only columns when the template is SINK and every node it offers is a kitchen one (Canada's "Recommended Browse Nodes", the USA's "Item Type Keyword"). **For Amazon every sink but a bathroom sink is a kitchen sink**: bar / prep, laundry and outdoor sinks list on the kitchen sink template (`AMAZON_KITCHEN_SINKS` in templates.js); bathroom sinks keep theirs. Letters are the Canada kitchen sink template's:
- **Every category:** **A SKU** = the market's Amazon Seller SKU (`amazon_links`, the Aliases tab), our SKU only when there is none (no USA Seller SKUs are loaded yet), Model / Part Number stay ours; **V Manufacturer** "Stylish International Inc." in Canada, **"Stylish USA Inc." on Amazon USA**, Azuni always "Azuni"; **AH** swatch = main image; **AO** generic keywords in ONE cell (user, 2026-10-14) — phrases about the product separated by commas, an English part, then a Spanish part, then a French part, ≤ 250 bytes ([amazonKeywords.js](src/features/syndication/lib/amazonKeywords.js)): English and French are the PIM's own `keywords_en` / `keywords_fr` in their order; the PIM has no Spanish keywords, so Spanish (and the English or French of a product without keywords — 56 accessories, K-153S) is built from the product's kind, material, mounting, size, bowls, features and color, the color agreeing with the noun ("placa negra mate"). The part with the fewest bytes takes the next phrase (≈ 80 bytes each), a phrase adding no new word is skipped, a French phrase in the English list (C430L, S-310G) or one over a third of the cell is not used, and composite granite always says "composite" and "granite" in English; **BQ** Included Components in one cell, one per line; **DD** Skip Offer "No"; **DG / DH** MAP Blue of the market (`map_cad` with CAD; USA `map_usd`, no currency column); **EE** Fulfil(l)ment by Merchant (Default) for EVERY listing, FBA Seller SKUs included (user: "todos deberían ser FBM"), in each template's own spelling; **EF** the market's stock (`product_inventory` available, 0 when not tracked); **FI** "ALL CANADA SHIPPING CHARGES" (USA "FBM USA", its only option); **FR** 2 boxes for a "-2" SKU; **FS** Safety Warning "No" (no such column in the USA files); **GE / HK** empty; **IN / IX** (and the user manual) **English-only first**, then EN-FR, then language-neutral, never French-only — a sink takes the installation manual of its own mounting (`installation_undermount` / `_dual_mount` / `_drop_in` / `_top_mount`, then `installation_manual`).
- **Faucets:** Item Height = the faucet height (they have no external dimensions); Hole Count = their installation holes, unit "Holes" (the templates' only count unit — "Count" is not on their list).
- **Kitchen sink template only:** **L** browse node (USA: Item Type Keyword) Single / Double Bowl by the bowls; **AU** Material "Quartz" and **CZ** Rock Material "Granite" on composite granite only; **CF** Drain Type "Grid"; **CG / CH** "Holes" / 1 on top-mount sinks only; **CY** Set Name = `L" x W"` + mounting + "Kitchen Sink" (no Single / Double Bowl, user 2026-10-09).
- **G Item Name (≤ 75) / H Item Highlight (≤ 125)**, English only (the templates carry only en_CA / en_US fields), from Jessica's rules (2026-10-09, [amazonTitles.js](src/features/syndication/lib/amazonTitles.js) `amazonTitle(product, { colorVariant })`), only from the product's own data:
  - Sinks: Brand, series, `L inch`, material, bowls, mounting, Workstation (a cutting board / colander / rack / lid / basket in its accessories), "Kitchen Sink", color, SKU. Too long → drop SKU, bowls, Workstation, series, then color — never the color of a color variant (a non-archived family sibling with another finish, `colorVariantSkus` in amazonExport.js). A coating (graphite black, Dura-Tek) replaces "Stainless Steel" in the Name. Plain brushed stainless shows no color unless it is a variant. Bathroom sinks `L x W inch`. A sink without a series of its own ("Azuni Sink", "Laundry", none) takes its Model Name in the series' place, as on Amazon today (user, 2026-10-15): "Azuni Nantes 25 inch…", "Stylish Spinel 22 inch…" — the 12 Azuni sinks and S-320T; the Highlight then says "<model> collection".
  - Faucets: Brand, model name, handles, the title's type, finish; then the style words one by one ("Squared"). Accessories: the PIM title with its color moved to the end and "Set of 2" → "2-Pack"; a with / without / for clause goes first.
  - **The Highlight is in Amazon's current format** (user, 2026-10-14, after trying a title-style one: "hazlo con el mismo formato actual de Amazon"): short comma fragments, sentence case, ≤ 125, that complement the Name (Amazon shows them under it). S-320T: "18 gauge T-304 stainless steel, 12 inch deep, strainer included, …". What doesn't fit is left out and the next fragment still gets a try (`fitHighlight`; a `{ text, rank }` fragment is shown where it stands but tried at its rank).
    - Sinks: "N gauge T-304 stainless steel" (coated: "N gauge T-304 with graphite black coating"); the bowls / low divider / workstation the Name dropped; "N inch deep" (gives way to the accessories); the accessories "… included" (a grid reads "bottom grid"); minimum cabinet size, dual-mount install, series, drain, "N mm radius corners".
    - Faucets: the handles the Name dropped, the spray "… modes", sensor activation, "lead-free" + material, the cartridge, "360 degree swivel spout", GPM, "single-hole install", style words, deck plate / supply lines included, overall height, spout reach.
    - Bathroom sinks: "<shape> basin", material, with / no overflow, drain, depth, included. Accessories: the PIM's own material first, then what the Name dropped, included, size (longest side first outside boards / colanders / racks), finish, juice grooves, BPA-free.
  - The validator (`titleIssues`, a port of the skill's) bans `! $ ? _ { } ^ ¬ ¦`, quotation marks for inches, "reversible" (drains read "side drain"), promo phrases and a word more than twice. Title Case in the Name.
  - Data mismatches are reported, never fixed: a title color that is not the Finish, a title size that is no dimension, Number of Pieces ≠ 1 + the accessories.
- **Update listing (Amazon Canada, 2026-10-14):** the template is Seller Central's **Category Listings Report** (settings `flavor=inventory-report…`), kept in Templates as the MASTER — every live listing, every product type in one sheet (530 rows on 10-06). `generateAmazonFromTemplate` recognises it and hands it to `fillAmazonUpdate`:
  - Only the selected products' rows go out, found by their Seller SKU (Aliases; Amazon USA = our SKU). Each row is rebuilt from the PIM with its own product type from the report (SINK, FAUCET, DRAIN_STRAINER…), the per-type Valid Values (`buildAmazonValidValuesByType`) and the listing rules plus `AMAZON_UPDATE_RULES`; the report's own values never ride along. A product with no Seller SKU or not in the report is left out and the export says so.
  - **Listing Action "Edit (Partial Update)"**: a blank cell leaves Amazon's value as it is ("Create or Replace" would wipe it). Status and Product Id Type / Product Id are the report's (both required); Title = the new Item Name; the variation columns go blank — **Amazon's 78 "Family N" parents are kept as they are** (user, 2026-10-14; Amazon's numbering, not PIM families) and never written.
  - **Only the columns that apply to the row's product type are filled** (`columnApplicability`: each column's conditional format names `ApplicablePTList<n>`, a workbook name listing the product types). One label can be different attributes per type (Item Width Side to Side = `item_width_height` for drains, `item_depth_width_height` for sinks); `AMAZON_UPDATE_RULES` maps the report's spellings ("Item Depth Front to Back", "Height Base to Top"…) onto the listing rules, and a measure gets its unit (Inches / Pounds).
  - **A partial update replaces each attribute it names as a whole**, so where a row fills one member of an attribute with several, the others are copied from the report (`keepRestOfAttributes`): Amazon keeps the pricing rule ("Top 10"), the minimum seller price, the B2B price, the release date and the dimensions the PIM lacks. A list (bullets, keywords, materials: the same member #1, #2…) is the PIM's whole list. A unit without the PIM's measure is never sent (Amazon's 6.6 Centimeters would have read 6.6 Inches).
  - **Prices (user: "enviaría todo")**: Your Price = List Price = MAP Blue of the market; Sale Price + start / end = the promotion RUNNING on that Amazon today (`expectedPricesFor` in [expectedPrices.js](src/features/pricing/api/expectedPrices.js) — the same expected price Price Alignment uses: a flash deal / special event on `amazon_ca`, else the monthly promo for the market's members). No running promo → Sale Price blank (Amazon's own stays). Quantity = stock, FBM, as in the listing.
  - Kitchen sink browse nodes are read in either spelling (`bowlNode`: listing path "… > Single Bowl (8842616011)" or report name "Single Bowl Kitchen Sinks (8842616011)").
  - `amazon_links` holds ONE Seller SKU per product and market (unique index). On 10-14 the report's 14 Seller SKUs of products without one were loaded (ST03, ST-01, DA-01/04, H-03/06/07/24, P-200-2…); 74 products have a SECOND listing on Amazon Canada besides the one in Aliases, and 16 report rows match no PIM SKU — neither is updated.
- Still to come: the Amazon USA update listing (same code once its report is uploaded). Colanders have no Amazon template (as before their move to Accessory).

Home Depot Canada **update listing** (Vladimir's "Item Maintenance Export", Templates purpose "Update listing", 2026-10-13): `generateHomeDepotCaFromTemplate` recognises it (a sheet whose row 1 has Item Number + Online Product Name(EN)) and hands it to `fillHomeDepotCaUpdate`. One row per product from row 3 (Home Depot's example row is replaced), keyed by the **Home Depot CA item number** (Aliases tab; a product without one is left out and the export says so). Every column takes the new-listing rule — EN/FR name, marketing (description as text), bullets 1-14, warranty, image names `SKU.jpg`… and PDFs #1 spec sheet / #2 installation manual / #3 the brand's warranty — and the same media .zip, one folder per SKU. Columns are found by header.

Home Depot Canada also downloads a `<file>_media.zip` (`downloadMediaZip`, parts past 600 MB) with exactly the files the sheet names: ONE FOLDER PER PART NUMBER with its images and PDFs (user, 2026-10-05: entries carry `folder`; a folder is never split between parts, and the brand's warranty is fetched once and copied into every folder). **Home Depot USA has no zip** (user, 2026-10-09): its images go only as the links in the sheet.

Home Depot USA listing ([homeDepotExport.js](src/features/syndication/exports/homeDepotExport.js), user reviews of the Sinks file's kitchen sinks 2026-10-09 / 10-13 and of the Kitchen Faucets file 2026-10-13; letters are each file's own):
- **Both files:** **S** Country of Origin Name exactly as the ReferenceData writes it, in capitals: "CHINA", "VIET NAM" (it was "China" / "Vietnam"); **Y** boxes = N for a set of N (a "-2" SKU or "Set of N" in the title — P-201-2 ships in 2), else 1; **BO** Color Swatch = the main image; **EB / EC / EG** (CED states, screen size, compostable) empty.
- **Sinks file:** **BP / BQ** granite composite = "Granite" with no percentage (user, 2026-10-14; the 80% quartz / 20% acrylic split of 10-13 is not sent), any other single material = 100; **CG** Measurement Guide empty (the cut-out template does not go); **FF** Minimum Cabinet Size from `min_internal_cabinet_size_in`, rounded up to the list; **FO** Color Family "Silver" for stainless steel, "Black" for graphite black, granite and porcelain their own color. Kitchen-type sinks (every sink but a bathroom one): **FK** Included = "Strainer" and **FX** Features = "Rust Resistant", one value each.
- **Faucets file:** cold water / drinking taps (`isColdWaterTap`: category Cold Water Tap or a "cold / drinking water" type or title — never a combo, never a pot filler) are Beverage Faucets with **FD** Features "Cold Water Only"; **K** Item Weight = the package's − 1 lb when the PIM has none; **U** Sell Pkg Qty 2 on combos; **BZ** Warranty empty (no PDF link); **ES** "Commercial / Residential"; **EW** Sensor Activation = the PIM's `sensor_activation` (Specifications → Faucet Configuration, HD's list; K-150 = Touchless), else read from the spray / product type; **EY** "No Spacing - Single Hole" and **FH** "All Mounting Hardware" on every faucet; **ET** Manufacturer Warranty waits for Pedro. Columns the "Columns" sheet marks NA for a collection (Beverage Faucets, Pot Filler) stay empty.

Lowe's USA new listing ([lowesExport.js](src/features/syndication/exports/lowesExport.js), a two-file set: Item Template .xlsm + SOS Freight Analysis .xlsx; user's review with the team lead, 2026-11-06):
- **Values come from the file** (user: "lo que se pueda llenar libre lo llenamos tal cual"). A column with a closed list takes its value from the template, read at every export: the Item Template's Reference Data sheet and dropdowns (`referenceLists`, `dropdownsOf`), the Freight's dependent dropdowns on its Assortments sheet (`ASSORTMENT_BLOCKS`: SDV by Division, PG by SDV, Assortment by PG, Marketing Category by Division). A value off its list is left blank and named in the export's message (`describeLowesExport`). A free column goes as the PIM has it. Being on the list is not enough: read Lowe's definition in Reference Data ("Kitchen Sink Accessories … not intended for standalone pieces").
- **Cells keep the template's format.** The Freight's rows are pre-formatted (`mergeRows(…, true)`); the Item Template's data rows hold almost no cells, so a written cell takes its column's style (`columnStyles`) — without it, it loses the format and comes out locked, since that sheet is protected. Availability Start Date is a real date (`excelSerial`): the template's Readiness Score (`COUNTV` in its macro) counts a cell only if it passes its validation. That score is 40 of 41 today; the one left is **AA Like Product URL**, not filled yet. Lowe's own protection leaves A, J, L, M, BV, CO, DI and EC locked; everything else stays editable, as in the template.
- **The product files come in the ZIP** (user, 2026-11-06: everything is uploaded to Lowe's by hand). The set's ZIP holds the two files and `Products/<SKU>/`: `SKU.jpg`, `SKU_2.jpg` and on in the PIM's order, `SKU_spec_sheet.pdf` and the like by document type (the brand's warranty in every folder), `SKU_video.mp4`. **Language, for all of it (user): English-Spanish, else English, else English-French; French alone never** (`LANGUAGE_ORDER`, Lowe's own, not the pushes' USA order). Photos and videos: that one set plus the ones without language, never the gray Sinks Direct hero. Documents: one per type in the best language, a file without language where the type has none of the three. `downloadMediaZip(…, { into, root, partName })`: past ~600 MB the rest goes on in `_part2` and on (a SKU's folder is never split, the two files are always in the first).
- **Item Template:** **BH Lowes Merchant Email** changes often (user), so a new-listing export asks for it every time ([LowesMerchantDialog](src/features/templates/components/LowesMerchantDialog.jsx), the bulk bar and the product page; the last 5 typed in the browser are suggestions, none is filled in by itself) and the exporter keeps no default; **AV Online - Customer Maximum Order Quantity** stays blank; **BI Full Truckload Quantity - 53-ft Trailer** = how many of the product's shipping boxes fit in the trailer by size (`truckloadQty`: inside 630 × 100 × 108 in, the best of the box's six orientations, every box laid the same way; no weight limit; blank without the box's three dimensions).
- **Freight:** Kitchen sinks = SDV KITCHEN SINKS & DISPOSERS (188), PG KITCHEN SINKS, Assortment **SS KITCHEN SINKS**; **H GTIN** = our UPC as it is; **BI 52 Week Forecast** = USA stock, else what is on order at Odoo's ATL warehouse (Inventory → Reporting → Stock, Incoming; `odoo-stock`), else 10, and 10 when Odoo cannot be read; **BS** stays blank. The file's formula cells ship empty and the workbook does not recalculate by itself, so SDV #, PG #, Asst # and the margins are written as results (`setFormulaResults`) and `recalcOnOpen` is on. **Open with the user:** the other categories' Freight values (a file has one Division, and PLUMBING REPAIR is under ROUGH PLUMBING, so strainers and drains come out with A / D / F blank; boards, colanders, racks and deck plates with G blank), and whether granite kitchen sinks also take SS KITCHEN SINKS (the file has GRANITE KITCHEN SINKS; the code uses SS for every kitchen sink, as the user said).

Menards new product file ([menardsExport.js](src/features/syndication/exports/menardsExport.js), rules in [menardsFile.js](src/features/syndication/lib/menardsFile.js); Vladimir's Asana task "Review and Improve Menards New Product Template", 2026-11-25, with the file he completed for C234L: "NEW Template to be created.xlsx"). Menards takes products through Syndigo's **Menards Product Content** workbook (requirement set 39): sheet "Attributes" — rows 1-5 the columns (ids, source, locale, Required / Conditional / Optional, names), a product a row from row 6, 206 columns —, and "Reference", every column again with what it takes and a list's entries. **One file for every category** (Templates: Menards, New listing, no category): a selection's products go in it together, one row each.
- **The export did not work at all until 2026-11-26.** Every part of a file Syndigo writes starts with a byte-order mark, which the shared reader could not parse: the files in Templates (the July set for kitchen sinks: a content file of 251 columns and five "Containers" files) answered "No data sheet found" and nothing downloaded. `parse()` in templateFiller.js takes the mark off. And what a row held was wrong against his file: the account's columns empty (vendor, merchant, cost, terms, freight, master pack), "Yes" where the list says "Y", "inch" / "pound" / "year" for "inches" / "pounds" / "month", a retail pack "Box" that is on no list, the box's width and depth the wrong way round, Imported Flag "Yes", a Description 1 longer than its 40 characters.
- **The rules are his file** (C234L: 203 of 206 cells come out the same; the three that differ are a doubled space in his title, the Long Description he wrote by hand, and the cost — his 436, the PIM's 436.10).
  - **The company's account, the same on every product** (`MENARDS_ACCOUNT`): Vendor Number STYLI001, Merchant "Alexander Perrine (aperrine)|1662206", Associate Merchant "Crystine Adams (cadams)|1630168", Sales Unit EACH, Payment Terms "Net 30 Days", minimum order 1, lead time 1 day, Shipping Method OTHER, Ship Combination Y, Retail Packaging Type "Menards.com only-Direct to Guest", Imported Flag N (Menards is not the importer), Container Qty 1, Import Container Size N/A, Freight Type UNITS, Freight Min 1, Vendor Ships Via FED EX GROUND, Truckload Qty 0, FOB MENARD, DDP N/A, Inner Packaging "As is product- No Inner Package", Master Packaging "Regular-all right angles-Corrugated Box", Master Pack Qty 1, and the answers that are No on everything (expiration date, limited quantity, refrigerated / heated van, nestable, peggable, AGA, perishable, hazard code, Prop 65 — "Not Labeled" —, OSHA, Energy Star, no lithium). **No certification is declared**: ANSI Compliant No and Certified Mark Name "NOT REQUIRED", as his file answers for a cUPC sink. A change of merchant, terms or carrier is made there.
  - **From the PIM:** Model Number and Description 2 = the SKU; **Description 1** = what the product is in a few words, 40 characters at most (`menardsShortName`: "Workstation Double Bowl", "Undermount Single Bowl", "Pull-Down Kitchen Faucet", "Kitchen Faucet with Cold Water Tap", "Vessel Bathroom Sink"; an accessory its model name); **MCOM Short Description** = `Brand&reg; Model&trade;` and the General Title ("Azuni&reg; Laval&trade; 32 inch Dual Mount …"; an accessory's model name is its description and a combo's is "Combo": no mark, the brand and the title); **Long Description** = the PIM's description as plain text, whole (the file says 255 characters and his has 640: not cut); Brand; Country Of Origin as the list writes it ("China (CN)"); Mfg Location "None / Outside the USA"; **Mfr Warranty in months** (Lifetime = 9999, 5 Years = 60, 1 Year = 12); **Cost = WC Menards Blue, MSRP = Minimum_Advertised_Price = MAP Blue USD** (Menards' MSRP is the MAP, as on Wayfair, Rona and Walmart); HTSUS Number = the USA HS code, its description "Handmade kitchen sinks" (a handmade sink; else the material and the kind; a faucet its kind; an accessory its model name); ADA from the PIM (empty = N).
  - **Sizes:** Menards' Width is the PIM's length and its Depth the PIM's width (his box of 37 × 24 × 13.5 is Width 37, Depth 24, Height 13.5). The retail pack is the shipping box and its weight; **the master pack is the same box** (one unit a box), its UPC the product's and its SSCC-14 the UPC with zeros in front to 14 digits. Assembled Height / Width / Length / Weight = the product's own depth (a faucet: its height), length, width and weight. A unit is written only beside its measure, in the list's own word. **A 0 stored in the PIM is not a measure**: left empty (K-145's height that day).
  - **Only what his file says, never worked out:** Product Port Of Origin "China - Guangzhou" for China and "None" for the USA, Canada and Mexico (the file's own help) — **Vietnam's port is not known: empty on its 23 products** —, and Duty Rate 0.284 for 7324.10.0050 from China (80 products); every other tariff code and country is left empty.
  - **Pictures:** Master Image = the main image; "Alternate Product View NN" = the NNth picture of the gallery from 02 on (01 is the main one's place, as in his file), up to 20: the USA's choice of artwork (English, else English-Spanish, else English-French) plus the photos without language, never the gray Sinks Direct one. A cell holds the picture's **file name** as stored in the PIM ("c234l_workstation_kitchen_sink_00_e-i1o1lj.jpg"), as his file does, and the ZIP carries every picture named, under that very name, in an **Images** folder — to upload to Syndigo's library before the workbook when they are not there yet. `MENARDS_IMAGE_CELL` in menardsFile.js is the switch to the PIM's public link instead ('url': no folder then). **The user chose the names with the pictures in the ZIP** (2026-11-26, asked which of the two). Whether Syndigo's import would take a link for a product that is new there was not confirmed — his file reads like an export of the finished product, where Syndigo shows names: asked of Vladimir in the task.
- **Every value is held to the file's own Reference sheet**, read at each export: a list takes one of its entries in its own spelling (a yes / no is "Y" / "N" in one column and "Yes" / "No" in the next), a number its decimals, a text its length (cut at a word and said), a UPC its digits. A value off its list is left empty and named, never written. Every cell is a text in its column's format, as in Syndigo's files. The file is not altered: the workbook in the ZIP differs from the template only by its product rows (a template that still holds a product's row gives that row's place to the new ones).
- **The download** is one ZIP, `Menards_<SKU | category | N_products>_<date>.zip`: the workbook under the template's own name and the Images folder (past ~600 MB the rest goes on in `_part2`). After it, the message and the readiness report name what a person must look at: the Required columns left empty with their products (those of the same products said together), the values off a list, the texts cut, the pictures that could not be downloaded.
- **Pictures are asked for again when Storage turns a burst away** (`downloadMediaZip`'s `fetchBlob`, shared with Home Depot Canada's and Lowe's ZIPs): a 429, a 5xx or a dropped connection is tried three more times, later each time; 4 of 139 pictures had been left out of a test run that way.
- **The template in Templates** is his file without the product's row, the dropdowns and checks of rows 6:7 stretched to every row as Syndigo's own blank has them ("Menards Product Content - New Products.xlsx", made by that day's scratchpad `make-blank.py`; every other part of the workbook is his file's, byte for byte), put there on 2026-11-26 once the new code was on main, at the user's word (he had asked "lo subo a PIM, o lo subes tú?"). A blank downloaded from Syndigo works as well. **The July set was taken out of Templates the same day, at his word** (requirement set 30, kitchen sinks only: its seven rows; one Activity Log entry). Its files stay in the `templates` bucket under `marketplace-templates/menards/17840462…` to `…17840463…`, so one is put back by adding its row again. Such a set would still fill — by the same rules, its category's own columns as before and now held to their lists (Includes, Number of Faucet Holes "0" and Bowl Configuration "Double Bowl" are off Menards' lists there and stay empty) — and a file kept for one category goes to that category's products beside the general one.

Walmart Canada listing ([walmartExport.js](src/features/syndication/exports/walmartExport.js)): price = MAP CAD and **MSRP = MAP CAD too** (written 2026-11-06, confirmed by the user 2026-11-10), so Walmart shows no struck-through list price — the same rule as Wayfair and Rona. **Product Tax Code = 2038347 on every product** (`WALMART_CA_TAX_CODE`; user, 2026-11-17: "falta el product tax code" — the column went out empty and Walmart.ca does not publish without it). It is the code Seller Center shows on our listed products, read there by the user on a sink and on a faucet: Walmart keeps its list of codes inside Seller Center and no connection returns a listed product's own. Accessories take the same until the user says otherwise; Walmart's public guide names that number among its shipping tax codes, so if a file is refused for this field, ask the user to read it in Seller Center again. Checked that day: the file generated by the app's own exporter in a browser for C133L, C136L, C234L and C236L — the column filled on the four rows.

Rona new listing ([ronaExport.js](src/features/syndication/exports/ronaExport.js), the Vendor Quote Sheet, Templates purpose New listing; user, 2026-11-09): one sheet, labels on row 3, one row per product from row 4, columns addressed by letter because boxes and warehouses repeat their labels (the anchors are checked first). A vendor # and E vendor name = the promo files' `RONA_SUPPLIER`; B main image link; H from the template's own Product Categories (`CATEGORY`, accessories by name in `ACCESSORY`, off the list = blank and reported); I Bilingual; J the day of the export; K CAD; L WC Blue (`cost_cad_rona_hd`), M blank; N = O = MAP Blue CAD (Rona's MSRP is the MAP, user); P / R / S / U / V the prices the PIM read on Home Depot, Amazon, Walmart, Wayfair and Best Buy Canada (only readings Price Alignment trusts, in stock, in CAD); W–Z box 1 = long side, short side, height, weight (a set of N fills N boxes, up to 5); AQ Ship on Skid N, AR / AS / AT the one warehouse (85 Thompson Dr, Cambridge, N1T 2E4, loading dock Y); HS Code blank (everything ships from Canada). Missing data is listed in the export's `warnings`.

**Rona's new listing is two files, and the export asks which one** (user, 2026-11-19: "se va a generar preguntando al usuario cuando le dé new listing si quiere generar el primer template … o el segundo"; "el primero ya estaba lleno, el que hay que llenar es el que te acabo de subir"). After the purpose question, [RonaListingDialog](src/features/templates/components/RonaListingDialog.jsx) — on the catalog's bulk bar and on the product page's Export Templates — offers **DSCO Template** and **VQS Template**, each with the name of the file it fills; nothing is asked when a selection has only one of them (a kitchen sink: only the Vendor Quote Sheet). [ronaTemplates.js](src/features/syndication/lib/ronaTemplates.js) tells a Templates row by its file name (`ronaTemplateKind`: "Vendor Quote" / "Catalog Items"), and `generateRonaFromTemplate` opens the file and fills it the way its own sheets say, so a wrong guess still fills right.

In his message the user called the one just uploaded "VQS Template": told to him, the two labels swap in `RONA_LISTING_KINDS` if that is how the team says it.
- **The DSCO catalog file** ([ronaDscoExport.js](src/features/syndication/exports/ronaDscoExport.js), `fillRonaDsco`) is one file per Rona category. "Catalog Data": names on row 1, a row per product from row 2; the file comes with row 2 holding only `sftp_retailer_id` and `sftp_category`, and every row written carries them. "Help and Glossary" repeats the columns with what each asks, Required / Recommended / Optional (72 / 137 / 92 in the faucets file), its type and, under them, a pick list's values. **Columns are found by name, never by letter** (a category's own attributes follow the shared ones and the next category's file has others); a pick list takes only a value of the file's own list, a Number column a number, and the DSCO platform's own Optional columns, which repeat Rona's under other names ("msrp", "map", "warranty", "currency_code"), are left alone. The file downloads as `…_DSCO_<date>.xlsx`.
  - **A file takes only the products of its category** (`CATEGORY_FITS`, from `sftp_category`): "Bathroom Sink Faucets" = bathroom faucets. Another product is left out and named ("Left out: P-200 (this DSCO file is Rona's "Bathroom Sink Faucets" category)"); a selection with none is refused in words. A new category's file gets its line there and the rules its attributes need. A selection of several categories downloads one DSCO file per category, and the question names every file under DSCO Template.

No sink includes a faucet: Faucet Included No, Manufacturer Faucet Finish "N/A", Max Flow Rate the list's "N/A". On a faucet the PIM keeps no such list, so Drain Included stays empty there.

    - **Kitchen sinks' own columns:** Bowl Configuration from the bowls and the Basin Split (50/50 = Double equal bowl, another split = Double offset bowl) and Basin Split itself; Depth of Bowl 1 / 2 = the left and right bowl depths of a double sink, the internal depth of a single one; Length / Width = the external length and width; Color/Finish Family ("Stainless steel" for brushed stainless, else the Color, "Graphite Black" = Black); Finish = Finish Type; Material; Sink Mount Type (Dual Mount = "Drop-in or undermount", Top Mount = "Drop-in"); Sink Type "Apron-front" for a farmhouse sink, else "Standard"; Drain Location (Rear, Center, a side drain = "Offset") and Reversible from the same field (Yes when it says so — "Side Drain / Reversible" —, No when it does not — "Rear Center"); Workstation from the name or the flag; **from the Includes list**: Strainer Included, Drain Included (a kitchen sink's drain is its strainer: Yes on all 119), Sink Grid Included (also the switch), Drainboard Included No; Faucet Included No; Sink Gauge (the gauge, "N/A" for granite); **Minimum Cabinet Size = the PIM's minimum EXTERNAL cabinet size, as the next size up on Rona's list** ("Less than 30-in" … "Greater than 36-in" — my reading; Home Depot USA's column takes the internal size); Series Name; Drain Connection Diameter (3.5).

Kitchen sinks, 2 Required: Apron Height and Apron Pattern (they are for apron-front sinks; Vladimir's file leaves them empty too) — the other ten took the answers of his file, below. Per product: Finish on the 6 Dura-Tek sinks (not on Rona's list), Heat Resistance on the 33 granite sinks, one sink without faucet holes (C136L: the PIM has no count for it, its three siblings have 0); on faucets Faucet Type (6 taps), High-arc Spout (7), Spray and Stream (6), heights (3 pot fillers), and K-153S, which has no content yet.

### Edge functions (supabase/functions/)

- `assistant` — the assistant's chat with Claude (the Assistant page and the panel over any page): answers from this file and looks live data up with the session of the person asking; a change is only prepared, for that person to confirm, and an Excel file only described, for the page to fill when that person downloads it. Every request its lookups send to the database goes through a read-only gate (`readOnlyFetch`): it cannot write, delete or call another service. Also the modes `warm` (keep-alive while in use), `briefing` (the Dashboard's weekly card) and `user_guide` (the team's guide). See "Assistant" above. Secrets `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `CRON_SECRET`; `verify_jwt` off; its header lists the shared files to upload with it.
- `odoo-push` — the one function that WRITES to Odoo: a product's UPC, shipping box (and its volume in m³) and weight, product weight, general title, model name, brand, category, color, material, finish type, HS code, country of origin and main image (and, on products the PIM created there, its name) when they are saved in the PIM — adding to Odoo's lists the colors, materials and finishes they lack —, the creation of a new product, the hourly retry of what waits for Odoo, and since 2026-11-22 the hourly pass over every product (`sync`) with the "Managed by PIM" mark on each connected one, behind the Settings → Odoo switch (see "What the PIM owns goes to Odoo the moment it is saved" under Data model conventions).
- `odoo-stock` — one Odoo warehouse's stock for a list of SKUs, read only, the same numbers as Inventory → Reporting → Stock with that warehouse selected (on hand, free, incoming, forecasted; body `{ skus, warehouse }`, default `ATL`). It needs the context `warehouse_id` plus `allowed_company_ids` of the warehouse's company: `warehouse` alone gives other numbers. A signed-in session or a service-role bearer; `verify_jwt` off. Uses the `ODOO_*` secrets. Lowe's Freight forecast reads it.
- `admin-users` — user CRUD with service_role (caller must be an authenticated admin). A person's own password never goes through it (Settings → Security and the reset email are browser → Supabase Auth); its `resetPassword` also clears the note that the password is the person's own, so the reminder returns (deployed 2026-11-23, v33; see Passwords).
- `wayfair-*` — Wayfair API integration (pull-groups, push-content, push-attributes). `wayfair-add-products` (the New listing card): MSRP = MAP = the market's MAP Blue (user, 2026-11-06: `map_usd` / `map_cad`, as in Wayfair's price change files, and the Wayfair Canada Excel new listing the same with `map_cad`, its Base Cost `cost_usd_wayfair_ca` like the API; the PIM's MSRP columns do not travel); a number question (DECIMAL / INTEGER) never takes "Does Not Apply", it stays unanswered (Faucet Hole Diameter on a kitchen sink refused it and blocked Create on every one). One language per listing (user, 2026-11-06: Wayfair takes at most 10 documents, C234L went with 13): one PDF per document type, EN-FR, else English, else EN-ES, never French alone (`wayfair-push-content` sends documents to listed products the same way, and videos: the ones without language plus one language in that order); images one set (`IMAGE_SETS`, the push's order) plus the ones without language. The card speaks plainly (user, 2026-11-06; [wayfairMessages.js](src/features/syndication/lib/wayfairMessages.js)): a sentence saying what happened and what to do, Wayfair's repeated lines once, no attribute codes; its raw statuses stay in the line's tooltip. Check status also follows a Validate, and a request already sent cannot be sent again from the same answer.
  - **The attribute push never offers to change a value that was right** (Vladimir, 2026-11-26, about the "Push <SKU> to Wayfair Canada" dialog: C132 "Standard Kitchen Sink → Kitchen Sink Workstation — eso no es cierto", and S-642WK "Overall Shape: Rectangular → Rectangle — where does this rectangle come from?"). The rules are [_shared/wayfairAttributes.ts](supabase/functions/_shared/wayfairAttributes.ts), shared by `wayfair-push-attributes` and `wayfair-add-products`.
    - **A workstation sink says so** (`isWorkstation`): its Product Type, its title or its model name says "workstation", or the product page's Workstation switch is on — the reading Rona's file already had. An accessory alone does not make one: until that day a drying rack, a colander or a cutting board in the Includes list was enough, so C132 and C233 (Azuni dual-mount sinks sold with a roll-up drying rack) came out "Kitchen Sink Workstation" against Wayfair's "Standard Kitchen Sink", and the outdoor S-520C / C1 / C2 "Yes" against its "No". The switch OFF is no answer by itself: nine sinks named Workstation have it off, and 57 more have never had it set. On every other sink the two readings give the same answer. Only a sink is one. Amazon's titles keep their own rule (a rack, a colander, a board or a lid in the accessories: Jessica's, 2026-10-09), under which C132's Item Name still says Workstation — told to the user, not changed.
    - **A shape Wayfair writes two ways is left as the listing has it.** Wayfair's Overall Shape list (the Valid Values of its Product Addition templates, kitchen sinks 628 and bathroom sinks 588: 80 entries) has both "Rectangle" and "Rectangular", "Circle", "Circular" and "Round". `shapeForms(word)` gives the forms of a shape, the PIM's own word first; the push keeps the word the listing holds when it names the PIM's shape (Overall Shape and Sink Shape), else sends the PIM's own word where the class's list has it, else the form the list has; a new listing (`snap`) and the Excel file (`SHAPE_ALIAS` lost its rectangular → Rectangle) do the same. Until then a sink's Overall Shape went out as the noun whatever the listing held: 16 kitchen sinks and 4 bathroom sinks that hold "Rectangular" were offered a change to "Rectangle", while 91 kitchen sinks hold "Rectangle" and are not touched either. Both shape rules read the product page's field first (`attributes.sink_shape`): the old `products.shape` column is a leftover, and S-831WN's "Rectangle" there was being sent over Wayfair's "Rectangular".
    - **A cold water tap is "Cold Water Dispensers"** (`productType`, category `cold_water_tap`; found in the same review): read as any kitchen faucet it came out "Single Handle Kitchen Faucet", and the push offered to change the three K-147 listings, which hold Wayfair's own "Cold Water Dispensers". K-142G / N / S hold "Single Handle Kitchen Faucet" on Wayfair: the push now offers to change those three — said to the user.
    - **A sink's deck thickness is the PIM's number** (user, 2026-11-27, about S-131W's push: "dice que el maximum thickness no aplica, pero sí lo tiene y es 1.5 en PIM"). On Wayfair's Kitchen Sinks class the attribute is "Maximum Thickness - Deck", and its rule answered "Does Not Apply" for every sink, whatever the PIM held (it sat among the questions about a faucet sold with the sink): the push offered "1.5 → Does Not Apply" on the three listings that held the number (S-131W, S-300TG, S-306TG, both suppliers), while the other listings hold "Does Not Apply" (whether an earlier push wrote it there was not looked into). The rule now sends the product page's Max Deck Thickness, on a sink as on a faucet, and says nothing where the PIM has none (Wayfair's answer stays).
      - **What it changes, measured that day** with the deployed function's dry run on the 312 sink listings (it reads and changes nothing) and the rules before and after on all 363 products (119,427 answers: only this attribute on the sinks moves): all 126 kitchen-type sinks hold 1.5 in the PIM. The three listings are no longer offered a change; **the 104 Wayfair Canada and 108 Wayfair USA listings that hold "Does Not Apply" are now offered "Does Not Apply → 1.5"** — the PIM's number, which goes out when each is pushed. The simulation gave the deployed function's own answer on 218 of 218 listings with the old rule.
      - **Not touched by it:** the Bathroom Sinks class has no such attribute (the 34 bathroom sinks have no number in the PIM either); 13 Wayfair Canada and 9 Wayfair USA kitchen sink listings do not expose it at all (blank on Wayfair, and it is no question of the class, so the push cannot fill it: the four new Azuni sinks, S-307XG, S-425T, S-625W and the S-7xxWNK); the Utility Sinks class calls it "Maximum Deck Thickness", which already sent the number (S-320T: "Does Not Apply → 1.5" before and after). Wayfair's kitchen sinks Excel template has no such column.
    - **What the review left as it is, said to him:** kitchen faucets "Single Handle Kitchen Faucet" → "Pull-Down Faucet" (26 listings; both are on Wayfair's list and the PIM's faucets are pull-downs), "Bar Faucet" → "Pull-Down Faucet" (K-131CR / NR, K-141 G / NG / NS: pull-down kitchen faucets), the ten bar / prep sinks "Standard Kitchen Sink" → "Prep Sink", and the bathroom faucets' Overall Shape (30 listings: Wayfair's "Unavailable" or another word against the PIM's own Overall Shape field). "Kitchen Sink Workstation: No → Yes" stays on the 14 sinks whose own name says Workstation.
    - **Checked that day, Wayfair never written.** The deployed function's dry run (it reads and changes nothing) on the 116 Wayfair Canada listings whose audit named one of those fields; the rules before and after on all 378 products (58,968 answers: only Overall Shape's word on the sinks, the 5 sinks' workstation, C132 / C233's Product Type, the 6 cold water taps and S-831WN's Sink Shape move); the function's own file run on the dev machine as a dry run with Wayfair answered there from that morning's readings (31 changes gone — C132 and C233's two each, the three S-520C, the three K-147, the 20 "Rectangular" and S-831WN's Sink Shape —, none new) and 11 made-up states of a listing (a blank Overall Shape takes "Rectangular", or "Rectangle" where the list has only that; another shape is still changed; a list that cannot be read; a cold water tap either way; a sink named Workstation with its switch off is still one). **Deployed the same day at the user's word** ("Despliega las dos funciones, commit y push": `wayfair-push-attributes` **v59** and `wayfair-add-products` **v49**, 4 files each, `verify_jwt` on as it was; their deployed bundles were read first and already held every earlier change of those files, so the deploy carried only this one). After it, the dry run through the deployed function: C132 and C233 Product Type and workstation "same", S-642WK, S-831WN, S-828WH and P-205 Overall Shape "same" at Wayfair's "Rectangular", K-147G "same", K-142G "Single Handle Kitchen Faucet → Cold Water Dispensers", S-520C "same"; the USA supplier's C132 and S-642WK the same way. `wayfair-audit` calls the push function, so the next audit's snapshot no longer counts those fields. **Not done:** a real push from the dialog after the deploy, and a new listing with a blank Overall Shape.
- `channel-deals` — the same for the marketplaces the PIM is connected to: a deal's price on Best Buy Canada (the offer's discount), Walmart Canada and Walmart USA (a promotion carved out of what the product holds) the day it starts, and back when it ends (see "Deals on Best Buy and Walmart" under Data model conventions). **Live since 2026-11-13: cron `channel-deals`, every 10 minutes** (v3: a run with nothing new only reads the database — no call to Best Buy or Walmart; v6: a change it sees on a channel is followed by that channel's snapshot, so Price Alignment does not wait for the next daily one). Its header lists the shared files to upload with it: `promoCalendar.ts`, `serviceRole.ts`, `exclusions.ts`, `bestbuyDiscount.ts`, `walmartPromoPlan.js`, `priceAlignmentReport.js`, `pageStores.js`, `homeDepotCaNlp.js`, `priceTolerance.js`.
- `sinksdirect-deals` — puts the price of a flash deal, MK event or peak season event on the Sinks Direct stores the day it starts and takes it back the day after it ends; every 5 minutes, and from the Pricing page when a deal is created or changed (see "A flash deal, MK event or peak season event made for a Sinks Direct store goes on the store by itself" under Data model conventions). Deployed with its shared files: `promoCalendar.ts`, `serviceRole.ts`, `wixSites.ts`, `priceAlignmentReport.js`, `pageStores.js`, `homeDepotCaNlp.js`, `priceTolerance.js`.
- `wix-*` — Wix catalog sync (import/list/pull/push/read). A `wix-push-product` call with `only: [...]` touches only those Wix keys — Sinks Direct Canada's collections are re-synced only when `only` names `collectionIds` (v42, 2026-10-08; price-only and documents-only pushes used to re-sync them from the PIM). On Sinks Direct USA and Azuni Canada (promo-aware, no PIM sale fields) the PIM's price is the final selling price, so every price push also resets Wix's discount to 0 (`wix-push-product`, also with `only: ["priceData"]`) — on Oct 1 2026 September's discounts left on the site stacked on October's prices (61 products).
- `promo-apply` — the monthly promotion automation (pg_cron daily 04:05/05:05 UTC): Sinks Direct USA on the 1st, Sinks Direct Canada on the first Thursday, Best Buy scheduled the day before Canada's window, and since 2026-09-28 Walmart Canada / Walmart USA scheduled the day before their window opens (safety net on the boundary day). **Walmart Canada decides by itself whether a promo shows as a reduction** (strikethrough, "was" price): every November 2026 promo, sent with promotionType "Reduced", was kept as a BASE price (`priceDisplayCodes.isStrikethrough` false, no comparison price) while October's showed the strikethrough; re-created after October had ended they came back REDUCED, and an hour later Walmart turned them back to BASE (2026-10-22, after three rounds of delete + create that each left the products minutes at MAP). Neither the timing nor the feed changes it — likely Walmart's reference-price rule after a month at the promo price; do not churn promos to force it. Read a SKU's live promo with walmart-push-promo `{ mode: "promo", market: "ca", sku }` (reads go stale during bursts — two agreeing reads); its `delete` mode also finds BASE-kept promos. Switches in `app_settings.promo_automation` (`enabled`, `wix`, `bestbuy`, `walmart_ca`, `walmart_us`), Settings page "Run now" reconciles. A market also goes back to regular prices the day after its window closes when no calendar boundary falls that day (custom dates — Oct 2026: Canada closes Oct 22, its calendar turns Nov 5), and a promotion is marked ended only once BOTH markets are over (Canada can close first; the USA pass of the next boundary needs the promotion on the board). **It ends the day after the window that closes LAST, whichever market that is** (user, 2026-11-11: "cuando culmine la fecha más tardía, debería pasarse a ended"; v43): until then only the Canada pass ended promotions, so October 2026 — Canada closed Oct 21, the USA Oct 31 — stood "active" from Nov 1 and would have until the next Canada pass (Dec 3). `overBoth` (active, not a target, both windows before today) is ended by `endPromotions`: in the Canada pass as before, after the passes once nothing is owed to Wix, and on a day without a boundary (a promotion left open by an earlier run is closed there, with its own Activity Log line). Only the status changes — never use the card's "End promotion" on a monthly promotion whose days are over: it first clears the sale price of every product on its list (118 of October's 139 were on sale for November that day). A dry run accepts `{ dryRun: true, cron: true, asOf: "YYYY-MM-DD" }` to show what the nightly run will do on a given day — use it before every boundary. Flash deals / special events are never automated by it: the Sinks Direct stores take them through `sinksdirect-deals` and the Stylish stores through `stylish-event-discounts` (both in Data model conventions); every other portal is by hand. Wix is pushed at a pace (since 2026-09-29: Wix lets ~30 changes through, then answers "Retry after ~56000ms"; in September that left most prices unchanged while the market was stamped): 2 at a time, a block pauses until Wix's time, a run pushes for ~70 s (each change capped at 25 s; the runtime cuts a run at ~150 s — the Oct 1 2026 launch died twice at the old 105 s budget with nothing applied) and queues its continuation run (`{chain, reconcile}`) through Postgres — `promo_apply_continue()` ([20261001_promo_apply_continue.sql](supabase/migrations/20261001_promo_apply_continue.sql), pg_net with the nightly job's headers), because Supabase refuses a function calling itself within the same trace ("Rate limit exceeded for trace"). The changes done today sit in `app_settings.promo_apply_wix_progress`, saved after EVERY change so a cut-short run keeps its work, and `us_/ca_applied_at` is stamped only once nothing is owed to Wix. After a boundary, check `us_/ca_applied_at` and that progress: a lock left in `lock_until` with nothing stamped means the run died.
- `walmart-push-promo` — one market per call (`market: "ca" | "us"`): Canada = feed `PRICE_AND_PROMOTION` (Canada payload 5.0, `WM_MARKET: ca`), USA = **one `PUT /v3/price?promo=true` per product** (REDUCED promo price against the BASE MAP USD, `UPSERT`; each answered at once, stamped only when Walmart took at least one; `sandbox: true` uses the `WALMART_US_SANDBOX_*` pair and skips the listing filter). The bulk `promo` feed (raw-JSON PromotionalPriceFeed 1.5) had only passed the sandbox, which answers anything: November 2026's first real send (2026-10-31 04:05 UTC) came back feedStatus ERROR, 0 items, no reason (Walmart documents that feed as a multipart file and publishes no item schema). Sent again per product the same afternoon, 32 / 32 taken, read back on Walmart (`mode: "promo"`). A feed rejected whole now shows its raw answer in `status`. Modes push (dryRun returns the lines + payload), status, promo, and delete (Canada: takes a promotion's promo off `skus` that left its Canada list — reads each SKU's live promo and deletes only the one ending with that window; the import dialog calls it through `syncWalmartCaList`, which also pushes SKUs that joined). A whole-promotion push stamps `promotions.wm_<market>_scheduled_at` / `wm_<market>_schedule`. Functions that import `../_shared/*` are deployed through the Management API by uploading the shared files too (`entrypoint_path` `supabase/functions/<name>/index.ts`). `_shared/serviceRole.ts` proves a service-role bearer via GoTrue admin (the runtime's `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` is not the `.env` key).
- Best Buy discounts (`bestbuy-push-price`, promo-apply's `scheduleBestBuy`) go as Mirakl's NESTED `discount: { start_date, end_date, ranges }` built by [_shared/bestbuyDiscount.ts](supabase/functions/_shared/bestbuyDiscount.ts) (ET instants). The flat CSV names (`discount_price`, `discount_ranges`, `discount_start_date`…) are ignored by the JSON OF24 import, and since it runs in NORMAL mode each such line CLEARED the offer's discount — no PIM promotion reached Best Buy before 2026-09-30, while the imports reported success. An offer holds ONE discount: price-only lines carry the live one, `protect_running` keeps another promotion's running discount, `clear_discount: {start, end}` drops one that began in that window. Verify a send by reading the offers (`discount`), never by the import counts.
- `walmart-add-items` — Walmart USA listings, one product at a time from the product's Walmart USA card. `setup: "match"` (default on the card) = an OFFER on the listing Walmart's catalog already has for the UPC (feed `MP_ITEM_MATCH`, spec 4.2 from Walmart's documented sample: SKU, UPC, price = MAP Blue USD, shipping weight, condition) — most Stylish products are there from Walmart Canada without offers, and the seller API's catalog search can't see them. Otherwise a NEW item with full PIM content (`MP_ITEM` spec 5.0, Sinks only, validated against Get Spec); a new item on an existing UPC fails with ERR_PDI_0001 (found 2026-09-28 on S-414T). Production submits need `confirm: "CREATE"`; read-only modes `spec`, `account` (ship node 10003231796) and `get`. **Walmart USA goes in lots** (user, 2026-10-30: "queremos todos, pero podemos empezar por lotes"): the Walmart USA page's "Add products in lots" ([WalmartBulkAdditionCard](src/features/syndication/components/WalmartBulkAdditionCard.jsx)) lists every product not on Walmart USA yet, USA stock first; a lot (≤ 100) is checked and sent as offers by match with `enableChannel: true`, which also switches Walmart USA on for the products sent (the 2026-09-22 exclusions had switched off 344 of 378). A UPC Walmart's catalog does not have ("We couldn't find an available listing for the provided GTIN" — lot 1: S-127W, C132, S-601WK, S-628WK) has no listing to join: "Create as new items" checks those against Walmart's item spec and creates them with the PIM's content (MP_ITEM: Sinks, and since 2026-10-30 Faucets — Walmart's one product type for kitchen faucets, combos, cold water taps (Drinking Water Faucets), pot fillers and bathroom faucets, `faucet_type`; spout type, handles, drillings, hole, heights, reach, GPM and features from the PIM's faucet fields). Accessories since the same day, by what they are (`ACCESSORY_TYPES`, from the model name / title): basket strainers and pop-up drain caps = Drain Stoppers, cutting boards = Cutting Boards & Butcher Blocks, drying racks / dish racks / organizers = Dish Racks (height = the PIM's height or depth; state chemical disclosure "None"); an overflow ring (H-03) fits no Walmart type. A set of N ("-N" SKU or "Set of N" title) sends net content N, multipack quantity N, count per pack 1, count N. Lot 1 (2026-10-30): 30 kitchen sinks, 26 joined their listing; lot 2: 36, 30 joined. The card reads Walmart's live item list when it opens, and since a product Walmart accepted can take an hour to show there, every production submit is remembered in `app_settings.walmart_us_lots` (last 40 feeds): the card restores the week's lots and Walmart's answers, hides what was already sent ("Hide the N already sent"), never selects it again, and keeps the "not listed" ones for Create as new items after a reload. The listings keep Walmart's content until the brand is approved on Walmart's Brand Portal (the user took it over from Jessica, 2026-10-30); then the PIM's content goes with setup `maintenance`.
- `walmart-us-inventory` — Walmart USA stock (user, 2026-10-30). **Its stock sending is ON again since 2026-11-11** (user: "SI QUE LO CUBRA PIM" — it had been off since 2026-11-10, "todo lo va a sincronizar Odoo", but Odoo's Walmart sync was still off, so nobody sent; it goes off again when Odoo covers Walmart, on the user's word; the first send that day: 176 items, all taken); every hourly run also keeps our Walmart.com links (below). What it sends while on: every SKU listed on Walmart USA gets our USA stock (`product_inventory` 'us') **minus 2 units**, never below 0; a SKU we do not track goes as 0 and a product without stock stays listed at 0. One bulk feed `inventory` (spec 1.4, the default ship node — the only one, 10003231796 Flowery Branch) per run, with the SKUs whose quantity changed and every SKU once a day; the next run reads the last feed's outcome. Cron `walmart-us-inventory-hourly` (:35, after inventory-pull) runs it, and it sends only while the switch on the Walmart USA page's Stock sync card is on (`app_settings.walmart_us_inventory.enabled`; modes `status`, `switch`, `push` with `dryRun` / `all`). Deployed with `verify_jwt` off (x-cron-secret, service role or an admin / editor). **Every run also keeps our Walmart.com links** (user, 2026-10-30): each published Stylish product's `product_where_to_buy` walmart_us link becomes `https://www.walmart.com/ip/<itemId>` of OUR listing (source `auto`; the old links were other sellers' listings, 235 broken), and the changed products are queued for the Stylish pages; mode `links` (with `dryRun`) does only that, whatever the stock switch. 93 links were pointed at our listings that day.
- `where-to-buy-audit` — the WHERE TO BUY link check of the Stylish sites (Listing Health → Where to Buy). Since 2026-10-19 (user decision, after testing every store) the stores that block the server are opened through **Bright Data Web Unlocker** (primary, pay per success, zone `web_unlocker1`, secrets `BRIGHTDATA_API_TOKEN` / `BRIGHTDATA_ZONE`) and **ScraperAPI** (backup, `SCRAPERAPI_KEY`; Hobby plan since 2026-10-29 — 100,000 credits a month, up to 10 requests at a time, Overstock goes to it first); `ROUTES` in the function says which first per retailer (Overstock = Akamai: ScraperAPI first, standard proxies, 10 credits a page; BB&B Bright Data first, then ScraperAPI's ultra premium — its standard proxies stopped opening BB&B, tested 2026-10-29, `SCRAPERAPI_OPTIONS`; Rona only Bright Data; Menards and Lowe's through Bright Data's ready-made **scrapers** (`SCRAPERS`, Web Scraper API: Menards `gd_motp6b7s1n9zlq6lwj` "Menards Products", Lowe's `gd_lnvl79pfftqh18u2o` "Lowes.com" since Lowe's put a captcha in front of the Web Unlocker on 2026-10-20 — 70 links stuck on "captcha or protection page found", with or without a US exit; one snapshot job per store and batch, read on a later pass; a live page returns its model (`mpn` / `model_number`), UPC and price; a gone one `error_code: dead_page` is only a SOFT sign since 2026-11-03 (Menards 4 times 6 h apart, `SOFT_RULE`) — that day Menards' scraper had answered dead_page for P-203's LIVE page (Model P-203, $82 in the user's browser) and 78 Menards links of the 11-02 pass had left both Stylish pages at once; P-203 was set back to OK by hand and the other 77 rechecked — the Web Unlocker also gave up on some live Menards pages). Bright Data's own BB&B and Overstock scrapers were blocked too (captcha / "page didn't load") and the Rona / Menards.com entries in its list are data sets that take no links. The Loblaw sites defeat both: verdict `manual`. An address is opened once for every row that has it ([20261019_where_to_buy_unlockers.sql](supabase/migrations/20261019_where_to_buy_unlockers.sql): `next_check_at`, `checked_via`, `fail_count`, `signal_count`, `job_id`). **Rhythm (user, 2026-10-26: "lunes, miércoles y viernes a partir de la 1am hora Canadá Cambridge"): every link is checked on Monday, Wednesday and Friday from 1:00 AM Eastern** — an answered link is due again at the next pass (`nextPassAt`, DST-safe in America/Toronto; the cron still fires every 10 minutes and finds nothing due on the other days), a link without an answer is retried after 3 h, 6 h… within that day, else at the next pass (`onPass`), soft signs too. **BB&B and Overstock only on Monday and Friday** (user, 2026-10-31: "2 por semana"; `PASS_WEEKDAYS_FOR`): the Friday 10-30 pass used ~16,000 ScraperAPI credits — BB&B ~5,700 (ultra premium, 30 a page), Overstock ~2,200, and ~5,800 as Bright Data's first backup — so the 100,000-credit plan would have run out ~Nov 10. Where Bright Data opens the store, **ScraperAPI waits until a link has missed twice** (`SCRAPERAPI_AFTER_FAILS`; BB&B, Overstock and eBay keep it from the first try). **When Bright Data fails on BB&B the link is tried again in 10 minutes, starting with ScraperAPI** (user, 2026-11-09: since 5 AM that day BB&B had turned Bright Data away — "captcha or protection page found" — and 32 good addresses sat waiting 3 hours; a call has no time left for ScraperAPI after Bright Data's 15–47 s failure). **The credits are paced to last exactly to the renewal** (user, 2026-11-02: "que cumplamos el mes justos, no sobrados" — the Monday 11-02 pass of BB&B + Overstock took ~10,000, with 69,265 left for eight passes): each Eastern day's first call fixes its allowance in `app_settings.scraperapi_budget` — a BB&B / Overstock pass day gets (credits left − `SCRAPERAPI_RESERVE` 1,500) ÷ the passes left before `nextBillingDate` (that one included), any other day `SCRAPERAPI_OFF_DAY` 500 (new links, retries) — and once it is spent the day's other links keep their verdict and wait for the next pass ("ScraperAPI's share for today is used"); each pass shares again what is really left, so the month ends near zero. Read it with mode `budget` (it reads the account, spends nothing; the function answers only the cron's call — copy the check job's command). Two exceptions (user's choice the same day): a link never verified yet (new or changed — the product tab, the Excel import, a used suggestion) is checked right away and retried on any day, and the promotion re-reads run whatever the day. Suggestion candidates are opened on the check days only. A full pass is ~5,800 addresses (11,000 rows; ~3,100–3,600 billed by Bright Data ≈ USD 5) and takes ~4–5 hours; ~13 passes a month ≈ USD 60–70. Before (2026-10-20, ~55 USD/month): OK every 3 days, broken every 2. Pace: 20 Bright Data pages at a time per call (`BD_MAX_IN_FLIGHT`, each store at most 2 lanes); a batch takes at most 10 unlocker rows / 20 direct rows of one host (`capPerHost`) and 50 direct rows in all (`DIRECT_BATCH`; the cron chains up to 5 more calls, `MAX_CHAIN`), and the probe downloads only the head of a page (`readHead`, 600 KB, Amazon 3 MB — the readers find the product in the first ~300 KB, Sinks Direct's Wix pages are 3 MB): with 250 direct rows a call and whole pages, every check call died of WORKER_RESOURCE_LIMIT from 2026-10-21 20:41 to 10-22 10:00 ET, once Sinks Direct's ~850 links came due together three days after their first pass (the morning's promotion re-read waited until then), so a store's backlog (a dealer's first pass, BB&B / Overstock waiting) never fills a batch since the account's verification lifted "Your account exceeded the allowed rate limits" (tested 2026-10-20: 60 at once to one store drew only 7 per-site "global adaptive rate limit" answers); a check call's `brightDataLimited` counts the rate-limit answers it got (each pauses its lanes 20 s). The zone keeps the user's 10 USD/day cap (`disable_notify`). A 404 / 410 or a not-found page is Broken at once; weaker signs need repeated answers (`SOFT_RULE`): a page that opens without the brand / SKU (title, product data or text) twice on different days; Menards' unlocker giving up FOUR times 6 h apart — it also gives up on some live pages (B-108G, checked by hand 2026-10-20), while a dead product (sent to Menards' error page) fails every time. Walmart and Amazon answer the server with bot pages dressed as normal ones ("Robot or human?", "Verify Your Identity", Amazon's captcha): a direct OK from them must show the product, else Bright Data tries (`BOT_PRONE`). The panel shows OK / Broken; `missing` is the "Links to add" KPI, not a problem. **Walmart USA is not covered yet** (user, 2026-10-29: "seguimos trabajando en ello"; `NOT_COVERED` in src/features/dashboard/lib/whereToBuy.js): its links are still checked and a broken one still leaves the websites, but they are no problem in the panel (a note gives their count), no link to add, and only on the Excel's All links. **Both copies of a link** (the same address on the Canada and USA pages) are settled by one answer: a batch also takes the non-due rows of its addresses (`siblingsOf`, audit v71) — they had drifted apart (USA OK, Canada "Not checked yet"). **A link keeps its last verdict until a new answer replaces it** (audit v72, `priorState`): the daily scan used to drop an OK / Broken older than a week, so links a store kept turning away that long went back to "Not checked yet" without their readings (48 BB&B links on 2026-10-30). **A site's rows are replaced in ONE transaction** (`where_to_buy_replace_rows`, [20261110_where_to_buy_replace_rows.sql](supabase/migrations/20261110_where_to_buy_replace_rows.sql), used by the scan and by `sync`): on 2026-11-10 the scan deleted Stylish Canada's rows and its insert was then refused (rows carried from earlier ones had `review_count`, new ones did not — PostgREST takes a batch only when every row has the same keys, now `sameKeys`), so the site stood empty from 4:20 to 8:29 AM Eastern until the scan ran again (the state came back from the USA copies: same SKU + address). Now a refused insert undoes the delete, a scan that would leave a site under half its rows is refused, and one site failing no longer stops the others. Test one product with `{ mode: "check", skus: ["S-611W"] }`. The company is leaving Price2Spy: do not build on its reports.
  - **The page a check opens is also READ** (user, 2026-10-20: "que apunte al producto correcto por SKU" / "traer el precio para el price alignment"), at no extra cost: [_shared/pageProduct.js](supabase/functions/_shared/pageProduct.js) with one reader per store family in [_shared/pageReaders/](supabase/functions/_shared/pageReaders/) (Wayfair's Next.js flight, Home Depot USA's Apollo state, Home Depot Canada's `hdca-state`, Best Buy's `__INITIAL_STATE__`, Amazon's product-details rows and price block, Lowe's `__PRELOADED_STATE__`, Rona's `js-product-ddo`, Walmart's `__NEXT_DATA__`, BB&B / Overstock's Next.js data, the dealers' Shopify / X-Cart / 3dcart / Wix / eBay data; Menards from its scraper record) — each scoped to the MAIN product, never carousels or included accessories, written and tested on the stores' real pages that day. A deploy uploads `pageProduct.js` and every `pageReaders/*.js` with the function. Columns on `where_to_buy_links` ([20261020_where_to_buy_page_reads.sql](supabase/migrations/20261020_where_to_buy_page_reads.sql)): `page_model`, `page_item_id`, `sku_check`, `page_note` (a coupon or cart discount, open box, price only in the cart, a link that selects no option), `price`, `was_price`, `currency`, `in_stock`, `read_at` — carried across scans with the rest of the link's state (`priorState` is kept by SKU + address; another SKU's reading of the same address drops its SKU check).
  - **`skuCheck` per row** (several SKUs can share an address): `match` (the page names the SKU, a store suffix — Lowe's "S-310G1" —, the family model a dealer writes without its finish — "A-801" —, or its UPC); `listing` (user, 2026-10-20: the Wayfair.ca "Wrong product" links "realmente están bien"): a Wayfair link WITHOUT `?piid=` on a listing of two or more choices (`choiceCount`) whose default is not of another known family — Wayfair's server preselects the listing's DEFAULT option (part #, photo, price: A-801B's link opens TKJS1134 on A-801N Matte Black, Brushed Nickel one click away), so the shopper is on the right listing: stays OK and on the websites, shown as **Other finish**, its price left out of Price Alignment (the default's), its suggestion (the option's own `?piid=` address) reason `other_finish` ([20261020_where_to_buy_other_finish.sql](supabase/migrations/20261020_where_to_buy_other_finish.sql); 224 readings of 112 products moved that day). Wayfair also groups different models as options of one listing (sizes, gauges: S-742WNK Onyxo42 on Onyxo36's, S-200T Jaspel on Olivine's), so where a family number is missing a bare link reads `listing`; S-622W's Wayfair.ca link stays Wrong product (families 15 / 13) although its 22-inch option is on that listing. `variant` (another PIM SKU of the SAME family — A-801N for A-801B, P-205-2 for P-205 —, ours among the listing's options, or Wayfair's default when the link's `?piid=` is not on the listing — stale, `optionStale`, its own note —, the listing has one option, or the default is of another known family — K-152 Fano on K-151 Alba's listing): stays OK, shown as **Wrong product** for the link to be fixed; `other` (a PIM product of another family — KB Authority's links of 2026-10-20 opened B-122G for B-108G, a faucet combo for a drain): a soft sign, twice two days apart → Broken "Opens another product" (leaves the websites); `unknown` (no model the PIM can place). An OK page whose store reader finds no product (a brand, category or search page: Wayfair's STYLISH brand page for A-908, KB Authority's sink category for S-306TG) is the same soft sign, unless most pages of that store in the call read as nothing (`unreadableStores`, a store that changed its pages); Lowe's "no longer sold" listings (a live page with the product) are Broken at once (`gone`).
  - Amazon CA / USA go through **Bright Data first** (2026-10-20): the server's own probe gets Amazon pages without the price (Amazon.ca: a US address, no buy box) — ~10 USD/month more; when the probe settles one anyway its reading keeps the last price (`PRICE_BLIND`). The probe sends a browser's headers (sec-ch-ua, Sec-Fetch-*): the Shopify dealers answered 429 without them. Build with Rise sells in USD only although it sits under Canada. Vatero's Shopify store converts its prices to the visitor's currency (USD from the server's US address: 161 of 167 readings, all left out of Price Alignment), so the audit asks it for Canada (`?country=CA`, `ASK_PARAMS` / `requestUrl` in `checkRow` — only the request; the link kept and published stays as it is; 2026-10-21).
  - **Guards against false Brokens** (review of 2026-10-20): a bot or denial page dressed as a normal one is no answer, never a sign — Amazon's captcha, Walmart's "Robot or human?", Lowe's Akamai page titled "www.lowes.com" ("Access to www.lowes.com was denied", `BLOCK_BODY_RE`; it had put five live Lowe's links one sign from Broken); an unlocker's "page without the product" waits until the route's next method answers; a page whose reader finds a product counts as a product page whatever its title (Wayfair keeps its home page's title on a listing moved to a new id); a page whose title names the SKU is never "a page without the product"; a store's blank readings count only while its reader read most of that store's pages of the last 3 days (`unreadableStores`). **Soft signs are spaced** (`signal_at`): one counts only two days after the last (Menards 6 h) — rechecking a product sooner changes nothing — and the count starts over after a week. Publishing hides a broken address per product (`brokenUrls` by SKU: an address can be Broken for one SKU and right for another, also in scripts/publish-where-to-buy.mjs). The SKU check's UPC is `attributes.upc` (the `products.upc` column is empty); a UPC two products share is ignored, and a Stylish SKU and its Azuni twin (A-906 / A906) are told apart by UPC, else only the exact spelling counts. The results are written six PATCHes at a time.
  - **Suggested links** (user, 2026-10-20: "1 hazlo" / "2 hazlo"): mode `suggest` (cron `where-to-buy-suggest`, every 10 minutes at :05; candidates looked for once an hour and opened on the check days; [20261020_where_to_buy_suggestions.sql](supabase/migrations/20261020_where_to_buy_suggestions.sql)) finds the right address for a link that opens the wrong product, a broken link, or a store without a link: another link of the store whose page showed the product (`other_link` — KB Authority's shifted links), an option a read page lists with its own address (`page_option` — the readers return `optionLinks`, kept as `page_options`: KBA / Shopify / Walmart / Best Buy / Menards by model, Home Depot CA and Amazon.ca by item id, BB&B / Overstock by sub-SKU, Wayfair / Amazon by finish name), or an id the PIM keeps (`store_id` — Best Buy's active-offer product id → `/en-ca/product/<id>`, the Home Depot USA alias → `/p/<id>`, the BB&B / Overstock alias → `/<id>/product.html`, Overstock with any category / slug before it; Rona's bare article # answers 404, Lowe's search a captcha and Home Depot CA timed out, so those ids only confirm). Each is OPENED before it shows: `ready` only when the page shows the SKU or its own store id; a page that lists it as an option moves the suggestion to that option once; an id-built address becomes the store's canonical one. Shown under the store's row of the product's Where to Buy tab ("Use" fills the field — Save and publish applies it with source `suggested`; "Dismiss" = never again) and in Listing Health → Where to Buy behind the "Suggested links" button next to Scan pages (a dialog: Use / Use all / Dismiss save and publish like the tab — a button, not a section, user 2026-10-20). The sync marks a used one `applied`; a failed candidate is retried after 14 days; test with `{ mode: "suggest", dryRun: true }`.
  - **The panel's status counters are its filters** (user, 2026-11-12: "que esos status de ok, broken, y eso sean como filtros clikeables para ver como tal cuáles son los broken"; Stylish and Azuni panels alike). OK, Broken, Wrong product, Out of stock, Other finish, Not checked yet and Check by hand are buttons (`WTB_STATUSES`; Products and Links stay plain totals, a counter at 0 is disabled). Pressed, a counter lists the products that have such a link, most of them first: in the grid those links stand out and the others fade, the last column counts them under the status's name instead of Issues, an opened row lists only those links ("Show all N" brings the rest), the bar says "Showing: Broken · Clear" with the products and links kept, and "Copy N SKUs" copies the list. Pressed again, or Clear, the usual list ("Only broken or wrong links"). It works with the retailer and SKU filters and stays when the site is switched (Canada / USA); a "Links to add" portal takes it off, and the reverse.
    - **One function decides which counter a link is under**, `linkBucket` in [whereToBuy.js](src/features/dashboard/lib/whereToBuy.js): `summarizeWhereToBuy` counts with it (`byStatus`) and the filter lists with it, so a counter and its list cannot disagree — a store not covered yet (Walmart USA) is in neither, as before. Checked that day on the live rows of the three sites: every counter the same as before the change, and each filter lists exactly what its counter counts.
  - **Export / Import links** (user, 2026-10-21: "un export … de cuáles están broken, missing, y así poder subirlos in bulk"; the PIM Excel's look): the panel's **Export** (every role) downloads `Stylish_Where_to_Buy_<date>.xlsx` — Summary (by status, by store), **To fix** (Broken + Wrong product), **Links to add**, **All links** (one row per product and store: SKU, model, category, UPC, store, **Store Code** (the product's own code there, to search the store with — Aliases' Home Depot / Lowe's / Rona / BB&B-Overstock ids, the Amazon.ca ASIN or Seller SKU, Best Buy's offer product id from the latest snapshot, the Wayfair part number from either Wayfair link; `storeCodes`), country, **Our Stock** (our `product_inventory` stock in the store's country, else "Not tracked"), **Priority**, the status tinted in its color, why, current link, **What to Do**, the green **New Link** column, what the page showed — model, price, was, currency, stock, who checked it — and a ready suggested link) and How to use.
