A person works the lists (user, 2026-10-29), so To fix and Links to add are sorted by Priority — 1 a suggested link is ready, 2 in stock with us, 3 our stock not tracked, 4 no stock with us (the store may have taken it down: search once and move on; sold-out pages are Out of stock and never listed) — with a count per priority on the Summary; **Walmart USA is not covered yet** (user: "seguimos trabajando en ello"), so its links are only on All links; Azuni products are left out. **Import links** (editors) reads the same file back ([whereToBuyExcel.js](src/features/dashboard/lib/whereToBuyExcel.js) `planWhereToBuyImport`, every visible sheet): only SKU + Store + New Link count — an address replaces or adds that store's link (it must be the store's own site, `wtbUrlProblem`), `REMOVE` takes it off, an empty cell keeps it, a row added at the end adds any store; a product and store set two ways in the file, an unknown SKU or store, and Azuni are refused and listed. The dialog shows the changes first; Save and publish upserts `product_where_to_buy` (source `import`), writes one Activity Log entry and publishes through the audit's `sync` mode, 50 products a call (Wix's overflow goes to the publish queue). `buildSectionedWorkbook` gained per-sheet `widths`, `emptyText` (an empty cell written as empty text, so a long link does not spill over the column to fill) and `cellColor` (single tinted cells). `cleanWtbUrl` also cuts Amazon's `/gp/product/<ASIN>` and `/gp/aw/d/<ASIN>` to `/dp/<ASIN>`.
  - **Menards' scraper returns one record per finish for each address** (all under the same input: K-142N's link also brings K-142S's record). The address's own record is the one with its URL / Menards id (`menardsOwnRecord`), the others are its options; taking the last record swapped the model and price of 22 pairs (K-142N ↔ K-142S) until the fix of 2026-10-20.
  - **A page's reviews are read too: how many, and the average rating** (user, 2026-11-16: "¿podemos leer todas las reviews de todas las páginas? solo necesito la cantidad de reviews y el rating global"; "Pues sí, hazlo"). `where_to_buy_links.review_count` (2026-11-09, BB&B and Overstock only until now) and `rating` (out of 5, [20261116_where_to_buy_rating.sql](supabase/migrations/20261116_where_to_buy_rating.sql)) come from the page the check already opened, at no extra cost: [_shared/pageReaders/reviews.js](supabase/functions/_shared/pageReaders/reviews.js) (`reviewsOf(html, retailer)`, joined to the store reader's answer in `readProductPage`; the scrapers' records through `reviewReading`).
    - **Where each store writes it** (one real product page of each, 2026-11-16): Wayfair the listing's review header, else the listing's own `reviews` object (a listing's reviews are those of all its options); Amazon the stars under the title; Walmart `__NEXT_DATA__`; Home Depot Canada its hidden schema block, else the page state's item; Rona its product record, else the rating summary the page draws; Lowe's its scraper record (`rating`, `reviews_count`); Best Buy, Home Depot USA, BB&B, Overstock, Sinks Direct and any other store the page's own top-level JSON-LD Product — never a product of a list or a carousel. Read that day: Amazon.ca 4.0 · 6, Amazon.com 4.7 · 6, Wayfair.ca 4.75 · 264, Wayfair.com 4.64 · 137, Home Depot Canada 4.84 · 98, Home Depot USA 4.9 · 63, Lowe's 4.9 · 83, Rona 4.86 · 66, Best Buy 4.86 · 65, Walmart Canada 4.86 · 65, Walmart USA 4.93 · 45, BB&B 4.4 · 9, Sinks Direct USA 4.9 · 46.
    - **Only a number the page writes is kept.** A store that draws no rating for a product nobody reviewed reads as nothing, never as 0 (BB&B and Overstock keep their 0 for a page without a rating block: the ScoreCard's "# Reviews" counts on it); the rating goes only with at least one review; Amazon and Walmart count ratings. A later read that does not say keeps the last numbers; a broken link drops them. **Not read:** Menards (its scraper's `star_rating` / `review_count` came empty on the one record looked at), the small dealers (their reviews are drawn by a widget after loading — Judge.me) and a page without reviews (Sinks Direct Canada has very few).
    - **Shown only when the page read is this product's** (`linkReviews` in [whereToBuy.js](src/features/dashboard/lib/whereToBuy.js): never from a link that opens another product or another option; an "Other finish" Wayfair page counts): under the store's name on the product's Where to Buy tab ("★ 4.8 · 98 reviews", `reviewsText`: one decimal, as the stores write it), in the panel's line of a link, and in the Where to Buy Excel — Rating and Reviews next to the page's price on the link sheets, a **Reviews** sheet (one row per product: every store together, the rating weighted by each store's reviews, then a band per store) and "Reviews by store" on the Summary. Import links reads nothing of the Reviews sheet.
    - **Deployed 2026-11-16 (v94, at the user's "Despliega"; auto mode had refused it first).** The numbers fill in at each store's next pass (Monday / Wednesday / Friday; BB&B and Overstock Monday and Friday): until a link is read again the table holds only BB&B's and Overstock's counts, without a rating. **Checked that day:** the reader on 19 real store pages saved that day and the two scraper records (13 stores read, every price as before the change), 20 cases of each store's second reading place and of the helpers, and — in a browser with a stand-in sign-in, live reads, and that day's readings of two products put into the answers — the tab, the Excel (opened: 6,171 rows, the Reviews sheet, the Summary block) and the import of that file (nothing to change), at 1440 / 390 in both themes: axe's only finding the top bar's account button, no sideways scroll, nothing written. **Run for real that day:** one check of S-203T through the deployed function (the check job's own command with its body changed, run inside Postgres): rating and reviews written for nine stores — Amazon.ca 4.0 · 6, Amazon.com 4.1 · 6, Best Buy 4.86 · 65, Home Depot Canada 4.84 · 98, Rona 4.86 · 66, Sinks Direct USA 4.9 · 71, Walmart Canada 4.86 · 65, Wayfair.ca and Wayfair.com 4.75 · 264 (one listing).
  - **Rona has a new page since 2026-11: its price is read from Rona's own item service** (found 2026-11-16 on the way; user, asked whether to look for a way to read it: "siii"). A Next.js page with no `js-product-ddo`, served to part of the visitors: the first empty reading is of 2026-11-13, and on 11-16 164 of Rona's 330 products had no price in Price Alignment's readings. The reader takes the product's own record from the new page (`ronaRecord` in reviews.js, `readRonaNext` in lowesRona.js: model #, article #, GTIN, reviews); **the price is not in that HTML** — it is drawn after loading, and only the carousels' prices are there (never read).
    - **The service** (found in the page's scripts — `API_ROUTES`, root `/v2/api`): `GET https://www.rona.ca/v2/api/services/item/availability/productId?id=<article #>&locale=en&storeId=<store>` → `price.finalPrice` (paid now), `price.basePrice` (the regular price, only while on sale), `inventory.isAvailable`. It wants a real store (none: 400; the site's own 10151, or an unknown article: 500): **RONA+ Cambridge, 83224** (`RONA_STORE` in lowesRona.js) — our products are online-exclusive parcel items, and B-101C read the same at Vancouver. ~8 s through the Web Unlocker; a plain browser is turned away (DataDome), and even Bright Data's browser never drew the price.
    - **The check asks it in the link's own turn** (`viaBrightData`: `ronaServiceUrl(page)` → one more Bright Data request, twice at most → `withRonaService`), only for a reading of the new page without a price. A call with no time left for it leaves the link for the next one (5 minutes) instead of keeping a reading without a price; without an answer the reading keeps no price and its note says so, until the next pass. One more request per such link (~0.5 USD a pass at most). An old-layout page still gives its price itself.
    - **Checked that day:** the service against what the OLD page had read the same morning — A-04 82, A-801C 19 (sold out on both), K130SK147S 367, ST-06 41, B-101C 290 was 308 —; 11 cases of the join on the real page and the real answers; and for real through the deployed function (v95): S-203T's Rona row went from no price to 194, was 203, in stock. The 327 rows (164 products) without a price were then made due (`next_check_at = now()`) for the check's cron to read again that afternoon; the first hour left 11 products without a price because their page was read at the end of a call — hence the 5-minute retry (v97). By 11 AM Eastern 287 of the 330 products had a price (166 that morning) and 105 a rating; 36 products had not been opened yet (the unlocker gave no answer for their page: tried again that afternoon by the check's own rule) and 7 kept no price after the service was asked twice (made due once more).
  - **HomeDepot.com's stock is read from its buybox as it is drawn** (user, 2026-11-23: S-301G stood OK on the product's Where to Buy tab while Home Depot's page said "Out of Stock" in large type — "debería ser fuera de inventario … y gracias a ese estatus se hace la automatización hacia la sección de Where to Buy de Wix"). Until that day no HomeDepot.com link had ever been read with its stock: 124 products stood OK with their stock unknown, and 13 Out of stock only because their page said discontinued or not available. The page's own data (the Apollo state, the JSON-LD) never says it — the browser asks Home Depot for it after loading — but Bright Data's copy is the page as drawn and carries the buybox: `buyboxStockUs` in [homeDepot.js](supabase/functions/_shared/pageReaders/homeDepot.js). The Add to Cart button (component `add-to-cart:AddToCart`) = in stock; the "Out of Stock" heading in its place (`data-automation-id="fulfillment__out-of-stock"`, component `fulfillment:FulfillmentNotifyMe`) = sold out. Never the words alone: every page carries "Out of Stock" in its settings.
    - **A copy that is not drawn cannot tell, and keeps what the last read said** (`STOCK_ON_DRAWN_PAGE` in the audit's `patchFor`): ScraperAPI's page (the backup route) is the server's and has no buybox; without the rule a sold-out link would go back on the websites for a pass and leave again at the next.
    - From there it is the rule of every store: the link stays OK, shows as Out of stock, leaves the Stylish pages until Home Depot has the product again, is read again every check day. In Price Alignment such a page is left out only when it shows no price, or when our own warehouse has none either — see "A page sold out on the store counts unless our warehouse has none either" under Data model conventions (until 2026-11-26 this bullet said every sold-out link was left out, which the code never did).
    - **Not the way:** asking Home Depot's own product service for the stock (as Rona's item service is asked) was refused by the session's permission check as unauthorized access, and left there; ScraperAPI opens the drawn page only in its premium mode (25 credits a page — the month's credits do not allow it).
    - **Checked that day:** the PIM's own reader on 18 pages saved that afternoon — through Bright Data, S-301G and S-203T sold out, twelve of our products and two Glacier Bay sinks (a control: another brand's, surely in stock) in stock; ScraperAPI's two server pages: cannot tell. Deployed at his word ("Sí, publícala": `where-to-buy-audit` **v99**, 20 files, `verify_jwt` off as it was; the deploy also carried what had waited in those files since v98 — Cabinet Depot's removal and the price report's `skipKinds`). Then for real: three products' links made due and read by the cron's own run — S-301G and S-203T Out of stock, S-411T in stock, S-301G's price 482 where the morning's read had 513 — and S-301G's public page on both Stylish websites opened minutes later: the HomeDepot.com link is gone from WHERE TO BUY, the stores that have it are there. The other 134 HomeDepot.com addresses were made due the same hour.
    - **Found on the way, told to him:** S-203T is sold out on HomeDepot.com with 287 units in the USA (C227 with 3 and C126L with 2 too): the listing's own stock is not ours.
    - **Bright Data was suspended for lack of funds that afternoon** (the unlocker answers 200 with `status_code` 407 inside, `client_10020: Account is suspended`; the first time was 2026-10-26): from about 10:15 AM Eastern, its last answer, to about 3:25 PM, when he added funds. The Monday pass was over by then; the 10 AM second read of the problems (705 pages asked for) was cut a quarter of an hour in, and the 3 PM deal re-read asked for only 10 Lowe's links — about 540 pages (Lowe's 163, Home Depot Canada 149, Wayfair 55, Rona 18…) kept their morning reading, and no link turned Broken for it: the check takes that answer as no answer and tries again. Their retries were already set for that night and Wednesday; they were made due at once instead, with their miss counters back at 0 — a link that has missed twice or more starts its next try on ScraperAPI (`triesInOrder`), which would have spent the month's credits on pages Bright Data opens. **After a suspension: make due the rows with `fail_count` 1–3 and set it to 0** (not Menards', nor a link that is not OK). **What it costs:** the unlocker zone took 77.47 USD in the 30 days to that day (51,646 requests, 1.50 USD a thousand — `GET https://api.brightdata.com/zone/cost?zone=web_unlocker1&from=&to=` with the token; the balance call is refused to this key), so about 90–100 USD a month with the scrapers and the problems' re-read: told to him as "ponle 100".
  - **An Amazon link whose item Amazon cannot sell right now is Out of stock, not Broken** (user, 2026-11-24: K-131GR's Amazon.ca link stood Broken with its page open and in stock — "creo que es porque ayer no tenía inventario pues y hoy sí"; he was right). When the item a link asks for has no offer or no stock, Amazon does not show it sold out: it **moves the shopper to another option of the same listing** and takes the one asked for out of the choices. The page's own data names both — `"landingAsin"` the item asked for, `"currentAsin"` the one on show — so the check read the sibling's model ("Opens another product: K-131G", twice two days apart → Broken; a sibling of the same family → "Wrong product"), and the right link sat in To fix. The reader now says so (`movedFrom` in [amazon.js](supabase/functions/_shared/pageReaders/amazon.js): the link's own ASIN is the landing one and the page is on another), and the audit's `judgedFor` takes it as sold out, like Best Buy's taken-down pages: the link stays OK, shows as **Out of stock** with its reason ("Amazon shows another option of this listing (K-131G) while it cannot sell this one"), is off the Stylish pages meanwhile and comes back at the first pass that finds its own page again. **Not when the page on show IS the link's product** (an older item of it that Amazon sends to the current one: P-223W's link stays OK), and a link to another item with no such word from Amazon is judged as before.
    - **How many:** that day 49 Amazon links stood Broken as "Opens another product" (Amazon.ca 11, Amazon.com 38) and 29 as "Wrong product" with the page on another item than the link's — against 70 real 404s. Each turns Out of stock (or OK, if its item is back) when it is next read. An item Amazon never sells again stays Out of stock and leaves To fix: said to the user.
    - **Not the visitor's country:** the same link asked through Bright Data with a US and a Canadian exit read the same (Amazon delivered to a Canadian address each time). The user's own browser showed K-131GR "Currently unavailable" because his delivery address is outside Canada.
    - **Found on the way, told to him:** K-131GR's Amazon.com link is a real 404 (that item does not exist on Amazon.com); and the items Amazon.ca could not sell on those passes include products with stock in Cambridge (K-131GR had 148 units) — the Amazon offer's own quantity is not our warehouse's.
    - **Checked that day:** the reader and the rule on the pages saved that morning through Bright Data, with the whole catalog — S-301G → S-300XG, C430L → C432L and S-612WN → S-610W: Out of stock; K-131GR's own page (two visitors): OK, in stock, 338 CAD; an address two products share (P-223W: OK, the page is its own; P-220: Out of stock); the sibling's own link on a moved page and a wrong link without Amazon's word: as before — 9 of 9. **Deployed the same day at his word** ("Sí, publícala": `where-to-buy-audit` **v100**, 20 files, `verify_jwt` off as it was; a call without credentials gets its own refusal). Then for real, four links made due and read by the cron's own run two minutes later: K-131GR's Amazon.ca link OK again (its own page, in stock, 338 CAD) and back on both Stylish websites' public pages; S-301G and C430L on Amazon.com and S-612WN on Amazon.ca from Broken to Out of stock, each with its reason ("Amazon shows another option of this listing (S-300XG) while it cannot sell this one"). The other 73 Amazon addresses whose page had shown another item were made due the same hour.
  - **"Read again" on each link of the product's Where to Buy tab, with the time left** (user, 2026-11-25: "agreguemos un botón para volver a leer el link en el where to buy de cada website … y que diga un estimado de cuánto falta para que termine de leerlo"; until then only a price problem of Price Alignment could be read at will). Every saved link's row has the button, left of Open (`aria-label` "Read the <store> page again"; editors and admins — each read is a paid request —, none on a store without a link, and it waits while the field holds an address that is not saved: the page read is the saved one). [whereToBuyReads.js](src/features/products/api/whereToBuyReads.js) `startLinkRead` asks the audit's `reread` for that store and that product: the check's own read, so the row takes the page's status, price, stock and reviews, and a link that stops or starts opening the product leaves the Stylish pages or returns to them by the usual rules. One progress card and one Activity Log entry a read; several links can be read at once.
    - **The time left is the store's own.** While a link is read its status says "Reading" with "about 25 s left" under it (minutes for Menards; "almost done…" at the end, "a little longer…" past the estimate), and the card says the same. The audit keeps how long each store's reads took, from the person's click to the answer (`app_settings.where_to_buy_read_times`, `{ retailer: { ms, n } }`, each new time weighing two fifths; the tab sends `since` with every call), and the tab counts down from that time plus a fifth, in steps of 5 s ([readEstimate.js](src/features/products/lib/readEstimate.js)). A store never read this way starts from `SEED_S`: 8 s the stores the PIM opens by itself, 20–45 s the ones that are opened for it (Lowe's 45 s), 150 s Menards. The time left is seen, not read out: a screen reader hears the card's start and end.
    - **Menards** is asked for and read a little later (its scraper's job; Lowe's too until the same day's change of its route, below): the tab asks again every 20 s, for 6 minutes at most, so the answer comes in a couple of minutes instead of at the check's next turn.
    - **A read is not tied to the tab**: it goes on, with its card, while the person opens another tab of the product, and the row shows it as reading when they come back (`inFlight` in whereToBuyReads.js).
    - **What a read says when it ends**: "Wayfair.ca: still OK · C$290.00" or "Amazon.com: OK · $87.00 — it was Out of stock", on the card and in a "Read again:" box under the title. A read that finds the page as it was says "still": the row does not change, and the person must see that it was read all the same (user, the same day, about a link that stayed Out of stock: "pasaron 30 segundos pero no hizo nada"). No answer from the store, a page that opened without the product, or a store still answering are said in words — the card then stays until closed — and the link keeps its status. No text names a service or a schedule.
    - `reread` takes any store of the list (`WTB_RETAILERS`) besides the ones read from their pages: until this change SinksDirect.ca / USA, BestBuy.ca, Walmart.ca / .com and eBay.ca were refused. The status a row shows is one rule, [linkStatus.js](src/features/products/lib/linkStatus.js) `linkStatusKey`, for the row's chip and for the read's message.
    - **Checked that day** in a browser with a stand-in sign-in, live reads, every write kept in the test and the function answered by the test (no store page was opened): 46 checks on K-130N — a button per saved link (18) and none on a store without one; the time left and its countdown; the call (store, product, the moment asked); the row, the line and the Activity Log entry after a read; an edited field; two reads at once; a store without an answer; a refusal in the function's words; Lowe's over three calls while the person leaves the tab and comes back; a store's own time (9 s → "about 15 s left"); a viewer (no button) — at 1440 / 390 in both themes: axe 0, no sideways scroll, the document never scrolls. The function's file parses and its time keeper was tried on a stand-in store (6 cases). **Deployed the same day at the user's word** ("Sí, despliega y commit y push a main": `where-to-buy-audit` v101, 20 files, `verify_jwt` off as it was; a call without credentials gets its own refusal), then run for real through the deployed function with the check job's own command (K-151S on Amazon.com, below): the read answers in 3–9 s, the link's rows take the page, and the store's time is kept (Amazon.com: 6 s after 28 reads, so its count starts at "about 10 s left"). **Not run for real:** the button itself against the deployed function in a signed-in browser — the user's own first press was on v100, before the deploy — nor a read of the six stores it used to refuse.
  - **An Amazon page that says unavailable is asked for again before it is believed** (user, 2026-11-25, the first link he read with the button: K-151S's Amazon.com link "siguió en OOS cuando en realidad sí tiene stock"). Amazon shows a product as unavailable to a visitor whose delivery place it is not sold to: that page read "Currently unavailable. We don't know when or if this item will be back in stock." — no price, no buy button — when the unlocker's exit was "Los Angeles 90060", and in stock at $87 ("Only 3 left", our 3 units in ATL) from every other place of the country: 5 of about 57 reads that day came back unavailable (4 of the 11 the function made before the fix), the two whose place was noted both from Los Angeles 90060, and none of the 52 reads from some thirty other places. Each such read took the link off the Stylish pages until the next one. `lookAgain` in the audit (`SECOND_LOOK`: Amazon.com and Amazon.ca): a page that says unavailable, or that Amazon moved to another option of the listing, is opened again, twice at most and only while the call has time; **the link is sold out only when every read says so**, and a read that shows the product in stock, with its price, is the answer. A page truly sold out costs up to two more requests a pass (about 100 Amazon addresses were held as sold out that day).
    - The reading now keeps where the store thought the visitor was (`deliverTo`, `foreignExit` in pageProduct.js; Amazon's reader says them) and `reread`'s answer carries both. From an exit outside the country (asked for on purpose that day: Canada, the UK, Mexico, Germany, Colombia, India, Japan) Amazon.com hides the offer — no price, stock not said — but the unlocker's own exits for amazon.com were US addresses on every read that named its place. A Spanish-language copy of the page names none ("Actualizar ubicación"), which `foreignExit` reads as abroad although the page is the US one, with its price: no rule is built on that field until that is put right.
    - **Deployed the same day (v102 the place in the answer, v103 the second look):** twelve reads in a row through the deployed function, every one in stock at $87 (two took 9 s: a second look). K-151S's link went back to OK. The 105 Amazon addresses held as sold out (Amazon.com 75, Amazon.ca 30; 207 rows) were made due the same hour, to be read again with the rule.
    - Checked before the deploy: the rule on ten made-up answers (in stock at once: no second look; unavailable then in stock; unavailable three times; moved, then its own page; no answer to the second looks; no time left; another store), and 14 other Amazon.com links held as sold out opened again from this machine: all 14 sold out (12 of them products at 0 units in the USA), so the plain reading was right for them.
  - **Lowe's is read through the Web Unlocker again, and its scraper is the backup** (user, 2026-11-25, after four presses of Read again on P-200D's Lowe's link gave no answer: "Investiga qué pasa porque BrightData no lee Lowes?", then "Sí, hazlo y despliega"). Bright Data's ready-made "Lowes.com" scraper stopped reading Lowe's. Its own job history (`GET /datasets/v3/snapshots?dataset_id=gd_lnvl79pfftqh18u2o&status=ready`, then each snapshot's records with their `error_code`): 252 pages read on Nov 16, 235 on Nov 18, 136 on Nov 20 with 111 `detect_block` "Access Denied", 8–89 a job on Nov 23–24 (`wait_element_timeout` on parts of the page), and on Nov 25 8 and 18 read against 246 and 123 `no_peers` ("Crawler error: Unexpected Status 502 (no_peer)" — an error Bright Data's documentation does not list). That day 222 of the 256 OK Lowe's links carried three or more misses and 46 had no answer since Nov 18. Nothing of the PIM's had changed, and Menards' scraper read as usual.
    - **The Web Unlocker opens Lowe's again** (it had met a captcha there on 2026-10-20): 38 of 40 of those links, 34 at the first try, about 17 s a page, and the PIM's own reader of the page (`readLowes`: the page's `__PRELOADED_STATE__`) gave the stored model on 38 of 38 and the stored price on 35 of 36. ScraperAPI's standard route answered 500.
    - **The route** (`ROUTES.lowes_us = ["brightdata", "scraper"]`, `toScraper` in the audit). A Lowe's link is opened through the unlocker like the other stores' (the unlocker pool, 12 rows a call), with one more try in the link's own turn when the unlocker turns the page down (`UNLOCKER_SECOND_TRY`; a try it turns down is not billed). The scraper takes a link only as the backup: one with a scraper job in flight, and one turn in three once the unlocker has missed it twice in a row (misses 2, 5, 8…) — whichever of the two Lowe's turns away, the other still reads it, and a broken scraper never holds a link for good. **A read somebody asks for** (Read again on the product's tab or on Price Alignment, the check after a save) always goes through the unlocker. Menards is read only by its scraper, as before (`SCRAPER_FIRST`). A suggested Lowe's link is now opened like any other store's before it shows.
    - **What changes in sight.** Read again on a Lowe's link answers in seconds (it took minutes; the tab's first guess for Lowe's is 45 s until its own time is learned). A page Lowe's no longer sells — its data says `status: REMOVED`, the drawn page "This item is no longer sold on Lowes.com" — is Broken at once, where the scraper's record only said sold out (D-700B, D-700B2, D-701C, S-613WNK and five Azuni listings that day); either way the link is off the Stylish pages. The model kept is Lowe's own spelling (S-822N1 for S-822N: a match, as before).
    - **Checked that day.** The function's own file run on the dev machine with every write withheld (`audit-local.mjs` in that session's scratchpad: Node's `--experimental-strip-types`, a stand-in `Deno`, the two remote imports replaced, only database reads and unlocker pages let through): P-200D's link read in 34 s; the passes' own path on stand-in due rows (misses 0 and 1 → the unlocker, misses 2 and 5 → one scraper job of two addresses, Menards → its job); three asked reads whatever their misses, one of them a page no longer sold → Broken; a Menards asked read → its scraper as before. **Deployed at his word** (`where-to-buy-audit` **v104**, 20 files, `verify_jwt` off as it was; a call without credentials gets 401). Then for real: P-200D's link through the deployed function with the check job's own command — 7 s, in stock, $81.43, its seven misses back at 0 —; ten stuck addresses made due and read by the cron's own run in under two minutes, 10 of 10 with the stored model and price; then the other 227 made due with their miss counters at 0. An hour later 162 of them were read: the stored model on every one and the stored price on all but three (K-149N 163 → 153, S-709XN 354 → 373, S-622W 420 → 382), 13 back in stock and one sold out, a dozen Broken as no longer sold; about one in ten missed both tries and waits for its retry three hours later. The rest were read by the same runs afterwards (not seen).
    - **A copy without its data is asked for again** (v105, the same hour). Now and then the unlocker hands over a copy of a Lowe's product page with its title and without its data: 2 of the first ~100 reads (K-150SM, K-135S) — their stored price and model were emptied and one took a sign toward Broken — and each read whole at the next request, 3 of 3. Such a copy (an open page the reader reads nothing on, or a "page without the product") is asked for once more in the link's turn, and the copy with the product is the answer; only two copies without it count as a page without the product. Tried on the dev machine with a stand-in empty copy (one empty copy → read, 185 USD; two → the sign, as before); the two links were made due again and K-150SM's sign taken back.
    - That afternoon Jessica was working the Lowe's links by hand from the product pages (11 broken ones removed, new listings set on C126L and C430L): the row counts moved for that, not for the change.
  - **A page that is down while we have no stock of the product is Out of stock, not Broken** (user, 2026-11-26: "hay muchos productos que están fuera de stock … esos productos que vengan de Odoo con 0, en vez de broken, se coloquen como Out of Stock"). A store takes a product's page down while there is nothing to sell, so there is nothing for a person to fix. That day the panel showed 222 Broken links (272 with Walmart USA's 50, which the panel does not count): **146 were pages down on products at 0 units in that store's country**, 70 pages down on products we do have, 6 links that open another product — Menards 53, Amazon.com 44, Walmart.com 30, Warehouse-USA 22, Lowe's 17 (every one of its "no longer sold" pages).
    - **The check decides it, like Best Buy's offer rule** (`downWithoutStock` / `withOwnStock` in the audit): the link is written OK with `in_stock` false and its reason ("The page is down while we have no stock in the USA"), so every reader shows it as Out of stock with no change of its own — the panel's counter and filter, the product's tab, the Excel (off To fix), the Dashboard's briefing, Price Alignment (left out as sold out instead of "not on the store"). The websites do not change: a Broken link and a sold-out one are both off the pages.
    - **His three limits.** Only a page that is DOWN — 404 / 410, the store's "not found" page, "no longer sold", a scraper's dead page once its signs are in (the outcome's `down`): a link that opens another product or a page without the product is a wrong link whatever our stock, and so is one sent to another site, a login page or the store's home page (a store that closed or moved: a person looks at those). **Our stock is Odoo's Available for Use of the store's own country** (`product_inventory`: Canada = Cambridge, USA = Atlanta); a product Odoo does not track has no figure and is judged as before, never as 0. **Nothing is remembered:** the link is judged again at every read, so the first pass that finds the page still down with stock in the warehouse makes it Broken — a link that was really wrong does not stay hidden.
    - The signs a scraper's dead page needs (Menards: four, 6 h apart) are counted as before; the rule answers at the moment the link would turn Broken — and at once on a link that is already Broken and shows its dead page again (the signs keep a live link from being called broken, and that one was: Menards' 53 would have waited four more). The three dealers watched for their price only (site `dealers`) have no country in the registry and are judged as before.
    - **Known and said to him:** Amazon (its own FBA stock) and the dealers on the Stylish pages (Warehouse-USA, Kitchen & Bath Authority, Build with Rise) sell from stock of their own, so our 0 explains a page down there less; they are included all the same, since stock in our warehouse sends the link back to Broken. A product we never stock in the USA keeps its dead USA links as Out of stock for good. A link just saved with a dead address, on a product at 0, reads Out of stock with that reason rather than Broken.
    - **Checked 2026-11-26** with the function's own file run on the dev machine (Node's `--experimental-strip-types`, a stand-in `Deno`, its two remote imports taken from node_modules, every write withheld — that session's scratchpad `wtb/`): 23 cases of the rule on made-up answers (0 units, 5 units, not tracked, 0 in the other country, a Canada store, "no longer sold", "not found", another product and a page without the product at their second sign, Menards' first and fourth sign with and without stock, a Menards link already Broken, a link held as sold out that stays, turns Broken when stock arrives, or comes back in stock with its price, a price-watch dealer, Best Buy's own rule); and the real check on five broken Warehouse-USA links, pages really opened: the three at 0 units would be written Out of stock, the two with stock (1 and 17 units) Broken, and the same three Broken again with 7 units put in the stock answer.
    - **Live the same day, each step at his word** ("Sí, despliega", "Sí, hoy", "Sí, commit y push"): `where-to-buy-audit` v106, then v107 with the already-Broken shortcut (20 files, `verify_jwt` off as it was; nothing else had changed in its files since v105; a call without credentials gets 401). The 173 Broken links with a page down on a product at 0 units (336 rows; the list before is in that session's scratchpad) were made due — only `next_check_at` — and the check's own runs read them: **171 turned Out of stock within half an hour** (Amazon.com 44, Menards 53, Walmart.com 26, Warehouse-USA 22, Lowe's 17, Best Buy 4, Walmart.ca 2, Build with Rise 2, eBay 1), one Best Buy page was back and reads OK, one Walmart.com link waits for its retry. **The panel's Broken went from 222 to 76**: 70 pages down on products we have and 6 links that open another product. No page was written again (the publish queue stayed empty: the links were off the pages before and after).
  - **The links that need attention are read every day** (user, 2026-11-26, asked what a daily check would cost: "se haría todos los días solo de aquellos que salgan Out of Stock y que sí tengamos stock (los que no tenemos stock en dicho país, no se haría) y de los que salgan broken o other finish o wrong link, de los OK solo se seguiría haciendo como hasta ahora" — "Hazlo"). Mode `daily-reread` (`dailyReread` in the audit; cron `where-to-buy-daily-reread`, `0 5,6 * * *`, of which the call at 1 AM Eastern runs; [20261126_where_to_buy_daily_reread.sql](supabase/migrations/20261126_where_to_buy_daily_reread.sql)) makes due, on every day that is NOT a pass day of their store — Tuesday, Thursday, Saturday and Sunday, and for BB&B and Overstock Wednesday too —, the links shown as Broken, Wrong product or Other finish, and the Out of stock ones of a product we HAVE stock of in the store's country (`product_inventory` by the link's own `market`; a product Odoo does not track is read, never taken as 0). The usual check batches open them, so a link whose store has the product again is back on the Stylish pages that day instead of two or three days later. An OK link in stock keeps its passes.
    - **Left out:** the sold-out links of products we have no stock of (1,333 rows that day: nothing to sell there, they wait for their pass), a link already due and one read in the last 6 hours. A link that gets no answer on such a day waits for its next pass or the next daily read.
    - **What it costs.** 471 pages the first day (Broken 189, Wrong product 85, Other finish 10, Out of stock with stock of ours 187): about 0.5 USD a day through Bright Data, 8–10 USD a month. Fewer than the ~645 counted for him that morning (12–15 USD a month), because the same day's rule turned the dead pages of products without stock into Out of stock. ScraperAPI: what Overstock's and BB&B's links of the set may spend is added to the day's allowance, 800 credits at most (`DAILY_SCRAPERAPI_MAX`; ~640 a day were expected, ~14,000 a month of the plan's 100,000) — if BB&B turns Bright Data away again, its daily links wait for their pass instead of taking the passes' share of the month.
    - `{ mode: "daily-reread", dryRun: true }` only counts (`asOf` for another day's set), `{ force: true }` runs it at another hour; the last run's report is `app_settings.where_to_buy_daily_reread`.
    - **The same day he set Bright Data's daily limit to 20 USD** (it was 10 and switched the zone off when reached: it only notifies now) **and switched its auto recharge on** (100 USD), after the numbers of that morning: 76.43 USD in November so far, a check day 6–8.5 USD, the limit reached once (Nov 16). ScraperAPI stood at 88,265 of 100,000 credits three days before its renewal, with every BB&B and Overstock address read that week. Bright Data's balance cannot be read with the PIM's token (403).
    - **Checked that day.** Dry runs of the function's own file on the dev machine (that Thursday: 471 pages; a Wednesday: only BB&B's 61 and Overstock's 31; a Monday: none); the same dry run through the deployed function (`where-to-buy-audit` **v108**, 20 files, `verify_jwt` off as it was — built on the same day's v107, the dead pages of products without stock); then run for real for that Thursday (`force`): 827 rows made due, the day's ScraperAPI allowance 500 → 1,300. Ten minutes later the check's own runs had answered 386 pages (the set's, and some that were already due), with 46 rows still to read: 26 were OK and in stock again (44 rows, on their way back to the Stylish pages), about 190 still sold out, 100 still Broken, 62 still on another product; the Broken rows went from 383 to 276, most of them now Out of stock by the same day's rule. The first run by the job itself is Saturday 2026-11-28 at 1 AM Eastern (Friday is a pass day): read `app_settings.where_to_buy_daily_reread` after it.
- `bestbuy-inventory` — Best Buy Canada stock (user, 2026-10-30: "Podemos enviar el inventario desde PIM?" — "Sí, hazlo"): every Best Buy offer whose shop SKU is a PIM product (open-box "…-B-OB-A" offers and products switched off for Best Buy left alone) gets our Canada stock (`product_inventory` 'ca') **as it is — no safety margin** (user, the same day: "quítale eso"; Walmart USA keeps −2), 0 when not tracked, through Mirakl OF24 with the offer's live price and discount on each line (NORMAL mode blanks missing fields). It sends only when a NEWER Canada stock reading is in the PIM than the last one sent (or on Send now), and only the offers whose quantity differs. With the daily Excel that was once a day; since 2026-11-10 (the stock is read from Odoo every hour) a switched-on sync sends every hour, which also puts back the units of a Best Buy sale Odoo does not know about yet — check how fast Best Buy's orders reach Odoo before switching it on. Cron `bestbuy-inventory-hourly` (:45); the switch is the Stock sync card on the Best Buy page (`app_settings.bestbuy_inventory.enabled`), built off (on 2026-10-30 Best Buy already carried our exact Canada stock, uploaded by someone outside the PIM — the same source that left ~108 offers at 0 on Oct 27–28 — and the two would fight), switched on by the user that same day at 12:01 ET, OFF from 2026-11-10 (user: "todo lo va a sincronizar Odoo") and **ON again since 2026-11-11** (user: "SI QUE LO CUBRA PIM"): Odoo's sync has no Best Buy option, so nobody was sending. It goes off again when Odoo covers Best Buy, on the user's word — two senders would fight. **A line carries the offer's OWN price, `discount.origin_price`**: while a discount runs Mirakl's `price` is the discounted one, and sending it with the carried discount made price = discount price — Mirakl refused the line ("The discount price is incorrect: must not be null or must be lower than price"), 124 of 250 on 2026-11-10, so the stock of every product on promotion had stopped reaching Best Buy since the promotion began (S-601WK stood at 6 units with 144 in the PIM). Fixed 2026-11-11: 78 sent, 0 refused. Read `last.itemsFailed` after a send. The Walmart USA and Best Buy cards are one component, [StockSyncCard](src/features/syndication/components/StockSyncCard.jsx).
- `ai-format-html` — Gemini-backed description formatter (house style + typo repairs only; a word-level validator rejects rewording). The same formatter (functions/_shared/aiFormat.ts) runs automatically inside `wix-push-product` on EVERY push that carries a description.
- `odoo-sales-pull` — units sold per product, market and marketplace over the last 30 / 90 days, from Odoo's confirmed sales order lines into `product_sales`, read only (see "Units sold come from Odoo too"). Every hour (cron `odoo-sales-pull`, `25 * * * *` since 2026-11-22); ~5 s for 10,500 lines. `{ dryRun }`; the last report is `app_settings.odoo_sales`. `verify_jwt` off; x-cron-secret, a service-role bearer or a signed-in session. Self-contained.
- `inventory-pull` — stock into `product_inventory` from Odoo, read only (`ODOO_*` secrets): each market's warehouse (Canada CBG, USA ATL), `available_for_use` for the PIM's SKUs with the context `warehouse_id`, and `eta` from the open purchase order lines of the market's company (see the stock paragraph under Data model conventions). Runs every 5 minutes via pg_cron (`inventory-pull-hourly`, `*/5 * * * *` since 2026-11-22; the job keeps its name), after every product insert (trigger) and from the Refresh buttons; a pull of both markets takes a few seconds. Body `{ mode: "pull" | "ping", market: "us" | "ca" | "all", dryRun? }`; one market failing does not stop the other. Deployed with `verify_jwt` off; accepts `x-cron-secret`, a service-role bearer (proven via GoTrue admin, not by string match) or a signed-in session.

### scripts/

One-off Node maintenance scripts (`node scripts/<name>.mjs`) that hit the Supabase REST API directly with the service_role key from `.env.secrets.local` (e.g. data normalization and inspection utilities). All product media/documents live in Supabase Storage — Dropbox was fully removed 2026-07-23.
